# AVRL research ledger

Entries are appended by the autoresearch loop and by hand. Verdicts are against the pre-registered thresholds in PROJECT.md and EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md.

## E01 2026-09-30 kill (for claim 3 on this task/proxy pair)
Hypothesis: proxy error (group majority answer wrong) has exploitable structure in prompt space and is not already captured by cheap features.
Run: Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, GSM8K train, 3,000 prompts x 8 rollouts (vLLM, temperature 1.0), proxy = agreement with group majority, verifier = gold answer. Files: runs/p0/summary.json, result.json, feats.parquet, sim_*.json, analyze.log.

| quantity | value |
|---|---|
| rollout accuracy / majority accuracy | 64.2% / 85.5% |
| group proxy-error rate (majority wrong) | 14.5% |
| proxy error, unanimous groups (18.6% of groups) | 0.5% |
| proxy error, split groups | 17.7% |
| AUC for proxy error: agreement only / all cheap / embedding only / cheap + embedding | 0.914 / 0.923 / 0.606 / 0.922 |
| neighbor ratio of proxy error by embedding cell (k = 30 / 100 / 300) | 1.00 / 1.03 / 1.04 |
| confident subset (agreement >= 0.75, n = 1,551): error rate / cheap AUC / neighbor ratio | 1.1% / 0.63 / 1.16 |

Allocation replay (errors caught per budget, 100 cells, 8 seeds; columns: uniform, static with free labels [approximately the agreement threshold], static budget-matched, TS flat, greedy online self-trained, TS online, TS + free prior, neighbor, oracle):
10%: 28, 165, 98, 31, 138, 95, 118, 53, 218. 25%: 76, 264, 212, 83, 253, 208, 232, 117, 309. 50%: 157, 299, 287, 164, 296, 288, 294, 206, 309. Embedding features change nothing.

Verdict: pre-registered read in PROJECT.md triggers (embeddings add < 0.05 AUC, neighbor ratio < 1.5): claim 3 (edge from prompt-space structure) is dead on GSM8K with a majority-vote proxy. The fixed agreement threshold is near-optimal here; the self-training allocator matches it at 25% and 50% budgets and loses at 10%. This is the "threshold regime" and becomes the first point of the structure-statistic-versus-gain curve (see the revised proposal): cheap-feature AUC 0.92, gain of learned over threshold about 0.
What it changes: the allocator can only earn its keep where the proxy lacks a confidence signal. Next: E04 (small judge model as proxy, no self-consistency), E05 (self-verification proxy), E08 (code task where majority vote over programs is ill-defined), each reporting the same three statistics plus the replay, to populate the curve. Also add an explicit label-free agreement-threshold policy to the replay so the baseline is not approximated by "static with free labels".

## E04 2026-10-18 kill (for claim 3 on GSM8K, answer-only judge proxy; primary rung Qwen2.5-3B valid, no fallback)
Hypothesis: with a proxy that has no self-consistency signal (answer-only LLM judge that sees the question and the final number, not the reasoning), proxy errors depend on what the judge cannot evaluate, so they should show prompt-space structure that agreement and cheap features miss.
Run: the same 3,000 E01 groups (Qwen2.5-1.5B rollouts, 24,000 rollouts, 10,168 unique (pid, answer) pairs, 35 empty answers scored 0 without a call). Judge prompt: "Question / Proposed final answer / Is the proposed final answer correct? Reply with exactly one word: Yes or No."; vLLM, temperature 0, one token, logprobs restricted to the Yes and No tokens, judge_p = P(Yes) renormalised. judge_sel = distinct answer with highest judge_p (ties by count); judge_error = judge_sel != gold. Files: runs/E04/result.json, judge_*.parquet, feats_*.parquet, sim_*.json, analyze.log, run.log; scripts avrl/exp/p0_judge_score.py, e04_analyze.py, E04.sh. GPU wall about 8 minutes for all three rungs (0.5B 77 s, 3B 135 s, 7B-AWQ 239 s), CPU analysis about 20 s. A 200-pair smoke check on the 0.5B rung (runs/E04/smoke) confirmed token ids and a non-constant judge_p before the full launch.

| quantity | E01 (majority vote) | 0.5B judge | 3B judge (primary) | 7B-AWQ judge |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| validity: rollout-level AUC of judge_p vs correct (gate 0.65) | n/a | 0.615 (fails gate) | 0.654 (passes, barely) | 0.702 |
| group error rate / count (floor 4.83%, majority vote 14.53%) | 14.53% / 436 | 50.1% / 1,503 | 47.3% / 1,419 | 43.8% / 1,313 |
| AUC agreement only for the error label | 0.914 | 0.808 | 0.803 | 0.784 |
| AUC cheap (no judge features) / cheap_j / cheap_j + emb | 0.923 / n.a. / 0.922 | 0.808 / 0.909 / 0.907 | 0.815 / 0.867 / 0.867 | 0.796 / 0.880 / 0.879 |
| delta AUC (embeddings add; threshold 0.05) | -0.001 (-0.002 under this script's CV) | -0.003 | -0.0003 | -0.002 |
| AUC embedding only | 0.606 | 0.558 | 0.540 | 0.534 |
| neighbor ratio cell30 / cell100 / cell300 (threshold 1.5) | 1.00 / 1.03 / 1.04 | 1.00 / 1.00 / 1.00 | 1.00 / 1.00 / 1.00 | 1.00 / 1.00 / 1.00 |
| Jaccard of error group set with E01 proxy_error (kill > 0.8, lever < 0.5) | 1 | 0.250 | 0.241 | 0.235 |

AUCs are 5-fold CV logistic regression (class_weight balanced), mean over 3 shuffled CV seeds, seed SD at most 0.001. Delta AUC is -0.003 to 0 on every rung, so there is no prompt-space structure beyond cheap + judge features. Top-10-percent-cell concentration of judge errors is 0.11 to 0.14 on every judge rung and cell count (a cell-agnostic layout would give about 0.10).

Allocation replay, primary configuration (100 cells, features cheap_j + emb, K = 1, 8 seeds), errors caught as learned arm (ts_online) / best pre-registered label-free threshold = ratio. The best threshold was thr_agreement (agreement ascending) in every cell; thr_margin (judge_margin ascending) is far worse (3B: 24 / 67 / 397 against 190 / 422 / 768).

| budget | E01 | 0.5B | 3B (primary) | 7B-AWQ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10% | 87 / 161 = 0.54 | 155 / 191 = 0.81 | 156 / 190 = 0.82 | 141 / 173 = 0.81 |
| 25% | 200 / 254 = 0.79 | 381 / 444 = 0.86 | 382 / 422 = 0.90 (SD 0.02) | 358 / 401 = 0.89 (SD 0.03) |
| 50% | 283 / 298 = 0.95 | 716 / 786 = 0.91 | 705 / 768 = 0.92 | 659 / 696 = 0.95 |

At 25% the 3B rung also has static_matched 371, greedy_online (self-trained, no prompt-space layout) 423, uniform 268, oracle 545 (budget-limited). Across the 36 cells (3 rungs x cell30 and cell100 x cheap_j and cheap_j + emb x three budgets) the learned arm exceeds the best threshold by more than 2 SDs exactly once, with the same cell under the augmented thresholds of caveat 4: 7B cell30 cheap_j at 50% (ratio 1.07). That is not the primary configuration and is one of 36 cells, so it is not evidence.

Verdict (pre-registered rules in the E04 spec): the 3B rung is valid (rollout AUC 0.654 >= 0.65, 1,419 judge_error groups >= 100), so no fallback to 7B. Structure present? No (delta AUC -0.0003 < 0.05 and neighbor ratio 1.00 < 1.5). Learned over best threshold at 25% is 0.90 <= 1.05, gap not > 2 SD. All three kill conditions hold: KILL for claim 3 on GSM8K for both a self-consistency proxy (E01) and an answer-only judge proxy (E04). Jaccard 0.24: by the spec's secondary read that says proxy choice is a lever, but see caveat 2. The result is the same on the 7B rung (delta -0.002, ratio 0.89), so it does not hinge on the 3B rung sitting 0.004 above the gate. The 0.5B rung fails the gate (judge_p > 0.5 for 99.4% of pairs) and serves as the null control; it gives the same picture.

Caveats, stated plainly:
1. The 3B gate margin is thin (0.654 against 0.65); the 7B rung independently supports the same read.
2. Jaccard 0.24 mostly reflects set size, not different errors: judge errors are 3x as common as majority-vote errors (1,419 vs 436), the maximum Jaccard attainable is 436 / 1,419 = 0.31, and 83% of E01's majority-vote errors (360 of 436) are also judge errors. The answer-only judge at 0.5B to 7B is a much worse proxy than free majority vote (44 to 50% against 14.5% error), so "proxy choice is a lever" is true only in the trivial sense that a worse proxy has more errors.
3. The "no confidence signal" regime was not actually reached. Agreement among the 8 rollouts is still free and predicts judge_error at AUC 0.78 to 0.81, because judge errors are almost entirely wrong picks inside split groups (judge_error is 0.54% on the 559 unanimous groups against 54% to 61% on the 2,441 split groups, for every rung). Any proxy computed over a rollout group inherits agreement as a free cheap feature. So E04 kills claim 3 for judge-over-group proxies on GSM8K, not for proxies that truly carry no cheap confidence signal. That regime needs a task where group agreement is uninformative (E08, code), which is not runnable on this machine.
4. The pre-registered thr_margin as specified (margin 0 for single-answer groups) ranks unanimous groups, which hold the fewest errors, first, so it is a strawman baseline (it catches fewer errors than uniform at 10% budget). I kept the pre-registered pair for the verdict and added two robustness policies (thr_margin_split: unanimous last; thr_agree_margin: agreement with margin tie-break). They change the best threshold by at most 1% (3B 25%: unchanged; 7B 25%: 405 vs 401, ratio 0.88 vs 0.89), so the kill is not an artifact of the weak baseline.
5. (Corrected on review.) The learned prompt-space arm (ts_online) is below the layout-free self-trained arm (greedy_online) at 25% on every rung (3B: 382 vs 423), so the layout costs exploration and buys nothing. The earlier draft of this caveat said greedy_online stays within about 5% of the agreement threshold at every budget; that holds only at 25%. Across the 12 judge cells per budget (3 rungs x cell30/cell100 x cheap_j / cheap_j + emb), greedy_online over thr_agreement is: 10% budget ratio 0.82 to 0.96 (below by more than 2 pooled SD in 11 of 12 cells, never above); 25% ratio 0.95 to 1.09 (above by more than 2 SD in 5, below in 2); 50% ratio 1.03 to 1.13 (above by more than 2 SD in 12 of 12; 3B cell100 cheap_j + emb 808 vs 768, 7B 762 vs 697, 0.5B 839 vs 786). On E01 the same arm only ties the threshold (252 vs 254 at 25%, 296 vs 298 at 50%). So a learned arm with no prompt-space layout does beat the single-feature agreement rule at high budget on the weak judge labels, simply because it can combine the judge features (cheap_j AUC 0.87 to 0.91 against agreement-only 0.78 to 0.81). It does not beat the offline-fitted multi-feature rule: the replay's static arm (logistic regression on cheap_j + emb, fit on a disjoint 25% of the cells with free labels) is at least as good as greedy_online in 46 of 48 cells (E01 plus three rungs, cell30 and cell100, two feature sets, three budgets; 3B cell100 cheap_j + emb: static 197 / 460 / 824 against greedy 161 / 423 / 808 at 10 / 25 / 50%; the two exceptions are 3B cell30 at 50% by 6 to 10 hits). The static arm's calibration labels (about 800 groups) are not charged to the budget, while the budget-matched static arm that is charged (371 / 25%, 3B) loses to greedy_online (423). The learned-versus-fixed-rule comparison therefore turns on how the fixed rule's calibration labels are accounted for, which is exactly the open G01 baseline.
6. The replay's re-run of E01 reproduces the original E01 simulator output exactly (zero differences on all original policies), so the E01 and E04 ratios are on the same footing. The E01 point here uses the agreement threshold (254 at 25%, ratio 0.79 for cheap + emb, 0.82 for cheap), which closes the earlier follow-up on an explicit label-free baseline.

7. The neighbor-ratio statistic is nearly vacuous at these cell sizes and base rates. aa.data.neighbor_hit_ratio conditions on 'at least one same-cell neighbor is a hit'. On the judge labels (base rate 44 to 50%) that event holds for 100% of groups at cell30 and cell100 (97 to 98% at cell300), and on E01 for 100% / 95% / 77%, so the ratio is 1.000 by construction rather than by measurement. The pre-registered 'neighbor ratio >= 1.5' arm of the pass condition therefore had essentially no power on this label; the verdict rests on the delta-AUC arm, the replay, and the checks below. Independent checks run on review (CPU, feats_*.parquet, 3 CV seeds): (a) out-of-fold target-encoded cell membership (shrinkage 10, honest two-stage encoding) added to the cheap(+judge) logistic model changes AUC by at most +0.0018 (3B cell30; E01 +0.0005, 0.5B -0.0001, 7B -0.0001), against the 0.05 bar, and the 16 PCs change it by -0.0002 to -0.0027, reproducing the reported deltas; (b) a permutation test of between-cell heterogeneity of the residual left by the cheap(+judge) model is significant for E01 (p = 0.008 / 0.06 / 0.02 at cell30 / 100 / 300) and for 3B (p < 0.001 / < 0.001 / 0.02) and not significant for 0.5B and 7B (p 0.13 to 0.58). So a small amount of cell-level structure is statistically detectable on E01 and 3B, but it is worth at most 0.002 AUC, which is why it does not show up in any allocator. Future entries should report the out-of-fold cell-encoding delta AUC in place of the neighbor ratio.
8. Review check of the run report: I re-derived the validity AUCs, error counts, per-rung AUCs, Jaccards, the 36-cell replay table, the 2-SD count and the G03 points from result.json, the sim_*.json files and feats_*.parquet. They match the run report, with the single exception corrected in caveat 5. The 3B judge is also close to a constant 'No' (judge_p > 0.5 for 3.8% of rollouts, mean 0.057) and the 0.5B judge to a constant 'Yes' (99.5%), so the 0.5B and 3B judges carry little ranking information beyond a few coarse levels (58 and 79 distinct judge_p values against 729 for 7B). In split groups the top two distinct answers have identical judge_p (so the count tie-break decides) in 29% of groups for 0.5B, 8.6% for 3B and 2.7% for 7B, which is part of why their group-level judge_error is high (44 to 50%, three times the majority-vote error) but not all of it.

First tentative G03 points (primary configuration, 25% budget; gain = learned / best threshold - 1; n = 4, not significant): E01 delta AUC -0.002, neighbor ratio 1.03, gain -0.21; 0.5B -0.003, 1.00, -0.14; 3B -0.0003, 1.00, -0.10; 7B -0.002, 1.00, -0.11. Spearman rho of delta AUC against gain is 0.80 (p = 0.20) and of neighbor ratio against gain is -0.80 (p = 0.20), but every delta AUC sits within noise of zero, so this is a flat structure axis with a gain that rises as the proxy weakens (agreement AUC 0.91 to 0.78) yet stays negative. Read it as: weaker cheap confidence narrows the learned arm's deficit without producing a win, and prompt-space structure explains none of it.

What it changes: Claim 3 (edge from structure in prompt space) is not supported on GSM8K for group-level proxies, with the structure measured three ways (linear PCs, cell target encoding, replay) at 0 to 0.002 AUC. The scope limit is binding: every proxy tested is computed over an 8-rollout group, so group agreement was always a free cheap feature (caveat 3), and the revised thesis ('the allocator wins only where the proxy lacks a confidence signal') has still not been tested in its own regime. Claim 2 (learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget) is not supported as stated: the prompt-space allocator (ts_online) is numerically ahead of the agreement threshold in only 5 of 36 judge cells (all cell30 with cheap_j, or 50% budget; ratio 1.006 to 1.07, beyond 2 SD in 1) and never beats the offline-fit multi-feature rule; the one family of wins is the layout-free greedy arm over the single-feature agreement rule at 50% budget on weak judge labels (12 of 12 cells), which is a feature-combination effect that a recalibrated multi-feature fixed rule with charged calibration labels (G01) could erase. Per the E04 kill rule, further GPU spend on group-level proxy variants on GSM8K (more judges, more policy sizes on the majority-vote axis) should stop. The remaining deliverable on this machine is the proxy-error measurement plus the fixed-threshold recipe, and the one open question that can still change that is whether a regime without free group agreement shows structure (follow-ups below).

Follow-ups (ranked by expected information value; ids match the backlog section 'Proposed by the loop'):
- L1a. Rollout-level, agreement-free replay on the existing E04 data (CPU only, about 10 to 15 min). Treat each of the 24,000 rollouts as one decision: the proxy accepts the rollout when judge_p > 0.5 (3B and 7B rungs separately), label = corrupted reward (accepted by the judge, wrong by gold) and also judge_wrong. The allocator may use only per-rollout features: judge_p, tokens, truncated, answer-format features (decimal, negative, magnitude), log question length, and the embedding PCs and cells; it may not use agreement, n_distinct, pass rate or any other group aggregate. Report the same three statistics (cheap AUC, OOF cell-encoding delta AUC, learned versus recalibrated threshold on judge_p) at 10 / 25 / 50% budgets. This is the cheapest way to populate the 'proxy lacks a confidence signal' end of the G03 axis (cheap AUC about 0.65 to 0.70 instead of 0.8 to 0.92). Information value: high. It can produce the first positive point for claim 3, or kill the revised thesis on GSM8K for good. Caveat to carry: it removes agreement by ablation, not by changing the task, so a null is weaker than a null on a real code task.
- L1b. Fair fixed-rule baseline with calibration-label accounting (CPU only, about 15 min, G01 made concrete). Add to sim_alloc.py a recalibrated multi-feature logistic rule fit on n_cal verified groups that are charged against the budget (n_cal in 50, 100, 200, 400), plus the free-label static arm as an upper bound, and compare with greedy_online and ts_online on the E01 label and the three judge labels, 8 seeds, bootstrap CIs on the learned-minus-fixed gap. Pass: greedy_online still exceeds the best charged fixed rule by more than 2 SD at 25% and 50%, which would be the first support for claim 2 and would justify running the Phase 1 GRPO harness (not runnable under the 45-minute cap). Kill: the best charged fixed rule matches or beats greedy_online at both budgets. Information value: high for claim 2, because the only learned-over-fixed edge seen so far (greedy_online at 50% against the single-feature agreement rule) either survives this baseline or does not.
- L1c. Trained reward-model proxy at rollout level (GPU, about 25 to 30 min in two jobs, needs a smoke check first). Regenerate the E01 rollouts with completion text saved (the p0 rollouts have none; 1.5B policy, 18 min) and score each completion with the on-disk Skywork-Reward-V2-Qwen3-0.6B (/homes/rdubash/active-audit/models), using vLLM pooling if the installed version supports the architecture and transformers on the GPU otherwise. Label = reward-model-accepted but wrong by gold, accept threshold set to the 64% rollout accuracy quantile; features per L1a plus text length and completion-level features; no group aggregates. This is the only on-disk proxy that is a trained scorer of full reasoning rather than a function of the answer or of group agreement, which is the proxy type the original claim was written for. Information value: medium to high; the risk is that the reward model is accurate on GSM8K and its errors are too rare or too diffuse to structure (check judge-error count >= 100 before analysis, as in the E04 gate). Run only after L1a, because a null on L1a lowers the prior that L1c finds structure.

**Critic note (E04, adversarial review 2026-10-18).** The KILL stands: claim 3 is dead on GSM8K for the majority-vote and answer-only judge proxies, with the 3B rung valid. Two things need correcting (item 1 and item 6 below), plus four smaller precision fixes. None of them changes the verdict.

What I re-derived from the raw files. The sources were result.json, sim_*.json, judge_*.parquet, feats_*.parquet, p0/rollouts.parquet, run.log and the scripts.
- Validity AUCs, error counts, Jaccards, unanimous and split error rates, every cell of the primary replay table, the 5-of-36 and 1-beyond-2-SD ts_online counts, static >= greedy in 46 of 48 cells, and ts_online below the free-label static arm in all 48 cells all match.
- I recomputed judge_sel independently from the judge parquet and got identical error counts (1,503 / 1,419 / 1,313).
- The E01 replay reproduction holds: 108 policy-budget means, 0 differences.
- The verdict logic in e04_analyze.py matches the pre-registered thresholds. Delta AUC -0.0003 < 0.05, neighbor ratio 1.000 < 1.5, ratio 0.904 <= 1.05, so all three kill conditions hold. The Jaccard 0.24 is correctly read as a set-size effect, since the maximum attainable is 436 / 1,419 = 0.31.
- There is no leakage. The judge prompt contains only the question and the extracted answer, and gold is merged into the pair table only for the post hoc pair_auc. Allocator features are agreement, n_distinct, token statistics, truncation, log question length, the four judge features and the MiniLM PCs. pass_rate and gold-derived columns are excluded.
- Budget accounting is equal across policies: every arm spends int(frac * n_sim) verifications at K = 1. The free-label static arm is the only uncharged arm and is reported as an upper bound.
- The logs show no crash or truncation. There are three RUNG_EXIT 0 lines, and 10,133 of 10,133 prompts were processed per rung. exit_code is 0, GPU time was about 8 minutes, and the cap was not hit. The 'EngineCore died unexpectedly' ERROR lines come after the summary json and 'Shutdown complete', so they are benign.

Corrections:
1. Caveat 5 and the 'What it changes' paragraph count greedy_online versus thr_agreement with an RMS pooled SD, sqrt((a^2 + b^2) / 2). The verdict script's gap_over_2sd and the entry's own ts_online count use sqrt(a^2 + b^2). Under that pre-registered convention the counts are as follows. At 10% budget greedy is below by more than 2 SD in 8 of 12 judge cells (not 11), never above. At 25% it is above in 4 (not 5) and below in 2. At 50% it is above in 11 of 12 (not 12); the exception is 0.5B cell30 cheap_j + emb, ratio 1.025, z = 1.6. The ratio ranges quoted are correct. The same counts are repeated in the L1b hypothesis in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md and should be read with these values. The qualitative picture is unchanged: greedy loses at 10%, is mixed at 25%, beats the single-feature agreement rule at 50% on weak judge labels, and never beats the free-label static arm.
2. Caveat 8: the tie fractions (29% / 8.6% / 2.7%) are for top-two judge_p within 1e-6, not identical. Exactly identical top-two judge_p occurs in 28.8% (0.5B), 7.1% (3B) and 0.4% (7B) of split groups.
3. Caveat 4: 'at most 1%' holds for the primary configuration at 25% (7B 405 vs 401). Across all cells the augmented thresholds exceed the pre-registered pair by up to 1.8% (7B cell30 cheap_j at 10%: 184 vs 180.75). The kill is unaffected.
4. Caveat 1 is incomplete. The 3B rung passes the gate only on rollout-level AUC (0.654). Its unique-pair AUC is 0.6455 and its within-split-group rollout AUC is 0.639, both below 0.65. The gate as specified is rollout-level, so using 3B as primary with no fallback is correct. With 7B as primary the verdict is identical (delta -0.0017, ratio 0.892 at 25%, KILL).
5. G03 paragraph: 'a gain that rises as the proxy weakens' is not monotone. 7B has the lowest agreement AUC (0.784) but a gain of -0.108, below 3B at -0.096. With n = 4 and every delta AUC within noise, read the four points as a flat axis with no trend.
6. Scope. The pre-registered consequence of this kill is that further GPU spend on claim-3 variants on GSM8K stops, and the deliverable collapses to the proxy-error measurement plus the fixed-threshold recipe. The entry narrows the stop to 'group-level proxy variants' and lists L1c (GPU, GSM8K, claim 3, rollout-level reward model) as a follow-up. L1c is a claim-3 variant on GSM8K, so under the rule as written it stays blocked unless Rustom explicitly overrides the stop rule. L1a and L1b are CPU only and are not affected. The caveat that agreement is a free feature is fair, but the 'regime without free agreement' is a post hoc opening, not a pre-registered path, and a null there should not reopen the kill.

Independent robustness checks (CPU, run in the shell, no files written):
- Nonlinear delta AUC. I used HistGradientBoosting instead of logistic regression, with 3 CV seeds. Adding the 16 PCs changes CV AUC by -0.005 (E01), -0.011 (3B) and -0.015 (7B), so the null on delta AUC is not an artifact of a linear model.
- Split variance. The replay's cell fit/sim split is fixed at rng seed 0, so the 8-seed SD does not cover data-split variance. I reran 3B cell100 cheap_j + emb with split seeds 1, 2 and 3. The ts_online / best threshold ratio is 0.896, 0.877 and 0.873 at 25% (reported 0.904), 0.814, 0.765 and 0.799 at 10%, and 0.934, 0.918 and 0.935 at 50%. The kill margin (needs <= 1.05) does not depend on the split.
- Sample size. 3,000 groups, 1,054 errors in the replay population and 8 seeds are adequate for a kill of this margin. They would not be adequate for a positive claim of a few percent, because the seed SD understates split variance.

Corrected verdict: KILL stands for claim 3 on GSM8K (self-consistency and answer-only judge proxies, primary rung 3B, confirmed on 7B). Jaccard 0.24 is a set-size artifact and not evidence that proxy choice is a lever. The caveat 5 counts are corrected as above. L1c needs an explicit override of the stop rule before any GPU is spent.

## L1b 2026-10-18 inconclusive (claim 2 vs a fixed rule whose calibration labels are charged; greedy_online keeps a 3.7 to 7.2% edge on the judge labels but no label set clears 1.05 at both budgets)
Hypothesis: the only learned-over-fixed edge in the ledger (greedy_online over the single-feature agreement threshold at 50% budget on the weak judge labels, ratio 1.03 to 1.13) exists because the learned arm combines judge features, so a fixed multi-feature logistic rule whose n_cal calibration labels are charged to the same budget will match it. Prediction: ratio of the better learned arm to the best charged fixed rule is at most 1.02 at 25% and 50% budget on all three label sets (kill), or at least 1.05 with a bootstrap CI above 1 on two of three label sets at both budgets (pass).
Run (CPU only, no GPU, no generation; E04 feature tables): exp/l1b_sim_alloc.py is runs/E04/sim_alloc.py plus a split-seed argument and policies recal_charged_<n> for n_cal in {25, 50, 100, 200, 400} with n_cal <= budget // 2 (n_cal random groups are verified, charged and their hits counted, StandardScaler + balanced LogisticRegression on the same cheap feature set fit on them, remaining groups ranked by predicted error and verified until the budget is spent; random order if the calibration sample has 0 or all positives). Groups cell100, E01 label proxy_error with the six cheap features, Qwen2.5-3B and Qwen2.5-7B-AWQ judge_error with the six plus four judge features, budgets 10 / 25 / 50% of the sim population (about 2,200 groups per split), K = 1, split seeds 0 to 3, policy seeds 0 to 7, 12 jobs in parallel. Regression gate passed first: at split seed 0 all 108 policy-budget cells (3 label sets x 3 budgets x 12 existing policies) reproduce runs/E04/sim_<name>_cell100_cheap.json with identical per-seed hits (exp/l1b_gate_check.py). Selection protocol: the best fixed rule (thr_agreement or any recal_charged_<n>) and the better learned arm are chosen per (label set, budget) on policy seeds 0 to 3 and every verdict number is on seeds 4 to 7 over the 4 splits (16 paired runs); ratio = mean learned hits / mean fixed hits, CI = percentile bootstrap over the 16 paired runs (10,000 resamples), plus the SD of the 4 split ratios and a split-cluster bootstrap. Scripts exp/L1b.sh, exp/l1b_sim_alloc.py, exp/l1b_summarize.py; results runs/L1b/ (sim_*_split*.json, summary.log, result.json, done.flag). I also re-implemented recal_charged independently for three (label set, split, budget, n_cal) cells and matched the saved per-seed hits exactly.

Errors caught (mean over 16 held-out runs). Learned = greedy_online (it was the selected learned arm in all 9 cells); fixed = the selected best charged fixed rule; ts_online = prompt-space layout arm.

| label set | budget | uniform | thr_agreement | best charged fixed (selected) | greedy_online | greedy / fixed (95% CI, SD of 4 split ratios) | ts_online / fixed | free-label static (upper bound) | oracle |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E01 | 25% | 83.8 | 269.6 | thr_agreement 269.6 | 267.4 | 0.992 (0.986 to 0.998, 0.004) | 0.810 | 276.8 | 327.8 |
| E01 | 50% | 165.2 | 316.1 | thr_agreement 316.1 | 315.8 | 0.999 (0.998 to 1.001, 0.002) | 0.967 | 316.5 | 327.8 |
| 3B | 25% | 268.6 | 434.0 | thr_agreement 434.0 | 450.9 | 1.039 (1.028 to 1.049, 0.011) | 0.930 | 469.0 | 561.2 |
| 3B | 50% | 536.4 | 779.2 | recal_charged_100 783.3 | 835.5 | 1.067 (1.060 to 1.073, 0.006) | 0.944 | 843.0 | 1069.2 |
| 7B-AWQ | 25% | 249.2 | 412.5 | thr_agreement 412.5 | 442.2 | 1.072 (1.054 to 1.090, 0.036) | 0.937 | 470.0 | 561.2 |
| 7B-AWQ | 50% | 493.8 | 713.2 | recal_charged_200 761.2 | 789.5 | 1.037 (1.026 to 1.050, 0.011) | 0.933 | 821.8 | 986.8 |

At 10% budget greedy_online is below the best fixed rule on every label set (0.858 on E01, 0.950 on 3B, 0.947 on 7B-AWQ, all CIs below 1). The charged recalibrated rule beats thr_agreement only at 50% on the judge labels (3B recal_charged_100 783 vs 779, 7B recal_charged_200 761 vs 713); at 10% and 25% every recal_charged_<n> is below thr_agreement on every label set (for example 3B 25%: best n_cal 100 gives 420 vs 434), so charging the calibration labels costs the multi-feature rule more than the judge features add until the budget is large. greedy_online over thr_agreement alone: 3B 1.039 / 1.072 and 7B 1.072 / 1.107 at 25 / 50%, E01 0.992 / 0.999, consistent with the E04 range 1.03 to 1.13. Free-label static is above greedy_online in all 6 cells at 25 and 50% (3B 25%: 469.0 vs 450.9; 7B 50%: 821.8 vs 789.5).

Verdict by the pre-registered rules: INCONCLUSIVE. Pass needs ratio >= 1.05 with CI lower bound above 1 at both 25% and 50% on at least two of three label sets; no label set clears both (E01: 0.992 / 0.999; 3B: 1.039 / 1.067; 7B-AWQ: 1.072 / 1.037; the near misses are 3B at 25%, CI upper bound 1.049, and 7B at 50%, CI lower bound 1.026). Kill needs ratio <= 1.02 in all six cells; it holds in only 2 of 6 (both E01). The result is the same under a sensitivity analysis biased against the learned arm (fixed rule and learned arm both picked as the max over the held-out seeds): the ratios are unchanged because the selected rule is also the held-out maximum in every cell. ts_online is below the best charged fixed rule in all 9 cells (0.597 to 0.967) and below greedy_online in all 9 (0.695 to 0.968), so prompt-space layout costs exploration and buys nothing, as in E04.

What it changes. The hypothesis that a charged fixed multi-feature rule would match greedy_online is rejected on the two judge label sets (greedy_online keeps a 3.7 to 7.2% edge with CIs that exclude 1 in all four judge cells at 25% and 50%) and supported on the majority-vote label (E01, ratio 0.99 to 1.00). So the E04 G01 worry is partly resolved: charging the calibration labels does not erase the greedy_online edge on weak judge labels, but the edge is smaller than the 1.05 bar in the two near-miss cells and appears only at 25% and 50% budget. Claim 2 (learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget) is therefore not supported as a verdict, not refuted either: the evidence is a small, consistent greedy_online edge on the 3B and 7B-AWQ judge labels and nothing on E01. Interpretation notes carried from the spec: greedy_online is itself a periodically recalibrated multi-feature threshold (G01 RecalibratedThreshold) that has no layout, so this edge is evidence for online recalibration, not for prompt-space learning; claim 3 stays dead (ts_online below greedy_online everywhere). One mechanism worth stating plainly: greedy_online's calibration labels are collected on-policy from the top-scored groups, so they double as hits, while recal_charged spends n_cal verifications at the base rate (about 44 to 48% on the judge labels, 14% on E01); the edge may partly reflect that sequential design rather than any property of a learned scorer, and a fixed rule can only close it with a better calibration design than a random sample. The Phase 1 GRPO harness is not justified by this result under the pre-registered pass rule (no label set passes), and the rule-of-thumb deliverable remains the agreement threshold or a recalibrated multi-feature rule at 50% budget.

Caveats. (1) The 4 splits overlap heavily (each sim population is a 75% draw of the same 3,000 groups) and the 8 policy seeds within a split share a population, so the 16 paired runs are not independent; the SD of the 4 split ratios (0.002 to 0.037) and the split-cluster CIs (for example 3B 25% 1.030 to 1.047, 7B 25% 1.041 to 1.099) are the more honest spread, and none of the judge cells in the 25% / 50% grid has a cluster CI that includes 1. (2) The best fixed rule was selected on seeds 0 to 3 of the same splits, so the selection is independent of policy seeds but not of the split populations. (3) All results are one task (GSM8K), one rollout policy (Qwen2.5-1.5B), one fixed family (balanced logistic regression on the six or ten cheap features, no PCs, random calibration sample); a stratified or agreement-seeded calibration design might close part of the gap and was not tried. (4) The 3B judge rung passed the E04 validity gate only barely (rollout AUC 0.654), see the E04 entry. (5) The spec's reference value "3B at 25%: greedy_online about 423" is the cheap+emb configuration; the reproduced cheap configuration is greedy_online 430.9 and thr_agreement 422.1, and the gate is the exact per-seed match to runs/E04/sim_<name>_cell100_cheap.json. L1c (GPU) stays blocked by the E04 stop rule and was not touched.

## L1b 2026-10-18 inconclusive
This is the verified entry for the same experiment as the run-agent entry above, with the added checks and the follow-ups. Every number below was re-derived from the per-seed hits in runs/L1b/sim_*_split*.json with a separate script and a different bootstrap seed, not copied from summary.log. All 9 cell ratios match to 3 decimals and the CIs to within 0.002.

Hypothesis: the only learned-over-fixed edge in the ledger (greedy_online, a periodically refit multi-feature scorer with no prompt-space layout, beating the single-feature agreement threshold at 50% budget on the weak judge labels, ratio 1.03 to 1.13) exists because the learned arm can combine judge features, not because of prompt-space structure. A fixed multi-feature logistic rule whose n_cal random calibration labels are charged against the same verification budget will match it, so claim 2 has no support on this machine. Pre-registered: pass if a learned arm is at least 1.05x the best charged fixed rule with the bootstrap 95% CI above 1 at both 25% and 50% budget on at least 2 of 3 label sets; kill if the best charged fixed rule is within 2% of or ahead of the better learned arm (ratio at most 1.02) in all 6 (label set, budget) cells at 25% and 50%; anything between is inconclusive.

Run: CPU only, no GPU, no new generation. GSM8K train, 3,000 groups of 8 Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct rollouts (p0), proxies from E01 (majority vote, label proxy_error) and E04 (answer-only judges Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ, label judge_error). Allocation replay on the E04 feature tables, bucket column cell100, about 2,200 sim groups per split (2,180 / 2,207 / 2,285 / 2,315), K = 1, budgets 10 / 25 / 50% of the sim population, split seeds 0 to 3, policy seeds 0 to 7, 12 (label set, split) jobs in parallel. exp/l1b_sim_alloc.py is runs/E04/sim_alloc.py plus a split-seed argument and recal_charged_n for n_cal in 25, 50, 100, 200, 400 with n_cal at most budget // 2 (n_cal random groups are verified, charged to the budget and their hits counted, balanced logistic regression on the same cheap features, rest ranked and verified). Regression gate passed first: 108 of 108 policy-budget cells at split seed 0 match runs/E04/sim_<name>_cell100_cheap.json with identical per-seed hits. Selection protocol: the best charged fixed rule (thr_agreement or any recal_charged_n) and the better learned arm (greedy_online or ts_online) are picked per (label set, budget) on policy seeds 0 to 3; all verdict numbers are on seeds 4 to 7 over the 4 splits (16 paired runs). Files: runs/L1b/summary.log, result.json, sim_*_split*.json, gate/gate_check.json; scripts exp/l1b_sim_alloc.py, l1b_summarize.py, l1b_gate_check.py, L1b.sh.

Mean errors caught over the 16 held-out runs. Fixed = best charged fixed rule selected on seeds 0 to 3 (thr_agreement unless named). Static is the free-label upper bound only. Ratio CIs are percentile bootstrap over the 16 paired runs. The better learned arm was greedy_online in all 9 cells.

| label set | budget | uniform | thr_agreement | best charged fixed | greedy_online | greedy / fixed (95% CI) | ts_online / fixed | static (free labels) | oracle |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E01 | 10% | 33.2 | 166.4 | 166.4 | 142.8 | 0.858 (0.844 to 0.872) | 0.597 | 164.8 | 224.2 |
| E01 | 25% | 83.8 | 269.6 | 269.6 | 267.4 | 0.992 (0.986 to 0.998) | 0.810 | 276.8 | 327.8 |
| E01 | 50% | 165.2 | 316.1 | 316.1 | 315.8 | 0.999 (0.997 to 1.001) | 0.967 | 316.5 | 327.8 |
| 3B judge | 10% | 106.9 | 194.9 | 194.9 | 185.1 | 0.950 (0.925 to 0.971) | 0.827 | 205.8 | 224.2 |
| 3B judge | 25% | 268.6 | 434.0 | 434.0 | 450.9 | 1.039 (1.028 to 1.049) | 0.930 | 469.0 | 561.2 |
| 3B judge | 50% | 536.4 | 779.2 | 783.3 (recal_charged_100) | 835.5 | 1.067 (1.060 to 1.073) | 0.944 | 843.0 | 1069.2 |
| 7B-AWQ judge | 10% | 98.3 | 178.1 | 178.1 | 168.6 | 0.947 (0.927 to 0.968) | 0.875 | 187.5 | 224.2 |
| 7B-AWQ judge | 25% | 249.2 | 412.5 | 412.5 | 442.2 | 1.072 (1.054 to 1.090) | 0.937 | 470.0 | 561.2 |
| 7B-AWQ judge | 50% | 493.8 | 713.2 | 761.2 (recal_charged_200) | 789.5 | 1.037 (1.026 to 1.050) | 0.933 | 821.8 | 986.8 |

Split-level spread: SD of the 4 per-split ratios is 0.002 to 0.037 (largest: 3B 10% 0.037, 7B 25% 0.036); the split-cluster bootstrap CIs exclude 1 in every judge cell at 25% and 50% (smallest margin: 7B 25% 1.041 to 1.099, 3B 25% 1.030 to 1.047). ts_online / greedy_online is 0.695 to 0.968 in all 9 cells. recal_charged_n is below thr_agreement at 10% and 25% on every label set and beats it only at 50% on the judge labels (3B +0.5%, 7B +6.7%).

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: INCONCLUSIVE. Pass needs ratio at least 1.05 with CI above 1 at both 25% and 50% on at least 2 of 3 label sets. E01 fails (0.992, 0.999). 3B passes at 50% (1.067) but fails at 25% (1.039, CI upper bound 1.049). 7B-AWQ passes at 25% (1.072) but fails at 50% (1.037). Zero label sets qualify. Kill needs ratio at most 1.02 in all 6 cells; it holds in only 2 of 6 (both E01), and the 4 judge cells sit at 1.037 to 1.072. Two cells (3B 25%, 7B 50%) fall in the pre-registered inconclusive band 1.02 to 1.05. The result is identical under the sensitivity analysis that picks fixed rule and learned arm as the held-out maximum (the selected arm is the held-out maximum in every cell).

Checks on review. (1) Recomputed all 9 cells from per-seed hits (ratio, bootstrap CI, split-cluster CI, SD of split ratios): match. (2) Read recal_charged in exp/l1b_sim_alloc.py: the n_cal calibration groups are placed first in the order, so they count as spent and their hits count; the fit uses only those labels and the cheap feature set; the 0 or all-positive fallback is random order. Budget accounting is equal across arms. (3) The fixed-rule family is not too narrow in the obvious label-free direction: adding the other label-free rules already in the sim (thr_agree_margin, thr_margin_split, thr_margin) and picking the best on seeds 0 to 3 leaves greedy / best label-free at 0.945 / 1.039 / 1.072 (3B at 10 / 25 / 50%) and 0.936 / 1.065 / 1.103 (7B), so nothing there closes the edge. (4) Headroom: the free-label static arm over the best charged fixed rule is only 1.027 (25%) and 1.001 (50%) on E01, 1.081 and 1.076 on 3B, and 1.139 and 1.079 on 7B-AWQ. So E01 can never pass by construction (no headroom), and the pass rule therefore required BOTH judge sets to pass at BOTH budgets. greedy_online captures 48% (3B 25%), 87% (3B 50%), 52% (7B 25%) and 47% (7B 50%) of the gap between the best charged fixed rule and the free-label static arm; reaching 1.05 at 3B 25% would have needed 62%. The 1.05 bar is therefore close to the ceiling of what these features allow, which matters for how to read two near misses.

What it changes. Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget) is not supported as stated and not killed. What survives is narrow: on the two weak judge labels, an online-recalibrated multi-feature scorer (greedy_online) beats the best charged fixed rule by 3.7 to 7.2% at 25% and 50% budget with CIs and split-cluster CIs excluding 1, but it loses to the plain agreement threshold at 10% budget on every label set (0.86 to 0.95) and ties it on the majority-vote label (0.99 to 1.00). The universal form of the claim ("any fixed rule at equal budget") is contradicted at 10% and on E01, so it cannot be asserted even if the judge-label edge replicates. The G01 worry is partly resolved: charging calibration labels does not erase the greedy_online edge on judge labels, but it shrinks it relative to the uncharged comparison (7B 50% goes from 1.107 over thr_agreement to 1.037 over the best charged rule). Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space) stays dead: the prompt-space layout arm ts_online is below the best charged fixed rule in all 9 cells (0.597 to 0.967) and below layout-free greedy_online in all 9, so whatever edge exists comes from recalibrating a multi-feature scorer, not from layout; combined with the E04 delta AUC of about 0 for embeddings, the layout arm costs exploration and buys nothing. The Phase 1 GRPO harness is not justified by the pre-registered pass rule. The deliverable for claims 1 and 2 stays the fixed-threshold recipe: the agreement threshold at 10 to 25% budget, a recalibrated multi-feature rule at 50%, with online recalibration as a 4 to 7% add-on on weak judge labels only.

Caveats. (1) The 4 splits are 75% draws of the same 3,000 groups and the 8 policy seeds within a split share a population, so the 16 paired runs are not independent; the original L1b spec asked for a bootstrap over groups, which was not done because per-group selections were not saved. The split-cluster CI has only 4 overlapping clusters, so treat all CIs as lower bounds on the real uncertainty about a fresh population. (2) One task (GSM8K), one rollout policy (Qwen2.5-1.5B), one fixed family (balanced logistic regression, random calibration sample, no PCs). The calibration design is the weak point of the fixed comparator: recal_charged spends n_cal verifications at the base rate (44 to 48% on judge labels), while greedy_online collects its calibration labels on-policy from the top-scored groups, so they double as hits. The edge may be a calibration-design effect rather than a property of learning, and a seeded or stratified calibration sample was not tried. (3) Sim populations of about 2,200 groups are far larger than one GRPO step; at the per-step batch scale the calibration cost is a larger fraction of the budget and the result may not transfer. (4) The 3B judge passed the E04 validity gate only barely (rollout AUC 0.654) and is close to a constant No. (5) The spec's reference value "3B at 25%: greedy_online about 423" is the cheap+emb configuration; the reproduced cheap configuration is greedy_online 430.9, thr_agreement 422.1 at split 0. (6) L1c (GPU) remains blocked by the E04 stop rule and was not touched.

Follow-ups (ranked by expected information value; ids match the backlog section 'Proposed by the loop', iteration 2):
- L2a. Decompose the greedy_online edge with a stronger charged fixed baseline (CPU only, about 15 to 20 min). Add seeded_charged_n (verify the n lowest-agreement groups first so the calibration labels are also productive hits, fit the same logistic rule, rank the rest) and a greedy_online grid over first-fit size n0 in 25, 50, 100 and refit period R in n0, 4 n0. Same selection protocol (choose on seeds 0 to 3, report on 4 to 7). Kill: best extended charged fixed rule within 1.02x of greedy_online in all 4 judge cells at 25% and 50%, which means the edge is a calibration-design effect and claim 2 has no support beyond a one-shot recalibrated threshold. Pass: ratio at least 1.03 with the split-cluster CI above 1 in all 4 judge cells, which means repeated refitting on its own labels is the active ingredient. Information value: highest, it can settle claim 2 with no GPU and tells the harness design what a learned allocator must do beyond fitting once.
- L2b. Batch-scale and independent-population sweep (CPU only, about 20 min). Draw 16 random subsets of m in 250, 500, 1000 groups from the 3,000 (pairwise overlap about 8%, 17%, 33%), split each 25 / 75, and run thr_agreement, recal_charged (n_cal capped at budget // 2), greedy_online, ts_online and static at 10 / 25 / 50% on the two judge labels. This gives population-sampling variance that the 4 overlapping splits lack, and tests the regime a per-step GRPO allocator actually sees. Kill: greedy_online / best charged fixed at most 1.0 at m of 500 or less on both judge sets, so the edge needs a large calibration pool and a per-step allocator cannot reach it. Pass: ratio at least 1.03 with the CI over the 16 subsets above 1 at m = 500 for both judge sets at 25% and 50%. Information value: high, it decides whether Phase 1 (per-step allocation) is even the right deployment unit and whether the 3.7 to 7.2% survives a fresh population.
- L2c. Fresh-population replication (GPU, two jobs, about 30 min total; run only if L2a does not kill, and only with Rustom's explicit go-ahead because it is GPU spend on GSM8K under the E04 stop rule, even though it is a claim-2 test and not a claim-3 variant). Generate 8 Qwen2.5-1.5B rollouts for 3,000 of the 4,473 GSM8K train prompts not used by p0, score with the 3B and 7B-AWQ judges (exp/p0_judge_score.py), and rerun the unchanged L1b pipeline (same n_cal grid, same selection protocol). Pass: greedy_online / best charged fixed at least 1.03 with CI above 1 in at least 3 of the 4 judge cells. Kill: at most 1.02 in all 4. Information value: medium to high, the only way to get a population independent of the 3,000 groups every result so far shares; worth the GPU only if L2a and L2b leave the edge standing.

**Critic note (L1b, adversarial review 2026-10-18).** The INCONCLUSIVE verdict stands, and so does every verdict number. What I re-derived from the raw files (sim_*_split*.json, summary.log, result.json, gate/, the scripts and runs/E04/feats_*.parquet): all 9 cell ratios, the paired-run CIs (my bootstrap seed differs and agrees to within 0.001), split SDs, split-cluster CIs, the selected fixed rule and learned arm per cell, the uniform / thr_agreement / static / oracle means, the ts_online ratios (0.597 to 0.967 against the fixed rule, 0.695 to 0.968 against greedy_online) and the label-free robustness ratios (0.945 / 1.039 / 1.072 on 3B, 0.936 / 1.065 / 1.103 on 7B). The regression gate holds: besides gate_check.json, I compared the main-run sim_*_split0.json directly with runs/E04/sim_<name>_cell100_cheap.json and found 0 differences in all 108 policy-budget cells, and gate/g_*.json are identical to the main-run split 0 files. I wrote a separate recal_charged implementation and matched the saved per-seed hits exactly in 4 cells (E01 split 1 25% n_cal 100; 3B split 2 50% n_cal 100; 7B split 3 50% n_cal 200; 7B split 0 25% n_cal 50). The n_cal calibration groups are first in the order, so they are charged and their hits are counted, and every arm spends int(frac * n_sim) verifications at K = 1. There is no leakage: the allocator features are the six cheap columns plus the four judge columns, the judge prompt contains only the question and the extracted answer, and gold, pass_rate and proxy_error are used only as labels. No crash, truncation or warning appears in the 12 job logs (run.log is empty, exit_code 0). The verdict follows from the pre-registered thresholds: no label set has ratio >= 1.05 with CI above 1 at both budgets (3B 25% is 1.039 with CI upper bound 1.049, 7B 50% is 1.037), and the kill condition holds in only 2 of 6 cells. It is also robust to the choice of fixed family, because against thr_agreement alone 3B at 25% is still 1.039 and E01 is still below 1.

Corrections to statements in the entries above (none changes the verdict):
1. The free-label static arm is not a ceiling. Check (4) of the verified entry says the 1.05 bar is 'close to the ceiling of what these features allow' and that E01 'can never pass by construction'. Static is fit on 25 disjoint embedding cells with a linear model, and the E04 ledger already records static below greedy_online in 2 of 48 cells. As a feature-ceiling probe I ranked the split 0 sim groups with a 5-fold cross-validated HistGradientBoosting model on the same features (a probe that sees about 80% of the labels, not a policy). At 25% budget on 3B it catches 503 errors, against static 467, greedy_online 430.9 and thr_agreement 422.1 (1.19x the agreement threshold); at 50% on 3B it catches 886 against static 836 and greedy_online 828.1. On 7B (465 vs static 456 at 25%, 804 vs 807 at 50%) and E01 (255 vs 264 at 25%, 300 vs 299 at 50%) the probe is close to static. So the two 'captures X% of the gap to static' figures remain arithmetically right, but 'static' is only a reference arm, the 3B headroom is larger than the entry implies, and the 3B near miss (1.039) cannot be excused as being near a feature ceiling. The E01 point (no headroom with these features) holds empirically for linear and boosted models, not 'by construction' (oracle / best fixed is 1.22 at 25%).
2. The deliverable line 'a recalibrated multi-feature rule at 50% budget' is supported on the 7B label only (recal_charged_200 761.2 against thr_agreement 713.2, +6.7%). On 3B it is a tie (783.3 against 779.2, +0.5%) and on E01 every recal_charged_n is below thr_agreement at 50% (best 312.6 against 316.1). Read it as: at 50% the agreement threshold is within 0.5% of the best charged rule on two of three label sets.
3. Split-cluster CIs: 'smallest margin: 7B 25% 1.041 to 1.099' is wrong. The smallest lower bounds among the four judge cells at 25% and 50% are 7B 50% (1.027 to 1.046) and 3B 25% (1.030 to 1.047). With only 4 overlapping clusters the percentile bootstrap is a crude interval anyway, so none of these CIs should be read as 95% coverage for a fresh population.
4. The gate count '108 = 3 label sets x 3 budgets x 12 existing policies' is a coincidence of arithmetic: E01 has 10 policies per budget (30 cells) and each judge set has 13 (39 cells each), total 108.
5. The splits are cell-level, not question-level. The 25 / 75 split shuffles the 100 cell100 embedding clusters (25 go to the static fit, 75 form the sim population, hence n_buckets 75 and varying n_sim). 'A 75% draw of the same 3,000 groups' is true only in that sense, which also means the static arm is fit on clusters it never sees in the sim.
6. Rule provenance. The older L1b item in the follow-up list (pass: more than 2 SD at 25% and 50%) was superseded by the ratio-and-CI rule used for this run; the verdict, and the statement that the Phase 1 GRPO harness is not justified, rest on the 1.05 ratio bar and I did not evaluate the 2 SD wording. E01 50% CI is 0.998 to 1.001 in summary.log and the first entry (the verified entry's 0.997 is a re-derivation difference of 0.001).

Sample size and seeds. For the question the verdict answers (does any label set clear 1.05 at both budgets on this population), 4 splits x 4 held-out seeds are adequate: seed SDs are small against the distance to the bar in the two near-miss cells, and the result does not depend on which fixed family is used. They are not adequate to claim that the 3.7 to 7.2% edge exists on a fresh population, since all 16 paired runs come from one set of 3,000 groups with heavily overlapping splits (L2b and L2c address this).

Corrected verdict: INCONCLUSIVE (no label set passes, kill holds in 2 of 6 cells). Claim 2 is neither supported nor killed on this machine; greedy_online (online recalibration, no prompt-space layout) beats the best charged fixed rule by 3.7 to 7.2% on the two judge labels at 25% and 50%, ties it on E01, and loses to the agreement threshold at 10%; ts_online is below the best charged fixed rule in all 9 cells, so there is no support for prompt-space learning. The Phase 1 GRPO harness is not justified by this result. The statement that static is a ceiling on what these features allow is withdrawn.


## L2a 2026-10-18 pass
Hypothesis: the 3.7 to 7.2% greedy_online edge over the best charged fixed rule (L1b; 3B and 7B-AWQ judge labels, 25% and 50% budget) is a calibration-design effect, not a repeated-learning effect. greedy_online collects every label after its first fit on-policy from the top-scored groups, so those labels double as hits, while recal_charged spends n_cal verifications at the base rate. A one-shot fixed rule whose calibration labels are the lowest-agreement groups (productive, label-free selection) should close the gap. Prediction: best greedy variant / best member of the extended charged fixed family is at most 1.02 in all four judge cells (kill for claim 2 beyond a one-shot recalibrated threshold). Falsifier (pass): ratio at least 1.03 with the split-cluster CI above 1 in all four cells, meaning repeated refitting on its own labels is the active ingredient.

Run (CPU only, no GPU, no generation; E04 feature tables, 3,000 GSM8K prompts x 8 Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct rollouts, label judge_error from the Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ answer-only judges, ten cheap+judge features, K = 1): exp/l2a_sim_alloc.py is exp/l1b_sim_alloc.py with ts_online, ts_flat, ts_static, neighbor, static_matched dropped, the reference policies kept (uniform, static as an upper bound only, oracle, thr_agreement, recal_charged_<n>, greedy_online at R = n0 = max(budget // 8, 40)), and new policies: seeded_charged_<n> (the n lowest-agreement groups, random ties, charged and counted as hits, then the same StandardScaler + balanced LogisticRegression ranks the rest), split_charged_<n> (n random groups drawn only from n_distinct > 1, guard against range restriction making the baseline artificially weak), and a learned grid greedy_n<n0>_R<R> and greedy_seeded_n<n0>_R<R> for n0 in 25, 50, 100 and R in n0, 4*n0, with n <= budget // 2 and n in 25, 50, 100, 200, 400. Split seeds 0 to 3 (about 2,180 to 2,315 sim groups each; 1,054 to 1,095 errors on 3B, 964 to 1,009 on 7B-AWQ), policy seeds 0 to 7, budgets 10 / 25 / 50% of the sim population, 8 jobs in parallel (about 4 min wall for the full run, 12 min for the whole step including the gate). Selection protocol as in L1b: best fixed rule (16 candidates at 50%: thr_agreement plus recal / seeded / split at each n) and best learned variant (13 candidates) chosen per (label set, budget) on policy seeds 0 to 3 averaged over splits; every verdict number is on seeds 4 to 7 over the 4 splits (16 paired runs). Files: exp/l2a_sim_alloc.py, exp/l2a_gate_check.py, exp/l2a_summarize.py, exp/L2a.sh, exp/l2a_verify.py (my audit script), runs/L2a/ (sim_*_split*.json, summary.log, result.json, gate/, done.flag, exit_code = 0).

Audit on review (I re-derived the numbers rather than trusting the run report). (1) Regression gate: 62 checks at split 0, 0 differences (runs/L2a/gate/gate_check.json); I extended it to the full-run files: all reference policies (uniform, static, oracle, thr_agreement, recal_charged_*, greedy_online) match runs/L1b per-seed hits exactly on all 4 splits and 3 budgets for both label sets, 216 checks, 0 differences. (2) Independent re-implementation (label by label, written from the spec, not from the sim code) of greedy_n25_R25, greedy_seeded_n25_R25, greedy_n100_R400, seeded_charged_100, split_charged_100 and recal_charged_100 on 3B split 0 at 25% budget reproduces the saved per-seed hits exactly for all 8 seeds in all six policies. (3) The four headline ratios and cluster CIs recomputed from the raw sim_*.json agree with summary.log to three decimals, and the selection picks match. (4) No policy exceeds its budget in hits (checked on 3B split 0), and no sim log contains an error. (5) Leave-one-split-out ratios stay between 1.048 and 1.161, so no single split carries the pass (the 7B 25% outlier split at 1.250 only moves that cell between 1.106 and 1.161).

Errors caught (mean over 16 held-out runs; selected fixed = best member of {thr_agreement, recal, seeded, split charged} picked on seeds 0 to 3; selected learned = best of greedy_online and the 12 grid variants):

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Sensitivity biased against the learned arm (fixed and learned both taken as the maximum over the held-out seeds): 1.053, 1.060, 1.105, 1.047 for 3B 25%, 3B 50%, 7B 25%, 7B 50%, all above 1.03 (cluster CI lower bounds 1.037, 1.048, 1.085, 1.034); 0 of 4 kill cells.

2x2 decomposition (best member per family chosen on seeds 0 to 3, held-out ratio to the selected best fixed rule), cells 3B 25% / 3B 50% / 7B 25% / 7B 50%:

| first phase x schedule | 3B 25% | 3B 50% | 7B 25% | 7B 50% |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| random labels, one-shot fit (recal_charged) | 0.968 | 0.973 | 1.000 | 0.996 |
| agreement-seeded labels, one-shot fit (seeded_charged) | 0.977 | 0.990 | 0.610 | 0.754 |
| random labels, repeated refit (greedy_online + greedy_n*_R*) | 1.050 | 1.060 | 1.139 | 1.055 |
| agreement-seeded labels, repeated refit (greedy_seeded_n*_R*) | 1.053 | 1.057 | 1.123 | 1.061 |
| random labels in n_distinct > 1 groups, one-shot (split_charged, the guard) | 0.996 | 1.000 | 1.000 | 1.000 |

Refit-frequency dose-response (random first phase, held-out mean / selected best fixed, same n0 = 25 so only the number of refits changes): R = 25 gives 1.050 / 1.060 / 1.139 / 1.055; R = 100 gives 1.012 / 1.028 / 1.099 / 1.045 (3B 25% falls below the 1.03 bar). Larger n0 also lowers it: n100_R100 gives 1.017 / 1.051 / 1.071 / 1.048 and n100_R400 gives 0.984 / 1.034 / 1.028 / 1.043. For the agreement-seeded first phase, infrequent refits are harmful: greedy_seeded_n25_R100 is 0.970 / 0.997 / 0.998 / 1.026 and greedy_seeded_n100_R400 is 0.952 / 0.999 / 0.615 / 0.866, so refit frequency is what repairs a range-restricted start (the seeded fit alone, seeded_charged, collapses on 7B to 0.610 and 0.754). The best learned variant is n0 = R = 25, the smallest values in the grid, in all four cells.

Verdict against the pre-registered rules: PASS. The regression gate passed (a failed gate would have voided the run). Pass needs ratio >= 1.03 with the split-cluster CI lower bound above 1 in all four cells: 1.050 (1.043), 1.060 (1.048), 1.139 (1.089), 1.061 (1.054), all four hold. Kill needs ratio <= 1.02 in all four: it holds in 0 of 4, also 0 of 4 under the held-out-max sensitivity. The unchanged L1b greedy_online alone also meets the pass rule in all four cells (1.039, 1.037, 1.094, 1.033 with cluster CI lower bounds 1.030, 1.028, 1.050, 1.022); the 7B 50% reference cell is the thinnest (1.033 against the 1.03 bar), so the pass does not rest on the grid search there but it is close. The stronger charged baselines did not close the gap: the selected fixed rule is split_charged in three of four cells (the range-restriction guard) and thr_agreement in the fourth, seeded_charged is weak or collapses on 7B, and the best held-out member of any one-shot family is at most 1.031 times the selected fixed rule (split_charged_200 on 7B 25%; 1.013 for split_charged_200 on 7B 50%; at most 1.004 elsewhere).

What it changes. The kill hypothesis (calibration-design effect) is rejected: giving the fixed rule productive calibration labels (seeded) or range-guarded ones (split) does not close the 3.7 to 7.2% gap, and the grid shows the edge scales with how often the scorer is refit on its own collected labels (at n0 = 25, R = 25 beats R = 100 in all four cells; at n0 = 100, R = 100 beats R = 400 in all four cells), while the first-phase design barely matters once refits are frequent (random and agreement-seeded first phases tie at 1.050 vs 1.053, 1.060 vs 1.057, 1.139 vs 1.123, 1.055 vs 1.061). Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget) gains its first defensible support in the offline replay, with scope that must travel with it: weak 3B and 7B-AWQ judge labels, 25% and 50% budget only (the edge reverses at 10%, 0.962 and 0.969, where the free agreement threshold wins), a single population of 3,000 groups, K = 1, and the universal form "any fixed rule at equal budget" is still contradicted at 10% and on the E01 majority-vote label (0.99 to 1.00, no headroom). The "learned allocator" that wins is a plain multi-feature logistic scorer with no prompt-space layout, refit every 25 labels from a 25-label random start. Claim 3 (edge comes from structure in prompt space) is untouched by this test and stays not supported: the winning arm has no layout, ts_online was below it in every L1b cell, and E04 measured embedding and cell-encoding gains at about 0; L2a adds that the edge is explained by the fitting schedule on cheap+judge features. Harness design implication for Phase 1: the allocator should be a frequently refit scorer (a refit about every 25 labels, or every 2 to 5% of the budget), started from a small random sample, with the free agreement threshold as the fallback at small budget (10% and below). The Phase 1 GRPO harness is still not justified by this alone: the pre-registered next step is L2b (does the edge survive batch-scale populations of 250 to 1,000 groups, where 25 random start labels are 10% to 2.5% of the pool), and L2c (fresh GSM8K population, GPU) still needs Rustom's explicit override of the E04 stop rule.

Caveats. (1) All 16 paired runs come from one set of 3,000 groups; the 4 splits are overlapping 75% draws, so the split-cluster CIs rest on 4 overlapping clusters and the per-split SD is the more honest spread (0.007 to 0.075; at 7B 25% the split ratios are 1.116, 1.078, 1.250, 1.124). A fresh population is the real test (L2b, L2c). (2) The best learned variant sits at the corner of the grid (n0 = R = 25, the smallest values) in all four cells, so the optimum is not bracketed and the ratio could be larger (or unstable) at smaller n0 and R; the fixed grids are also truncated at n = 400 although budget // 2 is about 545 at 50% (seeded_charged is still rising at n = 400 on 3B 50%: 630, 631, 743, 763, 797 against the best fixed 805, and split_charged_400 was the selected rule on 7B 50%), so the fixed family may be slightly understated at 50%; the sensitivity analysis bounds this at 1.047 to 1.105 but does not remove it. (3) Selection of the fixed rule on seeds 0 to 3 picked split_charged_100 on 7B 25% (404.2 held-out) although thr_agreement (412.5) and split_charged_200 (416.8) were better on seeds 4 to 7, so the primary ratio there (1.139) is optimistic and the sensitivity (1.105) is the safer number; the 13 learned and 16 fixed candidates share the same splits for selection and evaluation, so some winner's curse remains. (4) Offline replay with static labels: a real GRPO loop changes the rollout policy between steps (non-stationarity, E13) and a scorer trained on its own top-ranked labels may drift or collapse; the sim cannot show that. (5) The weak judge labels only: the 3B rung passed the E04 validity gate by 0.004 AUC. (6) The label-source mechanism is not isolated: repeated refit confounds the number of fits with the on-policy label distribution (top-scored groups), see L3b. (7) The 2x2 uses best-member-per-family chosen on seeds 0 to 3, so the one-shot rows and the repeated rows are compared at their selected members; the conclusion does not depend on it because even the best held-out member of every one-shot family is at most 1.031 times the selected fixed rule.

Follow-ups, ranked by expected information value.
1. L2b (existing backlog id, run next, CPU about 20 min; two edits: use the L2a selected learner n0 = R = 25 and also smaller n0 = R = 10, and use the extended fixed family from L2a with n capped at budget // 2). Draws 16 independent subsets of m = 250, 500, 1000 groups from the 3,000 so the CIs cluster over subsets and the scale approaches one GRPO step. Information value: highest, it is the only CPU test of whether the L2a edge survives a population that is not the shared 3,000 and a small-pool regime where the 25 random start labels and the 25-label refit cadence are a large share of the budget. Kill if the ratio is at most 1.0 at m of 500 or less on both label sets at 25% and 50%.
2. L3a. Bracket both grids and map the refit-count dose-response (CPU about 15 to 20 min): learned n0 in 10, 15, 25 and R in 1, 5, 10, 25; fixed n up to budget // 2 including 550; plus a staged rule (n random labels then k - 1 refits at equal label intervals, k = 2, 4, 8) as the bridge between one-shot and repeated. Information value: high, because it removes the grid-corner objection (caveat 2), tells the harness how often to refit, and says whether the edge is a refit-count effect or a label-distribution effect. Pass: ratio >= 1.03 with cluster CI above 1 in all four cells under the enlarged fixed family. Kill: at most 1.02 in two or more cells.
3. L3b. Label-source and exploration ablation (CPU about 15 min): greedy_n25_R25 against variants that put a fraction (10%, 25%) of each block on random unseen groups (charged, counted as hits), that stop refitting after k = 2, 5, 10 refits, and that refit on the earliest labels only. Information value: medium to high, it separates "on-policy labels near the decision boundary" from "having a recent fit", and decides whether a Phase 1 allocator needs an explicit exploration term to survive non-stationarity. L2c (GPU, fresh population) is no longer gated by an L2a kill, so it needs only Rustom's explicit go-ahead; I would ask for it only after L2b, because L2b is free.

**Critic note (L2a, adversarial review 2026-10-18).** The PASS verdict under the pre-registered rule and family stands, and so does every number in the table. What I re-derived from the raw files (sim_*_split*.json, summary.log, result.json, gate/, the scripts and runs/E04/feats_*.parquet; my scripts are exp/l2a_critic1.py to exp/l2a_critic5.py): all 12 held-out cells (uniform, thr_agreement, selected fixed, static, oracle, greedy_online, selected learned), the selected members, the paired and split-cluster CIs (my bootstrap seed differs and agrees to within 0.001), the SD of split ratios, the leave-one-split-out range (1.048 to 1.161), the held-out-max sensitivity (1.053, 1.060, 1.105, 1.047), the full 2x2 table and the refit dose-response ratios. Regression gate: I compared the main-run sim_*_split{0..3}.json directly with runs/L1b for every reference policy (446 comparisons including the gate files against main-run split 0), 0 differences. My own label-by-label re-implementation of the fixed families (recal / seeded / split charged at 48 policy cells, seeds 0 to 7) and of greedy_n25_R25 (all 8 label-set x split jobs, 25% budget) reproduces the saved per-seed hits exactly. No leakage: the ten allocator features are agreement, n_distinct, mean_tokens, std_tokens, any_truncated, log_qlen and the four judge columns; gold, pass_rate, majority_correct, proxy_error and judge_sel are not in the list, and the largest single-feature AUC against judge_error is 0.81 (n_distinct, 3B), so there is no leak signature. Budget accounting is equal (n calibration labels charged and counted as hits, every arm spends int(frac * n_sim) at K = 1; no policy exceeds its budget in hits on any split, maximum hits / budget 1.000 for the oracle). All 8 sim logs, the 2 gate logs and summary.log contain no warning, traceback, convergence message or nan, run.log is empty, exit_code 0, every per_seed array has 8 non-negative integers. The verdict follows from the thresholds as coded: ratios 1.050, 1.060, 1.139, 1.061, split-cluster lower bounds 1.043, 1.048, 1.089, 1.054, kill ratios met in 0 of 4 cells.

Corrections and qualifications (none changes the pre-registered verdict; items 1 to 3 change how robust it is):
1. The fixed family is under-searched at 3B 25% and the held-out-max sensitivity does not bound that. The 25% fixed grid is n in 25, 50, 100, 200 (400 is excluded by n <= budget // 2 = 272 to 289), so n = 250, which is inside the cap, was never tried. Adding n in 75, 150, 250, 300, 350, 400, 450, 500 (and for 50%: 550, 600, 700, 800, 900; my re-implementation reproduces the saved hits for existing n exactly) and keeping the pre-registered protocol (choose on seeds 0 to 3, report seeds 4 to 7), seeded_charged_250 is selected at 3B 25% and catches 443.8 on the held-out seeds against thr_agreement 434.0 (the plateau seeded_charged_250 to 400 is 442.4 to 443.8 on the held-out seeds, and the selection seeds agree: 441.2 at n = 300, 442.3 at n = 350). The 3B 25% ratio falls from 1.050 to 1.027 (split-cluster CI 1.017 to 1.043; per-split 1.051, 1.025, 1.014, 1.020), which is in the inconclusive band (1.02 to 1.03); with n allowed above the cap it is 1.030 (seeded_charged_400, CI 1.014 to 1.045). The reference greedy_online falls to 1.016 (CI 1.005 to 1.024) against that rule, so the sentence "the unchanged L1b greedy_online alone also meets the pass rule in all four cells" holds only against the pre-registered grid. The other three cells are unchanged or higher under the finer fixed grid (3B 50% 1.068 with split_charged_150, 7B 25% 1.139, 7B 50% 1.061; held-out max of the finer family gives 3B 50% 1.060, 7B 25% 1.105, 7B 50% 1.037 with CI 1.023 to 1.047).
2. A symmetric refinement restores the pass with a larger margin, so the PASS reading survives. The learned side also sits at the grid corner (the entry says so in caveat 2) and moving it confirms the ratio rises. I added random-start learners with (n0, R) = (10, 10), (15, 15), (20, 20), (25, 10), (25, 5), (10, 5) (greedy_n25_R25 reproduced exactly on all 8 jobs first) and reran the same protocol with both families enlarged: 3B 25% greedy_n10_R5 476.8 against seeded_charged_250 443.8 = 1.074 (cluster CI 1.058 to 1.094); 3B 50% greedy_n10_R5 870.1 against split_charged_150 799.4 = 1.089 (1.071 to 1.116); 7B 25% greedy_n10_R10 468.6 against split_charged_100 404.2 = 1.159 (1.114 to 1.232); 7B 50% unchanged at 1.061 (1.054 to 1.070; greedy_seeded_n25_R25 is still selected). Learner-only refinement (original fixed family) gives 1.099, 1.080, 1.159, 1.061. So the honest statement is: PASS is robust to a symmetric grid refinement (minimum 1.061) and fragile to refining only the fixed side at 3B 25% (1.027). The 3B 25% margin over the 1.03 bar (0.02) is the same size as the fixed-grid sensitivity (0.023), so do not read 1.050 as a tight estimate for that cell.
3. Caveat 2 mislocates the truncation. "seeded_charged is still rising at n = 400 on 3B 50%" is true only on the 5-point grid: the finer grid gives 797.3 at n = 400 and 450, 793.4 at 500, 793.6 at 550, 786.5 at 700, so the 50% fixed family is not understated (best held-out member stays split_charged_200 805.4). The under-search that matters is 3B 25% (item 1). Likewise "the best held-out member of any one-shot family is at most 1.031 times the selected fixed rule ... at most 1.004 elsewhere" is grid-specific: on the finer grid it is 1.023 at 3B 25% (seeded_charged_250 over thr_agreement, against the 1.000 quoted) and 1.023 at 7B 50% (split_charged_350, 781.8 over 764.1, against the 1.013 quoted).
4. The harness implication "a refit about every 25 labels" is a grid-corner artifact, not an optimum. In my extension, refitting every 5 labels from a 10-label start beats n0 = R = 25 on 3B 25% (476.8 against 455.9), 3B 50% (870.1 against 854.1) and 7B 25% (472.5 against 460.6), with no gain on 7B 50% (807.4 and 812.7 against 810.6, within noise). The dose-response is therefore not bracketed; L3a (and the smaller n0 = R = 10 already planned for L2b) should map it before any cadence is written into a Phase 1 design.
5. Timing line is wrong: "about 4 min wall for the full run" is 43 seconds (cmd.sh 20:07:28, done.flag 20:08:11; the gate files were written at 20:06:18 to 20:06:23). Harmless to the result.
6. Inference scope. The split-cluster CI has 4 overlapping clusters (75 of 100 embedding cells per split), so its lower bound exceeds 1 essentially whenever all four split ratios exceed 1; it adds little beyond the split ratios, and the pre-registered criterion is met on that basis. Policy seeds (8 per split) are adequate for seed noise (paired 16-run CI half-widths of 0.01 to 0.04 on the ratio), but the population is one set of 3,000 prompts, so none of the CIs speaks to a fresh population, and the 3B 25% cell is the one most exposed to it. The 2x2 still confounds number of fits with the on-policy label distribution (caveat 6), so "repeated refitting is the active ingredient" is the pre-registered reading of the pass, not a separated mechanism.

Corrected verdict: PASS under the pre-registered rule and family (regression gate passed; 4 of 4 cells at ratio >= 1.03 with split-cluster lower bound above 1; 0 of 4 kill cells). Reading, with the qualification: the edge survives the stronger charged fixed baselines of the L2a family and survives a symmetric refinement of both grids (1.061 to 1.159), but at 3B 25% it does not clear 1.03 if only the fixed n grid is refined (1.027), so claim 2's first support is real but thin there and its scope stays as written (weak judge labels, 25% and 50% budget, one population, K = 1). L2b is still the next step; add the finer fixed n (250, 300 and the other values inside budget // 2) to its fixed family, keep n0 = R = 10 and also R = 5 in its learned grid, and apply the pass rule to both-sides-refined families chosen on held-out-disjoint seeds. L2c would still need Rustom's explicit override of the E04 stop rule.

## L2b 2026-10-18 inconclusive
Hypothesis: the L2a pass (repeated-refit scorer over the best charged fixed rule, 1.050 / 1.060 / 1.139 / 1.061 at 3B 25%, 3B 50%, 7B-AWQ 25%, 7B-AWQ 50%) depends on a pool of about 2,200 groups in which 25 random start labels are a small share of the budget. At batch scale (m = 250 to 1,000 prompts, budget 25 to 500 verifications) the calibration cost is a much larger share and the edge shrinks to 1.00 or below, so a per-step allocator cannot reach it. Kill reading: best learned variant / best charged fixed member at most 1.00 at m = 500 and m = 250 in all four (judge, budget) cells. Falsifier: ratio at least 1.03 with the subset-cluster CI above 1 at m = 500 in all four cells.

Run (CPU only, no GPU, no generation; about 2 min of compute, cmd.sh 20:27:26 to done.flag 20:29:21, exit_code 0): E04 feature tables (3,000 GSM8K prompts x 8 Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct rollouts, ten cheap + judge features), label judge_error from the Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ answer-only judges (E01 has no headroom). exp/l2b_sim_alloc.py is exp/l2a_sim_alloc.py with run() and run_online() unchanged (AST diff: identical) and a new population step: m prompts drawn without replacement with default_rng(subset_seed), units = prompts, K = 1. Design: m in 250, 500, 1000 x subset seeds 0 to 15 x 8 policy seeds x budgets 10 / 25 / 50% of m (10% reported, not in the verdict), 96 jobs. Policies fixed in advance: thr_agreement and recal / seeded / split charged_n for n in 10, 15, 25, 50, 75, 100, 150, 200, 250 with n at most budget // 2 (fixed family); greedy_n<n0>_R<R> for (n0, R) in (10,5), (10,10), (15,15), (25,10), (25,25), greedy_online (L1b setting), greedy_seeded_n25_R25 (learned family); uniform, oracle and static_out (logistic fit on the 3,000 - m prompts outside the subset with free labels, ceiling only). ts_online dropped (below greedy_online in every cell of L1b and L2a). Selection: best fixed member and best learned variant chosen on subsets 0 to 7 (mean over subsets and seeds), reported on subsets 8 to 15 (fold A, the verdict fold); fold B swaps the roles; pooled = the 16 held-out values. CI: percentile bootstrap over the 8 held-out subsets, 10,000 resamples. Files: runs/L2b/result.json, summary.log, gate/gate_check.json, 96 sim_<judge>_m<m>_s<subset>.json. Caveat: subsets are draws from the same 3,000 prompts (pairwise overlap about 8%, 17%, 33% at m = 250, 500, 1000), so this is a scale and subset-variance test on one population, not a fresh population.

Audit (I re-derived the numbers from the 96 raw sim files with an independent script, exp/l2b_verify.py, own bootstrap seed, instead of trusting the run report). Regression gate: gate/gate_check.json shows 116 checks, 0 differences (per-seed hits of uniform, static, oracle, thr_agreement, greedy_online, greedy_n25_R25, greedy_seeded_n25_R25 and recal / seeded / split charged_n for n in 25, 50, 100, 200, m = 0, split 0, both judges, against runs/L2a); the gate script is read-and-compare on per_seed lists and the launcher voids the sweep on failure, so the gate is real. All 18 (judge, m, budget) cells reproduce: selected members, ratios and CIs (within 0.003 of summary.log), swapped-fold and pooled ratios, and the verdict counts. Slips in the run report, none changing the verdict: the 190.2 it quotes for 7B-AWQ m = 500 50% static_out is the 3B value (7B is 189.9), and "15 wall minutes" is script-writing time.
