| 7B-AWQ | 250 | 25% (B=62) | 27.1 | 45.9 | thr_agreement 45.9 | greedy_n10_R5 41.5 | 34.4 | 52.0 | 62.0 | 0.905 (0.882 to 0.931) | 0.926 | 0.916 |
| 7B-AWQ | 250 | 50% (B=125) | 54.0 | 79.9 | thr_agreement 79.9 | greedy_n10_R5 81.3 | 76.7 | 92.2 | 107.9 | 1.018 (0.993 to 1.044) | 1.031 | 1.024 |
| 7B-AWQ | 500 | 10% (B=50) | 22.3 | 39.9 | thr_agreement 39.9 | greedy_n10_R5 34.7 | 25.6 | 42.1 | 50.0 | 0.870 (0.832 to 0.910) | 0.856 | 0.863 |
| 7B-AWQ | 500 | 25% (B=125) | 56.0 | 93.4 | thr_agreement 93.4 | greedy_n10_R5 92.3 | 84.4 | 106.0 | 125.0 | 0.988 (0.968 to 1.010) | 0.966 | 0.977 |
| 7B-AWQ | 500 | 50% (B=250) | 112.2 | 162.3 | split_charged_100 162.5 | greedy_n10_R5 177.3 | 170.6 | 189.9 | 224.4 | 1.091 (1.078 to 1.106) | 1.113 | 1.102 |
| 7B-AWQ | 1000 | 10% (B=100) | 44.1 | 80.4 | thr_agreement 80.4 | greedy_n10_R5 75.4 | 67.1 | 86.1 | 100.0 | 0.938 (0.912 to 0.964) | 0.885 | 0.912 |
| 7B-AWQ | 1000 | 25% (B=250) | 110.5 | 186.1 | thr_agreement 186.1 | greedy_n10_R5 200.0 | 190.1 | 212.4 | 250.0 | 1.075 (1.050 to 1.098) | 1.075 | 1.075 |
| 7B-AWQ | 1000 | 50% (B=500) | 219.7 | 318.1 | split_charged_150 334.6 | greedy_n10_R10 358.9 | 351.3 | 370.8 | 440.8 | 1.073 (1.061 to 1.085) | 1.085 | 1.079 |

Ratio of selected learned to selected fixed against absolute budget B (fold A; cells with equal B but different m in the same row; L2a full-pool values for reference). Post hoc, from the table above:

| B | 3B | 7B-AWQ | cells |
|---|---|---|---|
| 25 | 0.705 | 0.782 | m=250 10% |
| 50 | 0.873 | 0.870 | m=500 10% |
| 62 | 0.880 | 0.905 | m=250 25% |
| 100 | 0.940 | 0.938 | m=1000 10% |
| 125 | 0.989, 0.978 | 1.018, 0.988 | m=250 50%, m=500 25% |
| 250 | 1.039, 1.025 | 1.091, 1.075 | m=500 50%, m=1000 25% |
| 500 | 1.076 | 1.073 | m=1000 50% |
| about 550 and 1,100 (L2a) | 1.050, 1.060 | 1.139, 1.061 | full pool, 25% and 50% |

Findings.
1. The edge is a function of the absolute number of verifications B, not of m or the budget fraction. The two pairs of cells with equal B and different m agree to within 0.03 (B = 125: 0.989 and 0.978 on 3B, 1.018 and 0.988 on 7B-AWQ; B = 250: 1.039 and 1.025, 1.091 and 1.075). The curve crosses 1.00 near B = 125 to 150 and reaches 1.03 near B = 250. At B of 62 or less the learner is 9% to 30% below the agreement threshold, and at B = 100 about 6% below. This is post hoc: 18 cells from one population, m and fraction confounded outside the two matched pairs. It is a hypothesis for L4a and L4b, not a result.
2. The learned optimum is the grid corner, again. n10_R5 (smallest n0 and R) is the best learned variant on the all-subset mean in 16 of 18 cells (exceptions: 3B m = 250 25%, greedy_seeded_n25_R25 by 0.2 hits; 7B-AWQ m = 1000 50%, n10_R10 by 0.8 hits) and the ordering n10_R5 > n10_R10 > n15_R15 > n25_R25 is nearly monotone. At m = 500 25% the step from (25,25) to (10,5) gained 4.7 hits on both judges (3B 90.4 to 95.1, 7B-AWQ 87.9 to 92.6) while the remaining gap to the pooled fixed rule is 2.2 and 2.1 hits (97.3 and 94.7), so the two 25% kill cells at m = 500 (0.978, 0.988) are not robust to extending the grid below n0 = 10, R = 5. The kill is therefore weaker than 5 of 8 suggests, and the pass failures are not a clean refutation either.
3. greedy_online at its L1b setting (R = max(budget // 8, 40)) no longer passes at batch scale: ratio to selected fixed at m = 500 is 0.898 (3B 25%), 1.007 (3B 50%), 0.904 (7B-AWQ 25%), 1.050 (7B-AWQ 50%). The edge is carried by the small-n0, frequent-refit variants.
4. Fixed side. thr_agreement is the selected fixed rule on subsets 0 to 7 in 15 of 18 cells; a charged rule is selected only at 50% budget (7B-AWQ m = 500 split_charged_100, 7B-AWQ m = 1000 split_charged_150, 3B m = 1000 seeded_charged_250). The cap n at most budget // 2 truncates the grid: at m = 500 50% the largest allowed n is 100, which is the 7B-AWQ pick, and that curve is still creeping up (split_charged_50, 75, 100: 159.9, 160.4, 162.7 on the all-subset mean against thr_agreement 162.0). The 1.091 there is partly a truncated-family number; a fixed rule worth 3 more hits would still leave about 1.07.
5. static_out (free labels) is above both arms in every cell: 1% to 7% headroom over the learner at B >= 250 (3B m = 500 50%: 190.2 against 179.6; 3B m = 1000 50%: 374.9 against 369.6), so the repeated refit does not reach the label-free ceiling.
6. Inference limits. Subsets overlap, so the 8 held-out subsets are not independent (most at m = 1000, 33% overlap) and the CIs are optimistic there; policy seeds are the same across subsets; every CI here speaks to subset variance of one 3,000-prompt population.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: INCONCLUSIVE. The regression gate passed (a failed gate would have voided the run). Pass needs ratio at least 1.03 with the subset-cluster CI lower bound above 1 at m = 500 in all four cells; 2 of 4 hold (3B 50%: 1.039, CI 1.019 to 1.062; 7B-AWQ 50%: 1.091, CI 1.078 to 1.106) and both 25% cells fail (3B: 0.978, CI 0.956 to 1.002; 7B-AWQ: 0.988, CI 0.968 to 1.010). Kill needs ratio at most 1.00 at m = 250 and m = 500 in all four cells (8 cells); 5 of 8 hold (the three exceptions are 3B m = 500 50% 1.039, 7B-AWQ m = 250 50% 1.018 with CI 0.993 to 1.043, and 7B-AWQ m = 500 50% 1.091). The swapped fold (0.966, 1.041, 0.977, 1.113 at m = 500), the pooled 16 subsets (0.978, 1.040, 0.977, 1.102) and the held-out-max sensitivity give the same pass and kill patterns, so the inconclusive outcome does not depend on the fold. Neither the m = 500 falsifier nor the kill reading is established.

What it changes. Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget) keeps its L2a support but is now conditional: in the offline replay it holds when the allocator has about 250 or more verified labels in hand (all 6 L2b cells at B >= 250 and all 4 L2a cells have ratio above 1.00, and 9 of those 10 are at or above 1.03; the exception is 3B m = 1000 25% at 1.025, with swapped and pooled 1.044 and 1.034) and fails below about 125, where thr_agreement beats a per-step restart learner (ratio 0.70 to 0.99 in 11 of the 12 cells at B <= 125; the exception is 7B-AWQ m = 250 50% at 1.018 with a CI that includes 1). The thesis claim as written ("any fixed rule at the same budget") is therefore not shown for a per-step allocator at small batches, and the Phase 1 design has to persist labels across steps (or pool verification budget over steps) for claim 2 to be testable there; a fresh allocator per step at B under 100 is expected to lose to the threshold arm in the Phase 1 kill test. The Phase 1 refit cadence read off the grid is "as frequent as the grid allows" (every 5 labels from a 10-label start was best in 16 of 18 cells), unbracketed. Claim 3 is untouched: L2b used no embedding or prompt-space features, and the edge it measures is a repeated-refit effect on cheap + judge features (E01 and E04 kills stand; ts_online was dropped because it lost in L1b and L2a). Claim 1 is untouched (needs the Phase 1 harness). The fixed agreement threshold stays the default deliverable at small B.

Follow-ups, ranked by expected information value (full specs in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under 'Proposed by the loop', Iteration 4).
1. L4a (CPU, about 15 to 20 min): bracket the learner's small end (n0 in 3, 5, 10; R in 1, 2, 5) and add a threshold-offset warm-start learner (score = agreement offset plus a ridge-penalized correction on the other nine features, so it equals thr_agreement at zero labels). Highest value: it decides whether the small-B loss is a fixable cold-start cost or a hard floor near B = 125 to 150 for this feature set, and it removes the grid-corner objection that has now been raised twice.
2. L4b (CPU, about 10 min): cross-step carry-over replay. 12 disjoint batches of 250 prompts, allocator keeps its labels across batches (and a sliding-window variant, W = 125, 250, 500, as a drift proxy), against thr_agreement and a calibrate-once fixed rule. Tests whether label history, not batch size, is the driver of finding 1. Medium to high value: the unbounded-carry arm is expected to recover the B >= 250 ratios (stationary by construction), so the information is the window curve (how stale the labels may be) and the batches-to-break-even count; it feeds E13.
3. L4c (GPU, two jobs, about 35 to 45 min; needs Rustom's explicit override of the E04 stop rule): L2c redesigned for B >= 250 (3 disjoint blocks of 1,000 fresh GSM8K prompts, n10_R5 and n10_R10 pre-specified). Medium value, the only item that addresses the one-population caveat; ask after L4a so the arms tested are final.

**Critic note (L2b, adversarial review 2026-10-18).** The INCONCLUSIVE verdict stands and every number in the per-cell table reproduces; the corrections below are to wording, one omitted report item and three over-strong readings, and none moves a verdict count. Checked: I re-derived all 18 table rows (uniform, thr_agreement, selected fixed and learned names and held-out means, greedy_online, static_out, oracle, fold A ratio and CI, swapped fold, pooled 16) from the 96 raw sim_*.json with a separate script (exp/l2b_critic.py, bootstrap seed 777, 20,000 resamples): 0 mismatches, CI ends within 0.004. gate/gate_check.json shows 116 checks and 0 differences, exit_code is 0, all 96 sim logs have the 4 expected lines with no warning or traceback, n_sim equals m in every file, no policy catches more than its budget or the subset's error count, and the ASTs of run(), run_online(), rule() and charged_order() are identical to exp/l2a_sim_alloc.py. No leakage: the ten features are label-free (agreement, n_distinct, token and question-length features from the rollouts; judge_* from a judge that sees only the question and the proposed final number), gold reaches the run only through the judge_error label and the verification oracle, the best single-feature AUC against the label is 0.81, and static_out (fit on the 3,000 - m outside prompts with free labels) is never a member of either selected family. Budget accounting is equal across policies (budget = int(frac * m); charged calibration labels count as spent and as hits; greedy variants count every collected label). Verdict re-derivation: pass cells 2 of 4 (3B 50% 1.039, lower bound 1.018; 7B-AWQ 50% 1.091, lower bound 1.078; the two 25% cells fail on the ratio, 0.978 and 0.988, not on the CI) and kill cells 5 of 8, identical under the swapped fold, the pooled-16 CI and the held-out-max sensitivity, and also under the backlog wording of line 156 (greedy_online / best fixed at m = 500 is 0.898, 1.007, 0.904, 1.050, so neither the pass nor the kill is met there either). Per-subset fold-A ratios exceed 1 in 1 of 8 (3B) and 3 of 8 (7B-AWQ) subsets at the m = 500 25% cells and in 7 of 8 and 8 of 8 at 50%, so the split is a stable structural pattern and not seed noise; the 8-cluster percentile bootstrap is somewhat narrow, but a t interval on the 3B 50% cell (1.039, SD 0.034, 8 subsets) is still about 1.011 to 1.067, so no cell flips.

Corrections, none changing the verdict:
1. Omitted report item. For an INCONCLUSIVE outcome the pre-registered report is the full per-cell table and the ratio-vs-m curve; the entry gave a ratio-vs-B table instead. The curve (selected learned / selected fixed, fold A held-out, pooled 16-subset cross-fit in brackets, L2a full pool last, from runs/L2b/summary.log):

| label | budget | m = 250 | m = 500 | m = 1000 | L2a full pool (n_sim about 2,250) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3B | 10% | 0.705 [0.719] | 0.873 [0.873] | 0.940 [0.940] | 0.962 |
| 3B | 25% | 0.880 [0.885] | 0.978 [0.978] | 1.025 [1.034] | 1.050 |
| 3B | 50% | 0.989 [0.989] | 1.039 [1.040] | 1.076 [1.078] | 1.060 |
| 7B-AWQ | 10% | 0.782 [0.771] | 0.870 [0.863] | 0.938 [0.912] | 0.969 |
| 7B-AWQ | 25% | 0.905 [0.916] | 0.988 [0.977] | 1.075 [1.075] | 1.139 |
| 7B-AWQ | 50% | 1.018 [1.024] | 1.091 [1.102] | 1.073 [1.079] | 1.061 |

The ratio rises with m in every row up to m = 1000 except 7B-AWQ 50% (1.091 at m = 500, 1.073 at m = 1000, 1.061 on the full pool), so the m = 500 pass is split by budget fraction (50% passes, 25% does not) rather than being a single threshold in m.
2. Transposed list. The verdict paragraph gives the swapped fold at m = 500 as "0.966, 1.041, 0.977, 1.113"; in the cell order used for the pooled list (3B 25%, 3B 50%, 7B-AWQ 25%, 7B-AWQ 50%) the values are 0.977, 1.041, 0.966, 1.113. The numbers are right, the 25% positions were swapped.
3. ts_online provenance. "ts_online dropped (below greedy_online in every cell of L1b and L2a)" and "dropped because it lost in L1b and L2a" are wrong for L2a: exp/l2a_sim_alloc.py dropped ts_online, so L2a has no ts_online cell. The evidence is L1b only (ts_online below greedy_online in all 36 L1b sim cells, and 0.695 to 0.968 on the 9 primary cells in the L1b entry). The claim 3 reading is unaffected.
4. Finding 1 is over-stated. "The two pairs of cells with equal B and different m agree to within 0.03" holds only in the verdict fold for 7B-AWQ. 3B pairs agree within 0.014 in fold A, swapped and pooled (B = 125: 0.011, 0.013, 0.011; B = 250: 0.014, 0.003, 0.006). For 7B-AWQ at B = 125 the m = 250 50% cell is above the m = 500 25% cell by 0.030 (fold A), 0.065 (swapped) and 0.047 (pooled), and at B = 250 the m = 500 50% cell is above the m = 1000 25% cell by 0.016, 0.038 and 0.027. So "a function of B, not of m or the budget fraction" is supported for 3B and not excluded-against for 7B-AWQ, where a smaller m at a larger fraction looks better at the same B. It stays a hypothesis for L4a and L4b, not a finding.
5. Finding 2's last sentence ("the two 25% kill cells at m = 500 are not robust to extending the grid below n0 = 10, R = 5") is an extrapolation, not a measurement. Measured: n10_R5 is the best learned variant in 16 of 18 cells, and at m = 500 25% the step from (25,25) to (10,5) gained 4.7 hits against a remaining 2.2 and 2.1 hit gap to the pooled fixed rule. Whether a smaller (n0, R) gains the missing 2 hits, or loses as the n0 = 10 label start becomes too small to fit ten features, is untested; read the 25% m = 500 kill cells as "not established and not refuted" until L4a.
6. "Held-out" is held-out by subset draw, not by prompt. A prompt in a subset from 8 to 15 also appears in at least one of subsets 0 to 7 with probability 1 - (1 - m/3000)^8, which is 50%, 77% and 96% at m = 250, 500 and 1000 (measured mean pairwise subset overlap 8.6%, 16.6%, 33.3%). Selection only chooses among 7 learned and up to 28 fixed members (thr_agreement in 15 of 18 cells, the grid corner in 16 of 18), so the optimism is small and, if anything, favors the larger fixed family; the swapped fold, pooled and held-out-max checks show the same pass and kill pattern. Every CI here is subset variance of one 3,000-prompt population, as caveat 6 says.
7. Minor, no effect: the backlog line for L2b words the pass CI as "over the 16 subsets"; the run used the 8 held-out subsets of the verdict fold with pooled 16 as the cross-check, and the pooled-16 rule also gives 2 of 4. The 3B m = 500 25% upper CI end is 1.002 in summary.log and 1.003 in the table, and 7B-AWQ m = 250 50% is 1.043 against 1.044, both bootstrap-seed noise.

Corrected verdict: INCONCLUSIVE (regression gate passed; pass cells 2 of 4, kill cells 5 of 8; the m = 500 pass fails at 25% budget on both judges and the kill fails at 50% budget). Reading: the learner's edge over the best charged fixed rule is real at 50% budget and at B of about 250 or more on both judges, absent at B of about 125 or less, and unresolved between; the B-only explanation is supported for 3B and open for 7B-AWQ; L2c is not triggered by this result and the Phase 1 cadence reading ("as frequent as the grid allows") remains an unbracketed grid-corner result.

## L4a 2026-10-18 inconclusive
Hypothesis: the L2b loss of the per-step learned allocator at small absolute budget (selected learned / selected fixed 0.70 to 0.99 in 11 of 12 cells with B <= 125, break-even near B = 125 to 150) is a cold-start cost, not a hard floor. The plain learner's optimum was the smallest grid corner (n0 = 10, R = 5 best in 16 of 18 cells), so it was unbracketed. A warm-start learner whose score is a fixed decreasing function of agreement plus a ridge-penalized correction on the other nine features equals thr_agreement at zero labels, so it should not lose at small B and should add information only as labels arrive; a plain learner at (n0, R) below (10, 5) may also cross 1.00 at B = 125. Pass: the best learned family (plain or offset) has ratio >= 1.02 with the bootstrap CI lower bound above 1 in at least 3 of the 4 B = 125 cells, and ratio >= 0.97 in all 12 cells with B <= 125. Kill: no variant reaches 1.00 in any B = 125 cell and the best offset arm is below 0.97 in at least 3 of the 12 cells with B <= 125. Valid only if the regression gate shows 0 differences.

Run (CPU only, no GPU, no generation; launched through bin/gpu_run.sh, cmd.sh 20:47 to done.flag 20:55, about 8 min, exit_code 0): E04 feature tables (3,000 GSM8K prompts x 8 Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct rollouts, ten cheap + judge features), label judge_error from the Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ answer-only judges. exp/l4a_sim_alloc.py is exp/l2b_sim_alloc.py with run() and run_online() kept verbatim, plus two additions. (1) Plain learned grid = the L2b grid (10,5), (10,10), (15,15), (25,10), (25,25) plus (3,1), (5,1), (5,2), (5,5), (10,1), (10,2), n0 <= budget // 2, plus greedy_online and greedy_seeded_n25_R25. (2) run_offset(budget, seed, n0, R, lam): ten features standardized on the m sim prompts (unlabeled), score = -2 x standardized agreement + w.x on the nine other standardized features, w = 0 at the start so the first labels are the lowest-agreement groups with random ties (identical to the thr_agreement prefix), then a ridge-logistic IRLS refit of w (offset = -2 x z_agreement, intercept unpenalized, no class weights) every R labels, skipped while the labels are one class. 12 offset arms fixed in advance: lam in 1, 10, 100; R in 5, 10; n0 in 0, 5 (none chosen on held-out subsets). Design as L2b: m in 250, 500, 1000 x subset seeds 0 to 15 x policy seeds 0 to 7 x budgets 10 / 25 / 50% of m, 96 jobs; fixed family = thr_agreement plus recal / seeded / split charged_n for n in 10, 15, 25, 50, 75, 100, 150, 200, 250 with n <= budget // 2; selection of the best fixed member, best plain learner, best offset learner and best of the pooled learned family on subsets 0 to 7, reported on subsets 8 to 15 (fold A, the verdict fold), swapped fold and pooled 16 as cross-checks, percentile bootstrap over the 8 held-out subsets, 10,000 resamples. Sensitivity only (not in the verdict): recal / seeded / split charged at n = budget // 2 added to the fixed family. Files: runs/L4a/result.json, summary.log, ratio_vs_B.png, gate/gate_check.json, sanity/, 96 sim_<judge>_m<m>_s<subset>.json, ledger_table.md. Caveat as in L2b: subsets are draws from the same 3,000 prompts (pairwise overlap about 8%, 17%, 33% at m = 250, 500, 1000), so this is a scale and subset-variance test on one population, not a fresh population.

Audit (I re-derived the numbers from the 96 raw sim files with an independent script, exp/l4a_verify.py, own bootstrap seed 424242, instead of trusting the run report; no adversarial Critic pass has been run on this entry yet). Regression gate: gate/gate_check.json shows 124 required checks (m = 500, subset 0, both judges, uniform, oracle, thr_agreement, greedy_online, greedy_n10_R5, greedy_n25_R25 and every shared recal / seeded / split charged_n against per-seed hits in runs/L2b) and 30 extra checks, 0 differences in both; the launcher exits 3 and skips the sweep on a failed gate, so the gate is real. lam = infinity sanity: 4 files x 96 per-seed comparisons (m = 250 and 500, subset 0, both judges, n0 in 0, 5, R in 5, 10, all three budgets) with max abs difference in hits 0. I also checked ridge_logit directly on random data: the gradient at the returned solution is below 3e-9 for lam = 1, 10, 100 and lam = infinity returns w = 0. No Traceback in any of the 96 sim logs, 4 sanity logs or the gate logs. All 18 cells reproduce: selected fixed, plain, offset and pooled arm names identical to summary.log, ratios identical to 3 decimals, CI ends within 0.002, swapped and pooled-16 ratios identical, and the verdict counts (pass cells 1 of 4, floor cells 12 of 12, B = 125 cells with a variant at or above 1.00 4 of 4, offset below 0.97 in 0 of 12) identical under fold A; swapped fold gives 0 of 4 pass cells and pooled 16 gives 1 of 4. Notes on the run report, none changing the verdict: its "best L2b-grid arm on all 16 is n10_R5 in 17 of 18 cells" counts random-start L2b grid arms only (16 of 18 when greedy_online and greedy_seeded_n25_R25 may win, as in the L2b entry); its "extended fixed family leaves the ratio unchanged in 15 of 18 cells" is conservative, the pooled ratio is identical to 3 decimals in 17 of 18 cells (only 3B m = 500 50% moves, 1.052 to 1.062, seeded_charged_125 becomes the reference) and no B <= 125 cell moves; and its "smallest B with pooled ratio >= 1.00" (3B 100, 7B-AWQ 25) is not informative, because at B <= 100 the selected pooled arm is an offset arm that mirrors thr_agreement and every ratio there sits in 0.984 to 1.009 with CIs that contain 1.

Free-label gap captured (post hoc, from the held-out means above: (selected pooled - thr_agreement) / (static_out - thr_agreement); static_out is a logistic rule fit on the 2,000 to 2,750 prompts outside the subset with free labels, so it is an optimistic ceiling, and static_out is 5.5% to 17.0% above thr_agreement in every one of the 18 cells):

| B | 3B | 7B-AWQ |

| 25 | -11% (-0.2 of 1.8 hits) | 0% (0.0 of 2.0) |

| 62 | -8% | 7% |
| 100 | 6% | 5% |
| 125 | 15% (m=250), -1% (m=500) | 18% (m=250), 2% (m=500) |
| 250 | 51% (m=500), 39% (m=1000) | 54% (m=500), 63% (m=1000) |
| 500 | 97% | 80% |

Findings.
1. The warm start removes the small-B loss and adds nothing at B <= 100. The 12 cells with B <= 125 go from 0.705 to 0.989 in 11 of 12 (L2b) to 0.984 to 1.028 in all 12 (L4a pooled / fixed). The mechanism is collapse onto the threshold, not a gain: at the 8 cells with B <= 100 the selected pooled arm is an offset arm in every cell, the pooled ratio is 0.984 to 1.009, and offset / thr_agreement is 0.984 to 1.009 with every fold-A CI containing 1 (the nearest thing to an edge is 7B-AWQ m = 250 25%, B = 62: fold A 1.009 with CI 0.995 to 1.023, swapped 1.015, pooled 16 1.012 with CI 1.003 to 1.021, about +0.4 hits). The extended plain learner still loses at B <= 100 (selected plain / fixed 0.830 to 0.979), so the cold-start cost of the plain learner is real and is the random first labels plus the noisy first fits; ranking the first labels by agreement removes it by making the learner equal thr_agreement. Net: the downside is fixable, the upside below B = 125 is zero within noise. The B <= 125 floor test (0.97) is therefore met by construction and says little about learning.
2. At B = 125 the edge is marginal and fragile. Pooled / fixed is 1.015 (3B m = 250, CI 0.998 to 1.032), 1.000 (3B m = 500, 0.993 to 1.007), 1.028 (7B-AWQ m = 250, 1.001 to 1.053) and 1.003 (7B-AWQ m = 500, 0.987 to 1.020). The pass test needs >= 1.02 with CI lower bound above 1 in 3 of 4 cells; 1 of 4 holds, and that cell's lower bound is 1.001. Swapped fold: 1.003, 1.004, 1.022 (CI includes 1), 0.992, so 0 of 4; pooled 16: 1.009, 1.002, 1.025 (1.005 to 1.046), 0.998, so 1 of 4. Even the post hoc best of 25 variants on the held-out subsets (1.015, 1.003, 1.034, 1.011) clears 1.02 in one cell, so no selection rule would pass. The L2b Critic's item 5 is answered: extending the grid below (10, 5) moves the two 25% m = 500 cells from 0.978 and 0.988 to 0.999 and 1.001 (plain, +2.1 and +1.2 hits on the held-out subsets against L2b gaps of 2.2 and 1.1 hits on the same subsets), parity and not a win. Break-even in the sense of a significant edge is bracketed between B = 125 and B = 250 on both judges (the single significant B = 125 cell is 7B-AWQ m = 250 50%); what moved is the downside, not the break-even.
3. The plain optimum is still at the grid corner, but the corner is now the design boundary. greedy_n3_R1 is the selected plain arm on subsets 0 to 7 in 15 of 18 cells (greedy_n5_R1 in 1, greedy_n10_R5 and greedy_n10_R10 in the two 7B-AWQ 50% cells at m = 500 and 1000, bracketed in 2 of 18) and the best random-start arm on all 16 subsets in 15 of 18; an R = 1 arm is best in 16 of 18. The dose-response is monotone and flattens at the bottom (3B m = 500 25%, mean over all 16 subsets: n10_R5 95.1, n10_R2 96.3, n10_R1 96.6, n5_R1 97.4, n3_R1 97.6; 3B m = 1000 25%: 199.1, 200.8, 201.7, 202.1, 202.3), so n5_R1 to n3_R1 gains 0.2 hits. n0 cannot go below 2 (the fit needs both classes) and R cannot go below 1, so the plain learner's reading is "fit as early and as often as the labels allow", and no further bracketing is possible for that family.
4. Offset and plain cross over near B = 125. At B <= 100 the offset family is at 0.984 to 1.009 and plain at 0.83 to 0.98; at B = 250 and 500 plain is ahead of offset in 5 of 6 cells (plain 1.039 to 1.091 against offset 1.020 to 1.080 in those five, with the largest gap at 7B-AWQ m = 1000 25%, 1.090 against 1.024; 7B-AWQ m = 1000 50% is the exception, offset 1.077 against plain 1.073). The selected pooled arm is plain in 7 of 18 cells (both m = 250 50% cells at B = 125 and every B >= 250 cell except 7B-AWQ m = 1000 50%) and offset in the other 11. The ridge strength shows the same trade: the selected lam is 10 or 100 in 7 of the 8 B <= 100 cells (lam = 1 only at 3B m = 1000 B = 100) and lam = 1 in all six B >= 250 cells (all 16-subset means, 7B-AWQ m = 1000 50%: lam = 1 359.8, lam = 10 331.5, lam = 100 318.3, thr_agreement 315.9), so lam = 1 is the edge of the offset grid and lam < 1 is untested. Why plain beats offset at B = 250 (7B-AWQ m = 1000 25%: 202.8 against 190.6) is not identified: the offset form fixes the agreement slope at -2 standardized units, uses no class weights and keeps a prior; the ablation is untested.
5. The labels are the bottleneck, not the features (post hoc). static_out beats thr_agreement by 5.5% to 17.0% in every cell, so the ten cheap and judge features carry usable information at every B, yet the selected learner captures -32% to 7% of that gap at B <= 100, -1% to 18% at B = 125, 39% to 63% at B = 250 and 80% to 97% at B = 500 (table above). A better small-sample learner cannot close this with under about 100 labels (the lam = 100 offset arms are the limit case and they equal thr_agreement); label persistence across steps is the lever, which is L4b's question. The caveat is that static_out is fit on free labels from the same population.
6. Fixed side and sensitivity. thr_agreement is the selected fixed rule on subsets 0 to 7 in 15 of 18 cells (a charged rule only at 50% budget with B >= 250: 3B m = 1000 seeded_charged_250, 7B-AWQ m = 500 split_charged_100, 7B-AWQ m = 1000 split_charged_150), as in L2b. Adding the n = budget // 2 charged rules (sensitivity only) changes the pooled ratio in 1 of 18 cells (3B m = 500 50%, 1.052 to 1.062) and in no B <= 125 cell, so the L2b cap truncation does not explain the small-B result.
7. Inference limits. Same as L2b: held-out is held-out by subset draw, not by prompt (a prompt in subsets 8 to 15 also appears in a selection subset with probability 50%, 77%, 96% at m = 250, 500, 1000); the 8-subset bootstrap is narrow; policy seeds are shared across subsets; one 3,000-prompt population; 18 cells and about 50 arms examined. The one pass cell (7B-AWQ m = 250 50%) has a CI lower bound of 1.001 and is the same cell that was 1.018 in L2b, so a 7B-specific effect at that m and fraction cannot be separated from noise on this population. The protocol-change item (out-of-fold cell-encoding delta AUC from the E04 Critic) is not applicable: L4a uses only the ten cheap and judge columns, no prompt-space features, so the item is neither triggered nor tested (also recorded in result.json notes).

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: INCONCLUSIVE. The regression gate passed with 0 differences (a failed gate would have voided the run). PASS needs both parts: (a) ratio >= 1.02 with the bootstrap CI lower bound above 1 in at least 3 of the 4 B = 125 cells: 1 of 4 holds (7B-AWQ m = 250 50%, 1.028, CI 1.001 to 1.053), so (a) fails (swapped fold 0 of 4, pooled 16 1 of 4); (b) ratio >= 0.97 in all 12 cells with B <= 125: holds, 12 of 12, minimum 0.984 (7B-AWQ m = 500 10%, CI 0.966 to 1.002). KILL needs both: (a) no learner variant reaches 1.00 in any of the four B = 125 cells: false, the best variant is 1.015, 1.003, 1.034 and 1.011 (all four at or above 1.00; the selected pooled arm is 1.000 at 3B m = 500); (b) the best offset arm below 0.97 in at least 3 of the 12 cells: 0 of 12. Neither rule fired, and the outcome does not depend on the fold (swapped 0 of 4 pass, 12 of 12 floor, 0 of 12 offset below 0.97; pooled 16 1 of 4 pass, 12 of 12 floor). Reading: half of the pass reading holds (the small-B loss is a fixable cold-start cost; the Phase 1 allocator gets the warm-start form) and half does not (break-even B did not move below about 125 in the sense of a significant edge; no learner beats thr_agreement at B <= 100).

What it changes. Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): its support stays conditional and is sharpened, not widened. In the offline replay the best learned family beats the best member of the fixed family in all 6 cells with B >= 250 (plain 1.039 to 1.091, every CI lower bound above 1; the offset family alone is 1.020 to 1.080 against the fixed rule, 1.0196 in the lowest cell, every CI lower bound above 1) and at B = 500 captures 80% to 97% of the free-label gap; at B <= 100 no learner, plain or warm-start, beats the free agreement threshold, and at B = 125 the edge is 0 to 2.8% with 1 of 4 cells significant. The claim as written ("any fixed rule at the same budget") is therefore not shown for a per-step allocator with fewer than about 125 labels in hand, and the Phase 1 design has to carry labels across steps (or pool verification budget over steps) for claim 2 to be testable there; at per-step B under 125 the Phase 1 kill test should be expected to return "thr_agreement". The Phase 1 allocator spec now reads, as a function of the per-step budget B: the warm-start offset learner with a strong ridge (lam 10 to 100) when B is under about 125, the refit-every-label plain learner (n0 = 3, R = 1) when B is about 250 or more, a weaker ridge (lam 1) as B grows, and B between 125 and 250 untested; all of this is one population, one task, offline replay on judge_error labels, and the B switch is a reading of two separate families, not a tested single arm (L5b). Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): untouched and still not supported; L4a used no embedding or prompt-space features, the edge it measures is a repeated-refit effect on cheap + judge features (E01 and E04 kills stand, delta AUC about 0 and neighbor ratio 1.00 on GSM8K). Claim 1 (same policy quality with fewer verifier calls): untouched, needs the Phase 1 harness. The agreement threshold stays the default deliverable at B under about 125.

Follow-ups, ranked by expected information value (full specs in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under 'Proposed by the loop', Iteration 5).
1. L5a (CPU, about 10 to 15 min; supersedes L4b): cross-step carry-over replay with the L4a arms added (carry_plain n3_R1, carry_offset lam 10 and 1, windowed W = 125, 250, 500, calibrate_once, restart offset per step, thr_agreement). Highest value: L4a shows the features hold 5.5% to 17% over thr_agreement but fewer than about 125 labels capture at most 18% of it, so whether persisted labels recover the B >= 250 capture (39% to 97%) at a per-step B of 62 or 125 decides the Phase 1 architecture and the window curve gives the tolerable label staleness (input to E13). It is expected to pass by stationarity, so the information is the window curve and the batches-to-break-even count; a kill (carry at or below thr_agreement) would mean the B curve was a subset-size artifact.
2. L5b (CPU, about 10 min): decompose why plain beats offset at B >= 250 and test a single B-agnostic arm. Ablate the plain learner (class weights, free agreement slope) and add prior-mean ridge arms (agreement slope free with a ridge toward -2, a lam schedule that decays with the label count, and an offset-to-plain handoff at 25 or 50 labels) on m = 500 and 1000. Medium value: B is known at allocation time, so a B switch is already available; the value is a single form that stays safe when labels in hand are not a clean function of B (persistent or windowed labels, L5a) and the identity of the ingredient to port into the Phase 1 harness, and it tests the lam < 1 edge of the offset grid.
3. L5c (GPU, two jobs, about 35 to 45 min; needs Rustom's explicit override of the E04 stop rule; replaces L4c's arm list): fresh-population replication with the arms L4a fixed (thr_agreement, greedy_n3_R1, offset_n0_R5_lam10, offset_n0_R5_lam1) at B = 125, 250 and 500 on three disjoint blocks of 1,000 fresh GSM8K prompts. Medium to high value for claim 2 generality, the only item that escapes the one-population caveat shared by L2a, L2b and L4a, but it spends GPU on GSM8K, so ask Rustom only after L5a fixes the architecture; L4c's "best offset arm if L4a passes" condition is not met (L4a is inconclusive) and its pre-specified n10_R5 arm is no longer the selected plain arm.

**Critic note (L4a, adversarial review 2026-10-18).** The INCONCLUSIVE verdict stands, and so does every verdict count; the corrections below are one mis-transcribed cell, one rounding artifact, two over-strong readings and one provenance line, and none moves a pass or kill count. This is the adversarial pass the Audit paragraph says had not been run. What I re-derived from the 96 raw sim_*.json with separate scripts (exp/l4a_critic.py, bootstrap seed 777, 20,000 resamples; exp/l4a_critic_offset.py): every file has m, subset_seed, n_sim = m, label judge_error, 8 policy seeds and a complete per-seed array for every policy; no policy catches more than its budget; oracle hits equal min(B, errors) in all 288 (cell, subset) combinations. I parsed the 36 rows of the two ledger tables and compared them programmatically (selected fixed, plain, offset and pooled names and held-out means, ratios, CIs, offset / thr_agreement, max variant): 1 mismatch (item 1), CI ends within 0.003 (bootstrap seed). Verdict counts reproduce: fold A pass cells 1 of 4, floor 12 of 12, selected offset below 0.97 in 0 of 12, best variant at or above 1.00 in 4 of 4 B = 125 cells (1.015, 1.003, 1.034, 1.011); swapped fold 0 of 4 pass, 12 of 12 floor, 0 of 12 offset below 0.97 (swapped offset ratios 0.992 to 1.022); pooled 16 1 of 4 pass, 12 of 12 floor. The pass failure does not depend on how "best learned family" is read: taking the better of the plain and offset ratio per cell, or the post hoc best of the 25 variants, still gives 1 of 4 cells at or above 1.02 (7B-AWQ m = 250 50%: 1.028 pooled, 1.034 offset, lower bounds 1.001 and 1.014). The selected pooled ratio at 3B m = 500 25% is 0.9995 unrounded, but the kill rule is about any variant (best 1.003) so it is not touched. Fold A has no exact ties in any selection (fixed, plain, offset, pooled); the swapped fold has exact ties only at the two B = 25 cells. Gate: gate_check.json shows 124 required and 30 extra checks, 0 differences. Determinism and implementation: I re-ran one sweep job (7B-AWQ, m = 250, subset 5) and all 116 policy-budget cells match the saved file; I wrote a separate offset learner with scipy L-BFGS instead of the IRLS ridge_logit (no shared code) and it reproduces the saved per-seed hits exactly in 40 of 40 values (7B-AWQ m = 250 subset 5 at 50% for lam 1, 10, 100 with (n0, R) in (0, 5), (5, 10), (0, 5); 3B m = 250 subset 2 at 25% for lam 1 and 10), so the offset arms do what the entry says. No leakage: the allocator features are the ten cheap and judge columns, standardization for the offset learner uses the m unlabeled sim rows, the judge sees only the question and the extracted final number (p0_judge_score.py), gold enters only through the judge_error label and the verification oracle, and static_out (fit on the outside prompts) is never a member of a selected family. Budget accounting is equal (budget = int(frac * m); charged calibration labels are spent and counted as hits; every learned arm counts each collected label at K = 1). No Traceback, warning, nan or convergence message in the 96 sim logs, 4 sanity logs and gate logs, each sim log has its 3 budget lines, exit_code 0, run.log empty as in L1b to L2b. The 8-subset bootstrap, the shared policy seeds and the single population are as the entry's caveat 7 says, and with CIs of about plus or minus 0.02 at m = 250 the 1.02 margin is not resolvable in a B = 125 cell, which is why the outcome is inconclusive and not a kill.

Corrections.
1. One transcribed cell. In both tables of the entry (and in ledger_table.md) the 3B m = 250 10% (B = 25) row gives swapped 1.004 and pooled 16 0.997; result.json and summary.log (and my script) give 0.995 and 0.993. The cause is an exact three-way tie on subsets 8 to 15 (offset_n0_R5_lam10, offset_n5_R5_lam10 and offset_n0_R10_lam10, all 21.828 hits): the summarizer takes offset_n0_R10_lam10 (first in sorted order, 21.78 held-out, 0.995), exp/l4a_verify.py takes offset_n0_R5_lam10 (first in insertion order, 21.97, 1.004). So the Audit sentence "swapped and pooled-16 ratios identical" is wrong for this cell, and the cell is tie-break dependent in the swapped fold (the other tied cell, 7B-AWQ m = 250 10%, gives the same 1.002 under both orders). Corrected values for the raw files: 0.995 and 0.993. Both are above the 0.97 floor and the fold A value (0.991) is unaffected.
2. Free-label gap percentages. The table is computed from the one-decimal means shown, which is imprecise at small gaps. From the raw per-seed hits: B = 25 3B -12% (-0.20 of 1.72 hits, not -11%), B = 50 3B -8% (not -7%), B = 50 7B-AWQ -28% (-0.62 of 2.25 hits, not -32% of 2.2), all other cells within 1 point of the table. So "captures -32% to 7% at B <= 100" reads "-28% to 7%"; the conclusion (no useful capture below 125 labels, at most 18% at B = 125) is unchanged.
3. "At B <= 100 no learner, plain or warm-start, beats the free agreement threshold" (the verdict Reading and "What it changes") is stated flatter than the data. Measured: in the verdict fold no learner is significantly above thr_agreement at B <= 100 (all 8 offset / thr_agreement CIs contain 1, 0.984 to 1.009). But 7B-AWQ m = 250 25% (B = 62) is above 1 with the CI excluding 1 in the swapped fold (1.015, 1.005 to 1.026) and pooled 16 (1.012, 1.003 to 1.021), and the post hoc best variant is 1.014, 1.006, 1.009, 1.010 and 1.005 at 7B-AWQ B = 25, 3B B = 50, 7B-AWQ B = 62, 3B B = 100 and 7B-AWQ B = 100. Read it as "no learner is shown to beat thr_agreement at B <= 100; the largest selected-arm edge is about +0.4 hits (7B-AWQ B = 62 and 3B B = 100) and it is not significant in the verdict fold".
4. The Phase 1 allocator spec in "What it changes" is a post hoc proposal, not something the pre-registered rules licensed. The PASS reading (warm-start form and refit cadence into the Phase 1 spec, break-even moves to about 60 to 125) did not fire, and the KILL reading did not fire either, so under the stated rule the outcome is "report the per-cell table and the ratio-versus-B curve". The B < 125 branch is operationally thr_agreement (the selected offset arms with lam 10 to 100 sit within 1.6% of it (0.984 to 1.009) with 0 gain and are the mechanism of the 0.97 floor pass), the B >= 250 branch comes from a different family, and 125 to 250 is untested. Treat "the spec now reads" as a provisional hypothesis to be tested by L5a to L5c, and keep thr_agreement as the committed default below about 125.
5. Figure. runs/L4a/ratio_vs_B.png joins cells of different m at the same B (vertical segments at B = 125 and 250) and its x tick labels overlap the minor log ticks near 25, 50 and 62; read the tables, not the line shapes.

Corrected verdict: INCONCLUSIVE (unchanged). Regression gate passed with 0 differences; pass cells 1 of 4 at B = 125 (7B-AWQ m = 250 50%, 1.028, CI 1.001 to 1.053; swapped 0 of 4, pooled 16 1 of 4), floor 12 of 12, selected offset below 0.97 in 0 of 12, best variant at or above 1.00 in 4 of 4 B = 125 cells, so neither the pass nor the kill rule fires.

## L5a 2026-10-18 inconclusive
Hypothesis: the B curve in L2b and L4a (learner ratio vs the best fixed rule rises from about 0.7 to 1.0 at B <= 125 to 1.04 to 1.09 at B >= 250; free-label gap captured 39% to 97% at B >= 250 but at most 18% at B <= 125) is a labels-in-hand effect, so a learner that keeps its labels across batches recovers the B >= 250 gain at a per-step B of 62 or 125, while a warm-start (offset) carry arm never starts below thr_agreement. Falsifier: carried labels are selection-biased (mostly low-agreement prompts), so carry could fail to beat the free agreement threshold, which would mean the B curve was a subset-size artifact and persistence does not rescue claim 2.

Run (CPU only, no GPU; launched through bin/gpu_run.sh, exit_code 0, about 16 min wall): E04 feature tables (3,000 GSM8K prompts x 8 Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct rollouts, ten cheap + judge features, label judge_error from the Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ answer-only judges). exp/l5a_carry.py copies run(), run_online(), run_offset() and ridge_logit() verbatim from exp/l4a_sim_alloc.py (that file executes at import) and adds carry_plain (greedy_n3_R1 logic, every earlier label kept, refit after every label, ranks only the current batch) and carry_offset (ridge-offset fit on all carried labels, refit every 5 labels, lam 10 and 1) with windowed variants W = 125, 250, 500 (last W labels only). 8 order seeds (permutation seed 10000 + order), 12 disjoint batches of m = 250 (each prompt used once per order seed), per-step budgets B = 62 (25%) and B = 125 (50%), policy seed = order seed, 32 sweep jobs (2 judges x 2 budgets x 8 order seeds). Arms per batch: thr_agreement (free), uniform, restart_offset (offset_n0_R5_lam10 from scratch), restart_plain (greedy_n3_R1 from scratch), carry_plain, carry_offset_lam10, carry_offset_lam1, carry_window{125,250,500}_lam{10,1}, calibrate_once (recal_charged_50 paid in batch 1, fitted rule reused free in batches 2 to 12), static_out (logistic rule on the 2,750 outside prompts with free labels). Standardization: restart arms standardize on the current batch as in L4a; carry_offset and carry_window use ONE global label-free z-score over all 3,000 prompts so carried and current rows share a scale; carry_plain keeps L4a's rule (StandardScaler fit on the labels in hand). Verdict fold: lam chosen per cell on order seeds 0 to 3, reported on 4 to 7 (4 order seeds), swapped fold and pooled 8 as cross-checks; percentile bootstrap over order seeds, 10,000 resamples; pooled over batches 4 to 12 (at least about 250 labels in hand). Files: runs/L5a/result.json, ledger_table.md, carry_ratio_vs_batch.png, summary.log, gate/gate_check.json, verify.log, 32 carry_<judge>_B<B>_o<seed>.json; scripts exp/l5a_carry.py, l5a_summarize.py, l5a_gate_check.py, l5a_verify.py, L5a.sh. runs/L5a/smoke holds smoke-test files that could not be deleted (harmless, not read by the summarizer).

Audit (I re-derived the numbers from the 32 raw per-seed JSONs with an independent script, exp/l5a_verify.py, own bootstrap seed 424242, instead of trusting the run report): every pooled ratio, CI end (within 0.002), lam choice, window ratio, gap-captured share and verdict count reproduces the summarizer. Regression gate: gate/gate_check.json shows 128 per-seed comparisons (thr_agreement, uniform, greedy_n3_R1, offset_n0_R5_lam10 x 8 policy seeds x 2 judges x 2 budgets on the L4a prompt set m = 250, subset 0) with 0 differences against runs/L4a, plus 176 self-check comparisons of the new carry code with 0 differences (carry arms equal run_online and run_offset on one batch; carry_offset with lam = infinity equals thr_agreement on every batch of a real 12-batch layout). Both in-file batch-1 equalities hold in all 32 files. I read the carry code for leakage: a label enters the carried set only after the pick is made, the current batch is scored by a fit on earlier labels, batches are disjoint, so no leakage. No Traceback in any log.

Pooled over batches 4 to 12, all 8 order seeds, ratio to thr_agreement (95% CI over order seeds; first row is the thr_agreement mean errors caught per batch of 250 prompts; mean errors per batch are 118.2 for 3B and 109.4 for 7B-AWQ):

Other measured quantities. Label horizon (carry_window_W / unbounded carry_offset_lam1, pooled 8, cells in the table order): W = 125: 0.966, 0.946, 0.969, 0.934; W = 250: 0.987, 0.965, 0.996, 0.974; W = 500: 1.001, 0.985, 1.001, 0.991. First batch at which the pooled-over-seeds ratio reaches 1.03 (raw / trailing 3 batches): carry_plain 3/4, 2/3, 3/4, 1/3; carry_offset_lam1 4/6, 2/4, 3/5, 1/3 (under the raw rule that is 125 to 250 labels collected through the crossing batch for both forms; under the less noisy trailing-3 rule it is 248 to 375 labels for plain and 310 to 500 for carry_offset_lam1). Carried-label selection bias (the falsifier's mechanism): the labels in hand are 65% to 83% errors against a population rate of 47.3% (3B) and 43.8% (7B-AWQ), with mean global z-agreement of -0.5 to -1.4, and the carry arms still beat thr_agreement. Restart control (no carry): restart_offset 0.997 to 1.008 sits inside the L4a band for the selected learner at B <= 125 (0.984 to 1.028 against the best fixed rule), and restart_plain 0.947, 1.014, 0.931, 1.043 reproduces L4a's held-out greedy_n3_R1 / thr_agreement at m = 250 (0.930, 1.014, 0.954, 1.028 for 3B B=62, 3B B=125, 7B-AWQ B=62, 7B-AWQ B=125) to within 0.025, and neither restart arm trends upward with batch index. calibrate_once with 50 labels never amortizes (0.991 to 1.014). Post hoc extras, not part of the pre-registered rules (in verify.log): over all 12 batches (equal total budget, warm-up batches included) carry_plain is 1.069, 1.090, 1.118, 1.135 times thr_agreement and carry_offset_lam1 is 1.035, 1.053, 1.028, 1.121; carry_plain is above 1 on batches 4 to 12 in 8 of 8 order seeds in every cell (minimum per-seed ratio 1.040 to 1.128) and carry_offset_lam1 in 8 of 8 in every cell, while the cumulative cold-start cost of carry_plain over batches 1 to 3 is nil (1.011, 1.052, 1.028, 1.113 times thr_agreement).

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: INCONCLUSIVE. The regression gate passed (a failed gate would have voided the run). PASS needs (a) carry_offset (better lam, chosen on order seeds 0 to 3, reported on 4 to 7) at ratio >= 1.03 with CI lower bound above 1 in all four cells: 3 of 4 hold, 7B-AWQ B=62 misses at 1.0295 (CI lower 1.011) by 0.0005; and (b) carry_window_250 within 2% of that arm: 2 of 4 hold (both B=62 cells; both B=125 cells are 3.4% and 2.8% below). No fold gives a PASS (swapped fold and pooled 8 give 4 of 4 on (a) but still fail (b) at both B=125 cells: 0.965 and 0.974 pooled). The wording of (b) does not say per cell; read per cell it fails, and the mean over cells (0.980) sits exactly on the line, so I take the strict reading and the verdict stands either way because (a) already misses in the verdict fold. KILL needs carry_plain and the better carry_offset both at ratio <= 1.00 in at least 3 of 4 cells: 0 of 4 (the lowest carry_offset ratio is 1.0295 and the lowest carry_plain ratio is 1.075 in the verdict fold). The falsifier did not fire. Note on pre-registration: the PASS rule tests the offset form, but the strongest persistent arm is carry_plain (1.075 to 1.144 in the verdict fold, every CI lower bound at or above 1.057); a PASS rule written on the better of the two forms would have passed 4 of 4, which is a post hoc observation and not a licensed verdict.

Caveats. (1) Batches are exchangeable draws from one fixed policy's rollouts, so there is no drift by construction and unbounded carry is an upper bound; for the same reason the window arms only lose sample size here. The window curve is a label-count dose-response (about 500 labels in hand keeps 98.5% to 100.1% of the unbounded gain, 125 labels keeps 93% to 97%), i.e. it gives the minimum label horizon a stationary learner needs, not a tolerable staleness; with B = 125, W = 250 spans only 2 batches, which is why the 2% test fails there. Real staleness needs drift injection or training-time rollouts (E13). (2) The 8 order seeds permute the same 3,000 prompts, so order-seed CIs are optimistic and the verdict fold has 4 order seeds. (3) One population, one task, offline replay on judge_error labels at a Qwen2.5-1.5B policy; L5c is the fresh-population check. (4) static_out is not a ceiling: carry_plain exceeds it in 2 of 4 cells (3B B=125, 7B-AWQ B=62) and is within 1% to 2% below it in the other two (labels from the learner's own top-scored region plausibly give a better ranker where the top-B cut falls than a fit on the whole population), so "gap captured" can exceed 100%. (5) lam = 1 is the lower edge of the offset grid and was chosen in all four cells. (6) calibrate_once uses only 50 labels, so it is a weak amortized fixed baseline, not the fairest one as the spec called it: a rule calibrated once on 250 or 500 paid labels was not tested, and that is the largest open hole for claim 2 in the persistent setting.

What it changes. The L2b and L4a B curve is a labels-in-hand effect, not a subset-size artifact: restart arms sit at 0.93 to 1.04 of thr_agreement at every batch index, while carry arms rise with batch index and cross 1.03 within the first 2 to 3 batches (125 to 250 labels collected; the trailing-3 average crosses at 250 to 375 labels) and then keep improving to about 1,500 labels (batches 7 to 12: carry_plain 1.09 to 1.15, carry_offset_lam1 1.04 to 1.14 of thr_agreement). Persistence converts a per-step B of 62 or 125 into the B >= 250 regime even though the carried labels are strongly selection-biased, and the total-budget comparison (all 12 batches, labels charged because they double as hits) stays above 1 in all four cells, by 7% to 13% for carry_plain and 3% to 12% for carry_offset_lam1, and over batches 4 to 12 both arms are above 1 in every one of the 8 order seeds (post hoc, verify.log). Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): conditional support is widened, not completed. It now holds in the offline replay at small per-step B provided labels persist across steps, against thr_agreement, the restart learners, a 50-label amortized fixed rule and (L2a, B >= 250) the charged one-shot fixed families; it is still not shown against a fixed rule calibrated once on 250 to 500 paid labels and amortized over the stream, under drift, or on a second population, and the pre-registered L5a PASS did not fire. The Phase 1 allocator spec changes from "B switch" to "persistent learner": keep a label store across steps, refit the plain learner (n0 = 3, R = 1 logic, class-balanced logistic on the ten cheap + judge features) on all labels each step; the warm-start offset form is not needed beyond the first batch (carry_plain's batches 1 to 3 already net to 1.01 to 1.11 of thr_agreement) and trails plain by 3 to 11 points in 3 of 4 cells, so the pre-registered reading "the Phase 1 allocator is a persistent warm-start learner" is only half supported (persistent yes, warm start no). The label horizon is at least about 500 labels, i.e. 4 to 8 batches at these budgets, so the training policy has to stay roughly stationary over that many steps' worth of verification for the learner to keep its edge; that is the E13 question. The Phase 1 kill test should no longer be expected to return thr_agreement at small per-step B if labels persist. Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): untouched and still not supported; L5a used only the ten cheap + judge features and no embedding or prompt-space feature, and the edge it measures is a persistent-refit effect (E01 and E04 kills stand: delta AUC about 0, neighbor ratio 1.00 on GSM8K). Claim 1 (same policy quality with fewer verifier calls) is untouched and needs the Phase 1 harness. The agreement threshold remains the free baseline every harness result must beat.

Follow-ups, ranked by expected information value (full specs in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under 'Proposed by the loop', Iteration 6).
1. L6a (CPU, about 10 min): large-n amortized fixed-rule control. Calibrate a fixed rule once on 125, 250 or 500 paid labels (random or lowest-agreement, charged inside the first batches' budgets and counted as hits), reuse it free for the rest of the 12 batches, and compare cumulative hits over all 12 batches with carry_plain. Highest value: it is the strongest fixed baseline still missing in the persistent setting (calibrate_once used only 50 labels), and it decides whether carry_plain's 7% to 13% edge is online selection or just labels in hand that any fixed rule would also use. Expectation before running (a back-of-envelope, untested): a random 250-label calibration costs about 75 hits at B = 62 and about 55 at B = 125 against what the threshold would have caught with those slots, so it should be a net loss or break-even; the contender is the seeded variant (lowest-agreement calibration labels double as hits, as in L2a), so the verdict on claim 2 turns on seeded amortization against carry_plain.
2. L6b (CPU, about 10 min): drift injection on the L5a layout (judge swap at batch 7: batches 1 to 6 from the 3B tables, batches 7 to 12 from the 7B-AWQ tables) with unbounded, windowed (W = 125, 250, 500) and exponentially decayed carry arms plus a reset-at-switch upper bound. High value: L5a's window curve cannot measure staleness because there is no drift by construction, and the Phase 1 harness trains a moving policy; the result gives the forgetting horizon (or shows persistence is unsafe) and feeds E13.
3. L6c (CPU, about 10 min): persistence at the thesis-relevant small budget fractions, B = 25 and 31 at m = 250 and B = 50, 62, 125 at m = 500 (6 batches), carry_plain and carry_offset_lam1 against thr_agreement and static_out. Medium value: tells whether carry still wins when only 10% to 12.5% of prompts are verified per step and whether the effect scales with the fraction B/m or with absolute labels per step (sizing rule for the Phase 1 verification budget). L5c (fresh population, needs Rustom's explicit override of the E04 stop rule) should add the persistent arms (carry_plain and carry_offset_lam1 over 12 batches at B = 62 and 125) when it is run, so one GPU spend answers the one-population caveat for L5a too; L5b is now lower value (plain beats offset under persistence in 3 of 4 cells, B is known at allocation time).

**Critic note (L5a, adversarial review 2026-10-18).** The INCONCLUSIVE verdict stands, and so does every verdict count (verdict fold: (a) 3 of 4 cells, (b) 2 of 4, KILL 0 of 4; gate 0 differences). The corrections below are one wrong provenance number, one mislabeled quantity that matters for the window-curve conclusion, three over-strong or imprecise readings and one robustness remark; none moves a pass or kill count. What I re-derived from the 32 raw carry_*.json with separate scripts (exp/l5a_critic.py, l5a_critic2.py, l5a_critic3.py; bootstrap seeds 777 and 31337, 20,000 resamples): every file has budget, m = 250, 12 batches and 12 values for every arm; no arm catches more than B in a batch and thr_agreement and carry_plain never exceed min(B, errors in the batch); carry_plain holds 12 x B labels after batch 12 (744 and 1,500); the batch error counts sum to 1,419 (3B) and 1,313 (7B-AWQ) for every order seed; the in-file batch-1 equalities are True in all 32 files. All 12 verdict-fold ratios reproduce (carry_offset_lam1 / thr 1.0368, 1.0608, 1.029518, 1.1348 with CI lower ends 1.020, 1.054, 1.011, 1.115; carry_plain / thr 1.0753, 1.0903, 1.1440, 1.1383; window250 / carry_offset 0.9845, 0.9662, 0.9977, 0.9717), lam 1 is chosen in all four cells on order seeds 0 to 3 (mean hits lam 10 / lam 1: 435.75 / 451.0, 795.75 / 827.0, 415.5 / 427.75, 759.5 / 806.5), and the whole pooled-8 table (static_out, carry_plain, both carry_offset lams, window250 and 500 lam 1, calibrate_once, restart_offset, restart_plain, uniform, gap captured) matches the entry to within 0.001 on ratios and CI ends. The 7B-AWQ B = 62 miss is real and exact: 1.029518, short of 1.03 by 0.00048. Gate: I re-compared the four g_*.json files with runs/L4a/sim_<judge>_m250_s0.json myself, 128 of 128 per-seed values equal, and all 176 self-check values are 0. Leakage: the ten allocator columns are agreement, n_distinct, mean_tokens, std_tokens, any_truncated, log_qlen and the four judge columns; none uses gold (the judge sees only the question and the extracted number, p0_judge_score.py), gold enters only through the judge_error label (rates 0.4730 and 0.4377, no NaN, so fillna(0) is inactive), a label joins the carried set only after its pick, the current batch is scored from earlier labels, and static_out fits on the 2,750 outside prompts and is never a policy. Budget accounting is equal (every arm verifies exactly B prompts per batch; calibrate_once pays its 50 labels inside batch 1). No Traceback, warning or nan in the 32 carry logs or the gate logs. The gate cannot exercise window truncation (one batch holds at most 125 labels), so I checked it from the data: at B = 62 carry_window500_lam1 equals carry_offset_lam1 exactly through batch 8 and diverges at batch 9 (labels pass 500), and window125_lam1 equals it through batch 2 and diverges at batch 3, so the slice binds where it should.

Corrections.
1. Wall time. The entry says "about 16 min wall". The file times give cmd.sh 21:18:59 and exit_code / done.flag 21:19:27 (28 s), carry_*.json between 21:19:09 and 21:19:25. result.json, summary.log, ledger_table.md and the figure are stamped 21:19:44 to 21:19:45, after done.flag, and exp/l5a_summarize.py was last edited at 21:19:43, so the summary and tables were regenerated by hand with the edited summarizer after the gpu_run job ended (exit_code 0 belongs to the earlier summarizer pass); verify.log (21:23:48) is also outside the job. My re-derivation reproduces every summarizer number, so this does not touch any result, but the run was a 30 s job, not 16 min.
2. The window curve is mislabeled as a share of the gain. Caveat 1 says "about 500 labels in hand keeps 98.5% to 100.1% of the unbounded gain, 125 labels keeps 93% to 97%". Those figures are carry_window_W hits divided by unbounded carry_offset_lam1 hits (the "Label horizon" row), not shares of the gain over thr_agreement. The gain over thr_agreement (pooled batches 4 to 12, all 8 order seeds, lam 1, cells in table order 3B B=62, 3B B=125, 7B-AWQ B=62, 7B-AWQ B=125) retained by the window is: W = 125: 0.13, 0.07, 0.14, 0.44; W = 250: 0.66, 0.40, 0.90, 0.78; W = 500: 1.02, 0.75, 1.03, 0.92 (carry_window125_lam1 / thr is 1.005, 1.004, 1.005, 1.060 against the unbounded 1.041, 1.062, 1.037, 1.135). So 125 labels keep 7% to 44% of the gain, 250 keep 40% to 90%, and 500 keep 75% to 103%, and the pre-registered "within 2%" test is on hits, which is lenient (W250 at 3B B=62 passes it at 0.984 while keeping 66% of the gain). The sentence "the label horizon is at least about 500 labels" survives as a lower bound, but 500 labels do not keep the full gain at 3B B=125 (75%) or 7B-AWQ B=125 (92%); staleness tolerance must be stated in gain terms, and L6b should report it that way.
3. "Restart arms sit at 0.93 to 1.04 of thr_agreement at every batch index" is the pooled range (restart_plain 0.931 to 1.043, restart_offset 0.997 to 1.008). Per batch index (mean over 8 order seeds) restart_plain ranges 0.880 to 1.101 and restart_offset 0.967 to 1.025. The substantive point stands: no upward trend with batch index (OLS slope per batch -0.0023 to +0.0001 for restart_plain, -0.0011 to -0.0005 for restart_offset).
4. "Carry arms ... cross 1.03 within the first 2 to 3 batches (trailing-3 average crosses at 250 to 375 labels) and then keep improving to about 1,500 labels" is looser than the data. Raw first crossing is batch 3, 2, 3, 1 for carry_plain but 4, 2, 3, 1 for carry_offset_lam1 (3B B=62 is batch 4), and the trailing-3 crossing at 250 to 375 labels holds for carry_plain only (batches 4, 3, 4, 3); carry_offset_lam1 trailing-3 crosses at batches 6, 4, 5, 3 (310 to 500 labels), as the "Other measured quantities" paragraph says correctly. There is also little improvement after about batches 4 to 6: pooled ratio to thr_agreement for batches 4 to 6 / 7 to 9 / 10 to 12 is carry_plain 1.079 / 1.084 / 1.102 (3B B=62), 1.102 / 1.093 / 1.111 (3B B=125), 1.139 / 1.162 / 1.143 (7B-AWQ B=62), 1.132 / 1.168 / 1.129 (7B-AWQ B=125) and carry_offset_lam1 1.032 / 1.042 / 1.048, 1.059 / 1.062 / 1.064, 1.029 / 1.049 / 1.033, 1.118 / 1.160 / 1.128, i.e. batches 7 to 12 add 0.00 to 0.03 over batches 4 to 6 and carry_plain is flat within noise in 3 of 4 cells. Read it as "rises over the first 3 to 6 batches (about 250 to 500 labels in hand, up to 750 at B = 125 for offset), then roughly plateaus".
5. "A PASS rule written on the better of the two forms would have passed 4 of 4" is true only for clause (a). Clause (b) cannot be evaluated for carry_plain because no windowed plain arm was run, and for the offset form it fails at both B = 125 cells. Under that rewritten rule the outcome would be undetermined, not a pass.
6. The verdict-fold miss at 7B-AWQ B = 62 is noise-sized, and what makes the outcome robustly inconclusive is clause (b). Per-seed carry_offset / thr on order seeds 4 to 7 is 1.063, 1.033, 1.015, 1.007 (SD about 0.025), the swapped fold gives 1.045 and pooled 8 gives 1.037 (CI 1.025 to 1.048) for the same cell, so the swapped fold and pooled 8 both pass clause (a) in all four cells. Clause (b) fails at both B = 125 cells in all three folds (0.966 and 0.972 verdict fold, 0.965 and 0.976 swapped, 0.965 and 0.974 pooled), and the cell-mean 0.980 is on the line only under a reading the rule does not state. Also, with 4 order seeds a percentile bootstrap has only 35 distinct resamples and behaves like the min-to-max range of the per-seed ratios, so "CI lower bound above 1" means "all four seeds above 1", and since the 8 order seeds permute the same 3,000 prompts the intervals say nothing about prompt-sampling variance (the entry's caveat 2 stands and is stronger than it reads).
7. "What it changes" states as settled that the B curve "is a labels-in-hand effect, not a subset-size artifact" and that "the Phase 1 allocator spec changes from 'B switch' to 'persistent learner'". Neither is licensed by the pre-registered rules (PASS and KILL both did not fire, and the PASS reading "persistent warm-start learner" is, as the entry itself says, only half supported). What the data support is narrower: carry_plain beats thr_agreement by 7.5% to 14.4% in the verdict fold with every CI lower end at or above 1.057 and in 8 of 8 order seeds, in a stationary, drift-free replay on one population with the 8 order seeds sharing the same 3,000 prompts, and restart controls do not gain. Treat the persistent-learner spec as a post hoc hypothesis for L6a to L6c and L5c, as the L4a critic note did for its spec, and keep thr_agreement as the committed default until a fixed rule calibrated on 250 to 500 paid labels (L6a), drift (L6b) and a fresh population (L5c) have been run.

Corrected verdict: INCONCLUSIVE (unchanged). Regression gate passed with 0 differences (128 per-seed and 176 self-check comparisons, re-verified); pre-registered PASS needs (a) in 4 of 4 cells and (b) in 4 of 4: verdict fold gives (a) 3 of 4 (7B-AWQ B = 62 at 1.0295, CI lower 1.011) and (b) 2 of 4 (window250 / carry_offset 0.984, 0.966, 0.998, 0.972); KILL (carry_plain and carry_offset both at or below 1.00 in at least 3 of 4 cells) 0 of 4 (lowest ratios 1.0295 and 1.075); so neither rule fires and the per-cell table and the ratio-versus-batch curves are the result.

## L6a 2026-10-18 pass (claim 2 survives the strongest persistent fixed baseline in the pre-registered family; the edge comes from the learner's own label stream, not from label count)
Hypothesis: carry_plain's 7 to 13 percent edge over thr_agreement in L5a (1.069, 1.090, 1.118, 1.135 over all 12 batches at 3B / 7B-AWQ x B = 62 / 125) is a labels-in-hand effect, so a fixed rule calibrated ONCE on 125, 250 or 500 paid labels (calibration charged inside the first batches and counted as hits) and then reused free would get within 1 percent of carry_plain. L5a's calibrate_once used only 50 labels (0.991 to 1.014 of thr_agreement). Falsifier (claim 2 survives): the best amortized fixed arm stays at least 3 percent below carry_plain over 12 batches in at least 3 of 4 cells. Pre-registered rules: PASS = carry_plain / best amortized >= 1.03 with the bootstrap CI lower bound above 1 in at least 3 of 4 cells (gate must pass); KILL = best amortized >= 0.99 x carry_plain in at least 3 of 4 cells; otherwise inconclusive.

Run (CPU only, no GPU, no generation; launched through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L6a 'bash exp/L6a.sh' after gpu_status.sh showed 15 MiB used; exit_code 0, 37 s wall, cmd.sh 21:38:04 to done.flag 21:38:41): the L5a layout on the E04 feature tables (3,000 GSM8K prompts, ten cheap + judge features, label judge_error from the Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ judges), permutation default_rng(10000 + order seed), 12 disjoint batches of 250, per-step B = 62 and 125, policy seed = order seed, 16 order seeds (64 jobs, about 4 s each standalone). exp/l6a_amort.py copies the L5a functions verbatim (run, run_online, ridge_logit, run_offset, carry_offset; carry_plain also returns its labels-in-hand list) and adds amort_{seed,rand,split}_{125,250,500} and the diagnostic freeze_plain_{125,250,500}. With k = ceil(n / B) calibration batches: amort_seed_n = thr_agreement picks in batches 1 to k (hits identical to thr_agreement), one StandardScaler + balanced logistic fit on the labels paid in those batches at the end of batch k, reused free (top B by score) afterwards; amort_rand_n = random picks in batches 1 to k-1 and the last n - (k-1)B random labels in batch k, fit on exactly n random labels, rest of batch k ranked by the rule (recal_charged style); amort_split_n = the same with calibration picks restricted to n_distinct > 1 (the L2a range-restriction guard); one-class calibration falls back to thr_agreement (never triggered in 768 fits, 576 amortized plus 192 freeze). freeze_plain_n = carry_plain through batch k, then the rule refit on the k*B labels in hand frozen. Two interpretation choices to flag: amort_seed_n fits on every label paid in its k calibration batches (k*B >= n, so 186 / 310 / 558 labels at B = 62 and exactly 125 / 250 / 500 at B = 125), which gives the seeded arm at least the nominal label count; freeze_plain_n refits on the k*B labels in hand at the end of batch k, which is carry_plain's own fit for the first pick of batch k+1. The 9 amortized arms are the pre-registered family, none chosen on held-out data; the best one per cell is chosen on order seeds 0 to 7 by cumulative 12-batch hits and reported on seeds 8 to 15 (verdict fold), with the swapped fold and pooled 16 as cross-checks (percentile bootstrap over order seeds, 10,000 resamples).

Audit. Regression gate: 0 differences in 2,688 per-batch values (224 vectors = thr_agreement, uniform, restart_offset, carry_plain, carry_offset_lam1, static_out and batch_base_errors x 4 cells x order seeds 0 to 7) against runs/L5a, and 0 failures of the in-job self-checks (batch-1 carry_plain = restart_plain; amort_seed_n hits = thr_agreement hits in batches 1 to k; freeze_plain_n = carry_plain in batches 1 to k; fit-set sizes) in all 64 jobs. Independent audit exp/l6a_verify.py (runs/L6a/verify.log, ALL CHECKS PASS): the verdict-fold headline re-derived from the raw JSONs with its own bootstrap seed (424242) gives the same best arm and ratio in all 4 cells and CI lower ends within 0.0006; carry_plain, the 9 amortized arms and the 3 freeze arms re-implemented in a different code structure from the parquet tables reproduce all 12 per-batch hits for four (cell, order seed) pairs (3B B=125 seed 5, 3B B=62 seed 3, 7B-AWQ B=62 seed 11, 7B-AWQ B=125 seed 14); amort_rand_n batch-1 hits equal uniform batch-1 hits in all 192 (cell, seed, n) combinations (same rng stream).

Other measured quantities. (1) Dose-response of the freeze diagnostic (freeze_plain_n / carry_plain, n = 125 / 250 / 500, all 16 seeds): 3B B=62 0.973 / 0.986 / 0.997; 3B B=125 0.948 / 0.968 / 0.988; 7B-AWQ B=62 0.957 / 0.985 / 1.000; 7B-AWQ B=125 0.949 / 0.973 / 1.001. With 186 to 558 labels from the learner's own stream and no further refitting, the frozen rule keeps 95 to 100 percent of carry_plain, so continued online refitting beyond about 500 labels is worth at most 1.2 percent here. (2) Fixed rules fitted on the same number of labels from another source do much worse over 12 batches: amort_seed 0.68 to 0.95 of carry_plain (collapsing on 7B-AWQ B=62, 0.676 to 0.793, the L2a range-restriction failure), amort_rand 0.69 to 0.94, amort_split 0.78 to 0.94; the best of the 9 on all 16 seeds (an optimistic post hoc choice) is amort_seed_250 0.939, amort_seed_250 0.945, amort_split_125 0.897, amort_rand_125 0.936 of carry_plain, i.e. at least 5.5 percent below in every cell. Only 5 of the 36 amortized (arm, cell) pairs reach 1.03 x thr_agreement or more (all in 7B-AWQ B=125), none reaches carry_plain. (3) Where the deficit comes from, for the verdict-fold best arm (errors per seed on seeds 8 to 15, deficit vs carry_plain during calibration batches 1 to k / after): 3B B=62 amort_seed_500 39.2 total = 23.0 / 16.2; 3B B=125 amort_seed_500 64.2 = 19.8 / 44.5; 7B-AWQ B=62 amort_split_125 70.0 = 27.5 / 42.5; 7B-AWQ B=125 amort_rand_125 72.6 = 32.8 / 39.9. So about 30 to 60 percent is calibration cost (random picks catch 0.59 to 0.69 of the hits thr_agreement catches per calibration batch, the uniform ratio, because errors are 0.44 to 0.48 of random labels) and 40 to 70 percent is a residual gap after calibration. (4) Steady state, batches 7 to 12, ratio to thr_agreement: 3B B=62 carry_plain 1.097, thr_agreement 1.000, best fixed arm on 16 seeds 1.064; 3B B=125 carry_plain 1.105, thr_agreement 1.000, best fixed arm on 16 seeds 1.076; 7B-AWQ B=62 carry_plain 1.157, thr_agreement 1.000, best fixed arm on 16 seeds 1.124; 7B-AWQ B=125 carry_plain 1.144, thr_agreement 1.000, best fixed arm on 16 seeds 1.151. After calibration the best fixed arm reaches 0.969, 0.974, 0.971 and 1.006 of carry_plain, so a longer horizon would shrink the 12-batch deficit; the extrapolated break-even horizon (post hoc, stationary replay only, verify.log section 4) is never in 23 of the 24 rand / split (arm, cell) pairs and 242 batches in the 24th, because carry_plain is still ahead after calibration. (5) First batch from which the cumulative ratio to thr_agreement stays above 1.00 (all 16 seeds, cells in table order): carry_plain 3 / 2 / 3 / 1, freeze_plain_500 3 / 2 / 3 / 1, the verdict-fold best amortized arm never / 5 / never / 5 (the full table is in runs/L6a/ledger_table.md). (6) carry_offset_lam1 / carry_plain is 0.970, 0.966, 0.927, 0.990; static_out / carry_plain is 1.031, 1.006, 1.024, 1.029.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: PASS. The regression gate passed with 0 differences. carry_plain / best amortized fixed arm is 1.068 (CI lower 1.052), 1.060 (1.030), 1.129 (1.069), 1.072 (1.063) in the verdict fold, so the pass cell rule (>= 1.03 with CI lower above 1) holds in 4 of 4 cells (3 needed); the swapped fold (1.071, 1.061, 1.138, 1.108) and pooled 16 (1.070, 1.061, 1.134, 1.090) also pass in 4 of 4 cells with every CI lower end at or above 1.032; KILL cells (best amortized >= 0.99 x carry_plain) 0 of 4 in every fold, the highest best / carry_plain being 0.943. The falsifier did not hold, so claim 2 survives against the strongest persistent fixed baseline in the pre-registered family.

Decomposition read. The hypothesis that the persistent edge is a label-count effect is refuted in its stated form: a rule fitted once on 125 to 558 labels from thr_agreement, random or n_distinct > 1 picks does not get within 1 percent of carry_plain (no arm of the 9 gets above 0.945 of carry_plain over 12 batches, and after calibration the best of them reaches 0.97 to 1.01 in batches 7 to 12 on a post hoc choice), while the same count of labels from the learner's own greedy stream, frozen, does (0.997, 1.000, 1.001 at about 500 labels in 3 of 4 cells, 0.988 at 3B B=125). What carries the edge is which labels are in hand, not how many, and refitting after the first few hundred labels adds little. The learner's stream is not cheaper per label than the seeded one (error rate of the labels in the freeze fit sets 0.68 to 0.82 against 0.63 to 0.78 for amort_seed), so the likely mechanism is coverage: the greedy stream is not range-restricted the way a lowest-agreement sample is, which is the L2a failure that collapses amort_seed on 7B-AWQ B=62; that mechanism is consistent with the data but was not isolated here. Calibration cost is the second component: charging calibration inside a 12-batch horizon accounts for about 30 to 60 percent of the best arm's 12-batch deficit.

Caveats. (1) Batches are exchangeable draws of one fixed policy: no drift by construction, the setting most favourable to a once-fitted rule; L6b tests drift. (2) The 16 order seeds permute the same 3,000 prompts, so the order-seed CIs are optimistic (order and tie-break variation only, not prompt-sampling variation) and each fold has 8 order seeds; the 7B-AWQ B=62 verdict-fold CI (1.069 to 1.235) is wide because amort_split_125 fits vary a lot across seeds (on seeds 8 to 15 its ratio to thr_agreement is 0.979 with CI 0.898 to 1.034). (3) One population, one task (GSM8K judge_error labels from E04), offline replay on weak-judge labels; the 3B judge barely passed the E04 validity gate. (4) The fixed-rule family is the pre-registered one (a logistic rule on the ten features, calibrated on seeded, random or n_distinct > 1 picks); a stratified or uncertainty-sampled calibration set would be a stronger fixed baseline but is itself an active-learning allocator, and freeze_plain shows such a stream works, which is the learner's contribution and not a counterexample. (5) The 12-batch horizon with calibration charged is part of the test; at B = 62 and n = 500 calibration takes 9 of 12 batches, and the break-even extrapolation in (4) above assumes stationarity. (6) amort_seed_n uses k*B >= n labels at B = 62, as stated above; the freeze arms are diagnostics and were not part of the verdict or the selection.

What it changes. The L5a follow-up that L5a's Critic named as the largest open hole for claim 2 (a fixed rule calibrated once on 250 to 500 paid labels) is closed in claim 2's favour on this replay: it does not recover the persistent learner's edge, and the Phase 1 harness is justified against that baseline on this ground (it is not yet justified against drift or a fresh population; L6b and L5c remain). The Phase 1 allocator spec can be narrower than "persistent learner forever": a learner that picks its own labels for the first 2 to 9 batches (about 250 to 560 labels) and is then frozen keeps 0.97 to 1.00 of carry_plain's 12-batch total on this replay, and the freeze point is a cheap knob to test in GRPO. thr_agreement stays the committed default until L6b and L5c.

## L6a 2026-10-18 pass
(Loop-level entry written after re-deriving the headline from the raw JSONs; the run-level entry above, line 466, carries the long audit text and every secondary table. If the two ever disagree, the numbers below were recomputed by exp/l6a_loopcheck.py and exp/l6a_horizon.py.)

Hypothesis. carry_plain's 7 to 13 percent total-budget edge over thr_agreement in L5a (1.069, 1.090, 1.118, 1.135 over all 12 batches at 3B / 7B-AWQ x B = 62 / 125) is a labels-in-hand effect, not an online-selection effect: a fixed rule calibrated ONCE on 125, 250 or 500 paid labels (calibration charged inside the first batches and counted as hits), then reused free, gets within 1 percent of carry_plain. L5a's calibrate_once used only 50 labels (0.991 to 1.014 of thr_agreement), so the strongest fixed baseline in the persistent setting had never been tested. Falsifier (claim 2 survives): the best amortized fixed arm stays at least 3 percent below carry_plain over 12 batches in at least 3 of 4 cells.

What was run. CPU only, no GPU, no generation (E04 stop rule untouched); launched through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L6a, gpu_status showed 15 MiB used, exit_code 0, 37 s wall. Task: GSM8K, 3,000 prompts, label judge_error from the E04 feature tables of Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ judges, ten cheap + judge features. Layout of L5a: permutation default_rng(10000 + order seed), 12 disjoint batches of m = 250, per-step budget B = 62 and 125 (25 and 50 percent), policy seed = order seed, 16 order seeds (0 to 15), 64 jobs (2 judges x 2 B x 16 seeds). Arms: references thr_agreement, uniform, restart_offset, carry_plain, carry_offset_lam1, static_out (verbatim from L5a); the pre-registered fixed family amort_{seed,rand,split}_{125,250,500} (one StandardScaler + balanced logistic fit on the calibration labels at the end of calibration batch k = ceil(n / B), then reused free; seed = lowest-agreement picks, rand = random picks, split = random picks among n_distinct > 1); diagnostics freeze_plain_{125,250,500} (carry_plain through batch k, then the last fit frozen), which never enter selection or verdict. Selection as L5a / L2a: best of the 9 amortized arms per cell chosen on order seeds 0 to 7 by cumulative 12-batch hits, reported on seeds 8 to 15 (verdict fold), swapped fold and pooled 16 as cross-checks, percentile bootstrap over order seeds (10,000 resamples).
