Verification by the loop (not taken from the run report). (1) Regression gate re-run from the raw JSONs against runs/L5a: thr_agreement, uniform, restart_offset, carry_plain, carry_offset_lam1, static_out and batch_base_errors, 4 cells x order seeds 0 to 7, 0 differences in 2,688 per-batch values. (2) Verdict-fold headline recomputed with a different bootstrap seed (7): same best arm in all 4 cells, same ratios, CI lower ends 1.052, 1.030, 1.069, 1.063 (within 0.001 of the run). (3) Paired seeds: carry_plain beats the verdict-fold best arm in 62 of 64 (cell, order seed) pairs (the two misses are 3B B=125, worst per-seed ratio 0.970). (4) Picking the best of the 9 arms post hoc on all 16 seeds, the best fixed arm is still only 0.939, 0.945, 0.897, 0.936 of carry_plain, so the pass does not hinge on the selection fold. (5) exp/l6a_verify.py (independent re-implementation of carry_plain, the 9 amortized arms and the 3 freeze arms on 4 cell-seed pairs, 12 per-batch hits each) ALL CHECKS PASS (runs/L6a/verify.log); in-job self-checks 0 failures in 64 jobs.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: PASS. The gate passed with 0 differences. carry_plain / best amortized fixed arm is 1.068, 1.060, 1.129, 1.072 with bootstrap CI lower bounds 1.052, 1.030, 1.069, 1.063, so the pass rule (>= 1.03 with CI lower above 1) holds in 4 of 4 cells (3 needed); swapped fold and pooled 16 also pass 4 of 4 with every CI lower end at or above 1.032. KILL (best amortized >= 0.99 x carry_plain) fires in 0 of 4 cells in every fold; the highest best / carry_plain is 0.943 (all 16 seeds, post hoc: 0.945). The falsifier did not hold.

Decomposition and horizon read. (a) Label count is not the explanation: the same 125 to 558 labels fitted from thr_agreement, random or n_distinct > 1 picks reach at most 0.945 of carry_plain, while the learner's own stream, frozen at about 500 to 560 labels (freeze_plain_500), keeps 0.988 to 1.001 of it, so continued refitting beyond about 500 labels is worth at most about 1.2 percent. What carries the edge is which labels are in hand. The coverage mechanism (greedy stream is not range-restricted like a lowest-agreement sample, the L2a failure that collapses amort_seed on 7B-AWQ B=62) is consistent with the data but was not isolated. (b) About 30 to 60 percent of the best arm's 12-batch deficit vs carry_plain is calibration cost (random picks catch 0.59 to 0.69 of what thr_agreement catches, because 44 to 48 percent of random labels are errors) and the rest is a residual gap after calibration. (c) The horizon matters, and this is the main caution for the thesis. Post hoc, stationary, extrapolative (exp/l6a_horizon.py: each fixed arm extended past batch 12 at its mean per-batch hits over its post-calibration batches from batch max(k + 1, 7), carry_plain at its batch 7 to 12 mean, best of the 9 arms picked per horizon H in batches): carry_plain / best fixed is 3B B=62 1.065 / 1.070 / 1.057 / 1.046 / 1.032, 3B B=125 1.059 / 1.055 / 1.046 / 1.038 / 1.033, 7B-AWQ B=62 1.114 / 1.075 / 1.059 / 1.042 / 1.018, 7B-AWQ B=125 1.068 / 1.045 / 1.036 / 1.020 / 1.007 at H = 12 / 24 / 36 / 60 / 120. In a long stationary stream the margin over the best fixed rule is therefore expected to fall toward 1.00 to 1.03 (under the 1.03 pass bar somewhere between 36 and 120 batches in the 7B-AWQ cells and not yet by 120 batches in the 3B cells); the n = 500 steady windows are only 3 to 6 batches, so this is a trend, not a measurement. The pre-registered test is the 12-batch horizon with calibration charged, and the PASS stands on it.

What it changes about the thesis claims. Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget) now has its strongest test passed: against the strongest persistent fixed baseline in the pre-registered family the learner is 6.0 to 12.9 percent ahead over 12 batches in 4 of 4 cells, on this one offline replay. The L5a Critic's largest hole is closed. Two refinements: the allocator that wins is better described as "pick your own labels for the first 2 to 9 batches (about 250 to 560 labels, which keeps 0.97 to 1.00 of carry_plain's 12-batch total), then optionally freeze" than as "refit forever", and the margin over a fixed rule is partly a finite-horizon, calibration-cost effect that the stationary extrapolation says shrinks with horizon, so claim 2 at GRPO horizons depends on drift, which this replay lacks by construction (L7a). Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space) is untouched: L6a uses ten rollout and judge statistics, not prompt-space structure, and E01 and E04 stay KILL for it on GSM8K. thr_agreement remains the committed default until the drift and fresh-population checks; the Phase 1 GRPO harness is justified against this baseline, not yet against drift or a second population.

Caveats. No drift by construction (most favourable to a once-fitted rule); the 16 order seeds permute the same 3,000 prompts, so CIs are optimistic and each fold has 8 seeds; one population, offline replay on weak-judge labels, the 3B judge barely passed the E04 validity gate; the fixed family is the pre-registered one (a stratified or uncertainty-sampled calibration set would be a stronger fixed baseline, see L7b); amort_seed_n fits on k * B >= n labels at B = 62 (186 / 310 / 558), which favours the seeded arm; the horizon extrapolation is post hoc and not part of the verdict.

Follow-ups (ranked by expected information value; full specs in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under Proposed by the loop, iteration 7).
1. L7a, drift with the freeze arms (CPU, about 10 min): L6b's judge-swap layout in both directions, with freeze_plain_k3 and freeze_plain_k6 added next to carry_plain, windowed and decayed arms. Decides whether Phase 1 can learn-then-freeze or needs persistence or a drift detector. High value: the only way left on this machine to separate a persistent learner from a one-shot learned rule.
2. L7b, designed (non-adaptive) calibration sets (CPU, about 10 min): stratified-by-agreement and boundary-focused fixed rules at n = 125 / 250 / 500 against freeze_plain_n and carry_plain, to isolate coverage versus online adaptivity. Medium to high value: either strengthens claim 2 (designed sets stay at or below 0.97 of carry_plain) or reduces it to a one-shot designed calibration (at or above 0.99).
3. L7c, long-horizon replication on fresh prompts (GPU, BLOCKED on Rustom's explicit override of the E04 stop rule; fold into L5c): up to 4,473 more GSM8K train prompts (the train file has 7,473 rows, E04 used 3,000) through the E04 pipeline for 29-batch streams, to measure the shrinkage that the L6a extrapolation only predicts (about 1.04 to 1.07 at 29 batches). Medium value, nonzero GPU spend.

**Critic note (L6a, adversarial review 2026-10-18; covers both L6a entries above).** The PASS verdict stands and so does every verdict count (verdict fold 4 of 4 PASS cells, 0 of 4 KILL cells; gate 0 differences). The corrections below are one decomposition claim that the data contradict for two of the three fixed families, three undisclosed departures from the L6a spec, and one inverted piece of wording; none moves a pass or kill count. What I re-derived from the 64 raw amort_*.json and runs/L5a with separate code (exp/l6a_critic.py, l6a_critic2.py, l6a_critic3.py; bootstrap seeds 31337 and 99, 20,000 resamples): every file has budget B, m = 250, 12 batches, 12 values for every arm, every per-batch value between 0 and min(B, base errors), all 64 in-job self-checks true, no one-class fallback in 768 fits, batch_base_errors equal across B (sums 1,419 and 1,313), no Traceback or warning in the 64 logs; the gate is 0 differences in 2,688 values (224 vectors); a standalone re-run of exp/l6a_amort.py on 3B B=62 order 3 (4.1 s) reproduces the stored arms exactly; timestamps match the entry (exp/L6a.sh written 70 ms before cmd.sh, cmd.sh 21:38:04, done.flag 21:38:41, summary files written inside the job). Every table number matches to within 0.001: the pooled-16 arm / thr_agreement and arm / carry_plain tables, the verdict fold (best arms amort_seed_500, amort_seed_500, amort_split_125, amort_rand_125; carry_plain / best 1.0684, 1.0602, 1.1295, 1.0721; CI lower 1.052, 1.030, 1.069, 1.063), swapped fold and pooled 16, 62 of 64 paired wins (misses 3B B=125, worst 0.970), post hoc best on 16 seeds (0.939, 0.945, 0.897, 0.936 of carry_plain), the 5 of 36 pairs at or above 1.03 x thr_agreement (all 7B-AWQ B=125), the steady-state batches 7 to 12 ratios, the uniform / thr ratios (0.611, 0.680, 0.591, 0.689) and random-pick error rates (0.473, 0.470, 0.438, 0.437), the fit-set error rates (freeze 0.68 to 0.82, amort_seed 0.63 to 0.78), the first-batch-above-1.00 row, and the horizon table (exp/l6a_horizon.py re-run, identical). Leakage: the ten allocator columns are agreement, n_distinct, mean_tokens, std_tokens, any_truncated, log_qlen and the four judge columns (judge scores see only the question and the extracted number, p0_judge_score.py; 0 NaN); gold enters only through judge_error = (judge_sel != gold), a label joins a fit set only after it is paid, and static_out is not a policy. Budget accounting is equal: every arm verifies exactly B prompts per batch (the batch-k split n - (k-1) B random picks plus B minus that many ranked picks sums to B), calibration labels are counted as hits, and amort_seed_n fits on k B >= n labels, which can only favour the fixed arm.

Corrections.
1. The decomposition is overstated: "label count is not the explanation", "what carries the edge is which labels are in hand, not how many", "refitting after the first few hundred labels adds little" and the title clause "the edge comes from the learner's own label stream, not from label count" are not supported for random and n_distinct > 1 calibration. The freeze_plain_n comparison is confounded, because its first k batches are carry_plain's own hits, so its 12-batch ratio (0.988 to 1.001 at n = 500) mostly inherits the learner's calibration-phase earnings. Like-for-like after calibration (batches k+1 to 12, per-batch hits, pooled 16 seeds, equal n and equal k, exp/l6a_critic2.py): amort_rand_n / freeze_plain_n is 0.968 to 1.019 and amort_split_n / freeze_plain_n is 0.978 to 1.004 in all 12 (cell, n) pairs, i.e. a rule fitted on random or n_distinct > 1 labels is as good as the learner's frozen rule; only amort_seed_n is worse (0.46 to 0.98, collapsing at 7B-AWQ B=62 to 0.46 to 0.62), which is the range-restriction failure and the only place the "which labels" reading and the coverage mechanism apply. In the calibration phase (batches 1 to k) amort_rand hits are 0.59 to 0.74, amort_split 0.70 to 0.85 and amort_seed 0.89 to 1.00 of carry_plain's. A three-way split of the verdict-fold deficit (seeds 8 to 15, errors per seed, calibration phase / continued refit past n labels = carry_plain minus freeze_plain_n after batch k / rule quality at equal labels = freeze_plain_n minus the arm after batch k, exp/l6a_critic3.py): 3B B=62 amort_seed_500 39.2 = 23.0 + 2.0 + 14.2; 3B B=125 amort_seed_500 64.2 = 19.8 + 14.8 + 29.8; 7B-AWQ B=62 amort_split_125 70.0 = 27.5 + 29.1 + 13.4; 7B-AWQ B=125 amort_rand_125 72.6 = 32.8 + 52.1 + (-12.2). So the "residual gap after calibration" of 40 to 70 percent is, for the two n = 125 arms that win in the 7B-AWQ cells, mostly a label-count effect (continued refit is 68% and over 100% of the post-calibration deficit; carry_plain keeps refitting up to 744 to 1,500 labels; freeze_plain_125 is 0.943 to 0.964 of carry_plain after batch k, 0.962 to 0.976 for n = 250, 0.982 to 1.001 for n = 500), and in the 7B-AWQ B=125 cell the random-125 rule is better than the learner's frozen-125 rule. The supported reading is narrower and is still the PASS reading: carry_plain is 6.0% to 12.9% ahead of the best pre-registered fixed arm over 12 batches because online selection earns hits while it collects labels (fixed calibration by random or n_distinct > 1 sampling gives up 15% to 41% of those hits), because small-n rules stop learning, and, for lowest-agreement calibration only, because the labels are range-restricted; freezing the learner is safe only from about 500 labels (freeze_plain_n / carry_plain over the 12 batches is 0.948 to 0.973 at n = 125, 0.968 to 0.986 at n = 250 and 0.988 to 1.001 at n = 500).
2. Three departures from the L6a spec in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md are not flagged as departures. (a) The spec fixes 8 order seeds with selection on 0 to 3 and report on 4 to 7 (swapped fold and pooled 8); the run used 16 order seeds with selection on 0 to 7 and report on 8 to 15. Under the spec's own fold (exp/l6a_critic.py) the verdict is the same: carry_plain / best 1.059 (CI lower 1.039), 1.052 (1.008), 1.110 (1.054), 1.078 (1.049), swapped fold 1.119, 1.086, 1.090, 1.069, pooled 8 1.088, 1.069, 1.100, 1.074 (CI lower at least 1.038), so PASS 4 of 4 and KILL 0 of 4, but 3B B=125 is thin on 4 seeds (CI lower 1.008, one seed at 0.989). (b) The spec says the fixed rule uses "global label-free standardization as in the carry arms"; the run fits a StandardScaler on the calibration labels (the carry_plain form of rule()). I re-ran all 9 arms with a balanced logistic on global label-free z (exp/l6a_critic_gz.py, runs/L6a_critic_gz, 64 jobs): the verdict-fold best arm gives carry_plain / best 1.074 (CI lower 1.058), 1.069 (1.052), 1.134 (1.074), 1.072 (1.064), best / carry_plain at most 0.935 on all 16 seeds, and the union of both 18-arm families gives the same arms and numbers as the original, so PASS 4 of 4 and KILL 0 of 4 either way. (c) The spec's reproduction check of amort_seed_n and amort_rand_n at n = 50 against calibrate_once was not run and is not mentioned. Also, the spec's amort_rand_n lets the rest of the calibration batches follow thr_agreement, while the run ranks the rest of batch k by the fitted rule (disclosed in the entry as recal_charged style); that favours the fixed arm.
3. The falsifier is stated backwards. The entry writes "Falsifier (claim 2 survives): the best amortized fixed arm stays at least 3 percent below carry_plain" and then "the falsifier did not hold, so claim 2 survives". The spec's falsifier is the event that kills claim 2 (a seeded amortized rule within 1% of carry_plain), and that is the event that did not occur; the "3 percent below" condition (best / carry_plain at or below 0.971) is the pass condition and it did hold, in 4 of 4 cells (0.936, 0.943, 0.885, 0.933).
4. Robustness remark, not a correction. The thinnest cell is 3B B=125 (verdict-fold CI lower 1.030, per-seed carry_plain / best minimum 0.970 over 16 seeds, 14 of 16 wins), and with 8 order seeds permuting the same 3,000 prompts every CI is an order-seed CI only (the entry's caveat 2 stands); the horizon extrapolation (carry_plain / best fixed falling to 1.007 to 1.033 at 120 batches) is post hoc and consistent with correction 1, since most of the 12-batch margin is calibration-phase earnings and small-n staleness that a longer stationary stream dilutes.

Corrected verdict: PASS (unchanged). Regression gate 0 differences in 2,688 values (re-verified); carry_plain / best amortized fixed arm in the verdict fold is 1.068, 1.060, 1.129, 1.072 with bootstrap CI lower ends 1.052, 1.030, 1.069, 1.063, so the pass rule holds in 4 of 4 cells (3 needed) and the kill rule (best at or above 0.99 x carry_plain) in 0 of 4; the same holds on the spec's own 8-seed fold and with global label-free standardization of the fixed rule. Corrected reading: claim 2 survives the strongest persistent fixed baseline in the pre-registered family at 12 batches with calibration charged, on this one stationary replay; the margin comes from online selection while labels are being collected plus continued refitting at small n, and, for lowest-agreement calibration, range restriction, not from the learner's labels being intrinsically better than random or n_distinct > 1 labels of the same count.

## L7a 2026-10-18 pass
Hypothesis. L6a showed that on exchangeable batches a rule frozen after 250 to 560 of the learner's own labels keeps 0.97 to 1.00 of unbounded carry_plain, and the post hoc horizon extrapolation says carry_plain's margin over the best fixed rule shrinks toward 1.00 to 1.03 at GRPO-length horizons, so claim 2 at those horizons rests on drift, which every earlier replay lacks by construction. With a proxy that changes mid-stream (judge swap, 3B table to 7B-AWQ table and the reverse; rollouts and agreement identical across the tables, judge_error labels correlate 0.511, judge_top mean 0.083 vs 0.570), a frozen rule should degrade after the switch while a persistent arm with a forgetting horizon should recover. Falsifiers: (i) freeze_plain_k6 stays within 1 percent of the best persistent arm over batches 7 to 12 (learn-then-freeze suffices), or (ii) every persistent arm falls below thr_agreement after the switch (persistence unsafe without a drift detector). Pre-registered rules: PASS = gate passes AND best persistent arm / freeze_plain_k6 >= 1.03 over batches 7 to 12 with the bootstrap CI lower bound above 1 in at least 3 of 4 cells AND the best persistent arm >= thr_agreement in batches 10 to 12 in those cells; KILL = (i) freeze_plain_k6 >= 0.99 x best persistent in at least 3 of 4 cells, or (ii) best persistent below thr_agreement in both batches 7 to 9 and 10 to 12 in at least 3 of 4 cells; VOID if the gate fails; otherwise inconclusive.

What was run. CPU only, no generation, no GPU spend (E04 stop rule untouched); launched through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L7a 'bash exp/L7a.sh' (gpu_status 15 MiB used), exit_code 0, about 10 min wall. Task GSM8K, 3,000 prompts, E04 feature tables of Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ (ten features, label judge_error, rows aligned across the two tables: pid and the six judge-independent features identical). Layout: permutation default_rng(10000 + order seed), 12 disjoint batches of m = 250, batches 1 to 6 from table A and 7 to 12 from table B (each prompt once), direction 1 = 3B then 7B-AWQ, direction 2 = 7B-AWQ then 3B, per-step B = 62 and 125 (25 and 50 percent), policy seed = order seed, 16 order seeds, modes drift, control_exact (L6a form on table A for all 12 batches, the regression gate) and control_z (per-batch-z form on table A for all 12 batches, the like-for-like no-drift reference); 192 jobs. exp/l7a_drift.py copies the L6a functions and adds carry_plain_window_{125,250,500}, carry_plain_decay (half-life 250 labels, sample weights in the logistic step only), freeze_plain_{k3,k6} and reset_at_switch (upper bound, never in the verdict). Selection as L6a: best persistent arm (carry_plain, the three windows, decay, carry_offset_lam1; freeze arms and reset_at_switch excluded) chosen per cell on order seeds 0 to 7 by batches 7 to 12 hits, reported on seeds 8 to 15, swapped fold and pooled 16 as cross-checks, percentile bootstrap over order seeds (10,000 resamples).

Headline (verdict fold, 8 order seeds; ratios of mean errors caught over batches 7 to 12; thr_agreement caught 47.7, 45.4, 46.2 errors per batch in batches 4-6, 7-9, 10-12 in d1 B=62, 86.0, 78.3, 78.8 in d1 B=125, 46.0, 47.8, 48.1 in d2 B=62, 80.9, 87.5, 86.4 in d2 B=125):

Verification by the loop (not taken from the run report). (1) Regression gate re-derived from the raw JSONs: control_exact vs runs/L6a on order seeds 0 to 7 for thr_agreement, restart_offset, carry_plain, carry_offset_lam1, batch_base_errors and freeze_plain_k3 vs L6a freeze_plain_125 at B=62, 2,112 per-batch values, 0 differences, 0 missing files; result.json reports the same, plus 0 in-job self-check failures (pre-switch drift vs control_z identical in batches 1 to 6, every arm and seed). (2) Verdict-fold headline recomputed from the 16 drift JSONs per cell with a different bootstrap seed (12345, 10,000 resamples): same best arm in all 4 cells (carry_plain, carry_plain_decay, carry_plain, carry_plain), best / freeze_plain_k6 1.038 (1.030 to 1.047), 1.078 (1.044 to 1.125), 1.039 (1.017 to 1.070), 1.006 (1.002 to 1.010), best / thr_agreement 1.151 / 1.151, 1.138 / 1.134, 1.077 / 1.070, 1.072 / 1.092, and the no-drift best / freeze_k6 of 1.022, 0.989, 1.049, 1.010; all equal the run's. (3) runs/L7a/verify.log (exp/l7a_verify.py): ALL CHECKS PASS, independent re-implementation of batch_base_errors, thr_agreement, carry_plain, window_250, decay, reset_at_switch and freeze_plain_k6 from the parquet tables reproduces all 12 per-batch hits on three (cell, seed) pairs (d1 B62 o3, d2 B125 o11, d1 B125 o9) after the verifier was changed to numpy's default argsort tie rule (an early fit on 3 labels puts 16 rows at probability exactly 1.0; not a bug in the run). I did not re-audit l7a_summarize.py line by line; I re-derived its outputs instead. (4) Premise check, post hoc, global-z logistic on all 3,000 rows, no policy: a rule fitted on one judge's labels transfers to the other judge's labels at AUC 0.868 (3B to 7B-AWQ) and 0.859 (7B-AWQ to 3B) against 0.882 and 0.871 in-sample, a loss of 0.012 to 0.014, and the ten-feature coefficients have the same signs (judge_top +, judge_mean -, judge_std -, n_distinct +) on both judges. This judge swap is therefore a mild drift for a standardized logistic: the 0.08 vs 0.57 judge_top mean shift is removed by the per-batch z, and what remains is a change in label noise rather than in the feature-to-label relation. For scale, negating the z of judge_top and judge_mean on the target table drops the transferred AUC to 0.489 (3B to 7B-AWQ) and 0.674 (7B-AWQ to 3B), and also negating judge_std and n_distinct to 0.176 and 0.168, so a sign-flip drift of that kind is a genuine relation change.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: PASS. The regression gate passed with 0 differences. The pass cell rule (best persistent / freeze_plain_k6 >= 1.03 with CI lower above 1, best >= thr_agreement in batches 10 to 12) holds in 3 of 4 cells in the verdict fold (d1 B=62, d1 B=125, d2 B=62; d2 B=125 misses at 1.006), in 3 of 4 in the swapped fold (1.038, 1.082, 1.056, 1.014) and in 3 of 4 pooled over 16 (1.038, 1.080, 1.048, 1.010), and best / thr_agreement in batches 10 to 12 is 1.070 to 1.151 in the three pass cells. KILL (i) (freeze_plain_k6 >= 0.99 x best persistent) fires in 1 of 4 cells (d2 B=125, freeze / best 0.994) in the verdict fold and pooled, 0 of 4 in the swapped fold, 3 needed; KILL (ii) fires in 0 of 4 cells in every fold (the lowest persistent-arm ratio to thr_agreement in batches 7 to 9 over 16 seeds is 1.018, d2 B=62). Neither falsifier holds, so the pre-registered pass stands, with a narrower reading than the spec's.

What the pass does and does not license. Licensed: (a) under a one-step judge swap, a persistent learner keeps a 7 to 15 percent edge over thr_agreement in both post-switch groups with no drift detector, in both directions and both budgets, so persistence is not unsafe at this drift size (kill (ii) 0 of 4); (b) continued refitting after batch 6 beats the rule frozen at batch 6 by 3.8 to 7.8 percent in 3 of 4 cells, but freeze_plain_k6 also beats thr_agreement in every post-switch group (1.027 to 1.117 in 7-9, 1.029 to 1.100 in 10-12), so a learn-then-freeze allocator is still ahead of the fixed rule and the cost of freezing is a few percent. Not licensed: the spec's reading "Phase 1 must keep fitting with a forgetting horizon, the best W is the spec". Unbounded carry_plain is the verdict-fold best persistent arm in 3 of 4 cells (best / carry_plain 1.000, 1.004, 1.000, 1.000); no window or decay arm beats it by the 3 percent the L6b-style rule would need; W=125 is at 0.964 to 1.033 of freeze_plain_k6 and loses to unbounded carry in every cell; reset_at_switch, which knows the switch, is below the best persistent arm in all 4 cells (0.907 to 0.985 of it in the verdict fold). The null case in the L7a spec fired: the drift is too mild to separate forgetting from carrying. Drift attribution is also partial: in the no-drift control the same best arm / freeze_plain_k6 is 1.022, 0.989, 1.049, 1.010 and only 1 of 4 cells passes the 1.03 line, versus 3 of 4 under drift, but the loop-level drift-specific excess table shows the freeze penalty is clearly drift-caused in only two cells: d1 B=125 (carry_plain 1.057, windows 1.074 to 1.095, CI lower ends above 1.03) and d2 B=62 (windows 1.025 to 1.047, reset_at_switch 1.084; carry_plain 1.022 with CI crossing 1), small in d1 B=62 (1.018) and absent in d2 B=125 (0.99 to 1.005). In d2 B=62 and d1 B=125 the forgetting arms recover more from the swap than carry_plain does, but they start from fewer labels and the sample-size cost outweighs the staleness gain, so unbounded carry stays best in absolute terms. The d2 pass at B=62 is therefore partly a label-count effect (freeze stops collecting at batch 6), not a staleness effect.

What it changes about the thesis claims. Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): support is extended from the stationary to a one-step-drift setting, and it is robust on the dimension that could have killed it: in all 4 cells and both post-switch groups, every persistent arm (min 1.018 to thr_agreement in batches 7 to 9) and even the frozen rule stays above thr_agreement. The Phase 1 allocator spec stays "carry the learner's own labels, optionally freeze after 2 to 9 batches", with a forgetting horizon NOT yet required and NOT yet ruled out: the drift tested is a change in label noise between two judges whose feature-to-label relation is nearly shared (transfer AUC loss 0.012 to 0.014), which is the drift most favourable to carrying stale labels. The E13 non-stationarity line gets its first number: a 372 to 750-label store from one judge, carried across a judge swap, retains 0.94 to 1.05 of its own no-drift ratio to thr_agreement (carry_plain thr-normalised, from the ledger table: 1.050, 1.023 in d1, 0.939, 0.952 in d2). carry_plain / thr_agreement shows no dip in d1 (batch 7: 1.109 and 1.124 vs batch 6: 1.097 and 1.115) and a level drop in d2 (batches 7-9: 1.073 and 1.075, 10-12: 1.086 and 1.090 vs batches 4-6: 1.107 and 1.135) that is flat across the post-switch batches, so it is not a staleness transient; it sits at the 3B table's own stationary level (carry_plain / thr_agreement over 12 batches on 3B in L6a: 1.068 and 1.089, bounded by static_out / thr_agreement 1.096 to 1.100). Direction is a confound (7B-AWQ labels are cleaner: error rate 0.438 vs 0.473; mean base errors per batch 118.2 on table A to 108.4 on B in d1, 110.4 to 118.3 in d2), so the directions are never pooled. Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): untouched by L7a, which uses rollout-level and judge-level features and no prompt embedding; the E01 and E04 kills on GSM8K stand. If anything, the cross-judge transfer reading is that the features carrying most of the edge (n_distinct and agreement plus the other judge-independent features reach AUC about 0.80 on their own and transfer unchanged) are rollout-group-level, not prompt-space.

Caveats. (1) A single step drift between two real judge tables; no gradual drift, no policy drift (the rollouts are fixed) and no relation flip, so nothing here says what a forgetting horizon is worth when the feature-to-label relation really changes. (2) The 16 order seeds permute the same 3,000 prompts, so the order-seed CIs are optimistic and each fold has 8 seeds; one population, offline replay on weak-judge labels, the 3B judge barely passed the E04 validity gate. (3) Direction confound as above. (4) Departures from the L7a spec: per-batch label-free z in drift and control_z (control_exact keeps the L6a form and is the gate), which itself absorbs mean and scale shifts and is the main reason the drift is mild; the gate also covers restart_offset and batch_base_errors (stricter than the spec); decay weights only the logistic step (StandardScaler unweighted); the summarizer adds the no-drift excess metric; selection on seeds 0 to 7 and report on 8 to 15 as in L6a (the Critic-accepted departure from the 8-seed spec). (5) The pre-registered pass rule does not separate drift from label count, which is why a PASS coexists with a drift-excess table that is positive in only 2 of 4 cells. (6) The "drift excess" table and the transfer-AUC check are loop-level post hoc analyses, not pre-registered.

Follow-ups (ranked by expected information value; full specs in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under Proposed by the loop, iteration 8).
1. L8a, sign-flip drift (CPU, about 10 min): the L7a layout with the z of judge_top and judge_mean negated on every post-switch row (labels unchanged), so the table-A rule becomes anti-informative (transfer AUC 0.49 to 0.67 by the diagnostic above) while thr_agreement is untouched. Arms as L7a plus a faster decay. Decides whether a forgetting horizon is ever needed and whether persistence without a detector is unsafe when the relation actually changes. Highest value: the only regime left on this machine in which kill (ii) can fire or a window can beat carry_plain by more than noise, and a +1 multiplier reproduces L7a exactly, so the regression gate is exact.
2. L8b, equal-label staleness test and gradual drift (CPU, about 10 min): fresh window of exactly the freeze set's size vs freeze_plain_k6 (same label count, only freshness differs), with the switch at batch 4, 6 or 8 and a gradual mixture over batches 4 to 9, in the cells where the excess table showed a staleness penalty (d1 B=125, d2 B=62). Medium value: isolates staleness from label count, which the L7a pass rule could not, and sets W.
3. L8c, drift-detector arm (CPU, about 10 min; run only if L8a shows unbounded carry_plain below thr_agreement or a window beating it): reset the label store when the realized hit rate of the last batch falls more than 2 binomial SE below the fit's own predicted precision. Medium to high value conditional on L8a, otherwise skip. L5c and L7c remain blocked on Rustom's explicit override of the E04 stop rule and are the only way to retire the one-population caveat.

**Critic note (L7a, adversarial review 2026-10-18).** The PASS verdict stands and so does every verdict count (verdict fold: PASS cells 3 of 4, kill (i) 1 of 4, kill (ii) 0 of 4; swapped fold 3, 0, 0; pooled 16 3, 1, 0; gate 0 differences). The corrections below are one inverted sentence and one mislabelled disclosure; none moves a pass or kill count. What I re-derived with separate code (exp/l7a_critic.py, l7a_critic_gate.py, l7a_critic_auc.py, l7a_critic_raw.py; bootstrap seeds 777 and 5, 10,000 resamples): all 192 JSONs exist and their budget, order_seed and mode fields match the file names, every arm has 12 values, all in-job self-checks are true, the stored feature list is the ten columns and the label is judge_error in all 192, and the 192 logs end in checks_all_true=True with no Traceback, warning or NaN; the gate is 176 vectors, 2,112 values, 0 differences against runs/L6a on order seeds 0 to 7 (no missing files), and drift vs control_z is identical in batches 1 to 6 for every arm and seed. Every number in the headline table, the arms-by-batch-group table (all 10 arm rows x 4 cells x 3 groups), the thr_agreement errors per batch, the swapped and pooled folds, the drift-excess table (all five rows to the third decimal, CI ends within 0.003; the text ranges "1.074 to 1.095" and "1.025 to 1.047" include carry_plain_window_125, which is computed but not in the table: 1.093 and 1.047) and the premise check (transfer AUC 0.868, 0.859 vs in-sample 0.882, 0.871; sign flips 0.489, 0.674 and 0.176, 0.168; label correlation 0.511; judge_top means 0.083 and 0.570) reproduces. Leakage: the allocator columns are agreement, n_distinct, mean_tokens, std_tokens, any_truncated, log_qlen and the four judge columns; the judge prompt contains only the question and the extracted number (p0_judge_score.py), pass_rate, majority_correct and proxy_error are not in the feature list, and gold enters only through judge_error = (judge_sel != gold), a label that joins a fit set only after the pick is paid. Budget accounting is equal: every arm verifies B prompts per batch, labels are counted as hits, and the freeze arms pick top B by the frozen score in batches 7 to 12.

Corrections.
1. "so unbounded carry stays best in absolute terms" (the sentence on d2 B=62 and d1 B=125 in "What the pass does and does not license") is not what the data show. Over batches 7 to 12 and all 16 order seeds carry_plain_decay is nominally ahead of carry_plain in exactly those two cells (d1 B=125 536.8 vs 531.7 hits, ratio 1.010; d2 B=62 311.5 vs 310.4, 1.003), and in d1 B=125 decay is the pre-registered verdict-fold best persistent arm in both folds (best / carry_plain 1.004 verdict fold, 1.015 swapped fold), which the entry's own headline table shows. The supportable statement is narrower: no window or decay arm beats unbounded carry_plain by more than about 1.5 percent on any 8-seed half in any cell (largest: decay / carry_plain 1.015 on seeds 0 to 7 of d1 B=125 and 1.012 on seeds 8 to 15 of d2 B=62; windows 0.942 to 1.005), far below the 3 percent that would make a forgetting horizon the spec, so the sample-size cost of forgetting roughly cancels the staleness gain. The count "carry_plain is the verdict-fold best persistent arm in 3 of 4 cells" and the null-case reading are unaffected.
2. Caveat (4) lists as "departures from the L7a spec" two items the L7a spec itself prescribes: per-batch label-free z with carried rows keeping their z, and 16 order seeds with selection on 0 to 7, report on 8 to 15, swapped fold and pooled 16. The "Critic-accepted departure from the 8-seed spec" was the L6a spec. The genuine additions are the stricter gate keys, decay weights in the logistic step only (the spec says exponential weights, half-life 250 labels, without saying where), the control_z mode, the excess table and the fit-set diagnostics. I found no undisclosed departure.
3. Robustness remarks, not corrections. (a) The pass hinges on two thin cells: d1 B=62 (1.038) and d2 B=62 (1.039) clear the 1.03 line by 0.8 and 0.9 points and a third pass cell is needed, so one miss would make the verdict INCONCLUSIVE; the ratio is stable across order seeds (d1 B=62 is 1.038 in all three folds, d2 B=62 is 1.039, 1.056, 1.048), and a paired batch-level bootstrap over the 48 seed-batch pairs of the verdict fold, which adds batch-composition noise that the order-seed CI lacks, gives CIs of 1.027 to 1.049 and 1.022 to 1.058 (P(ratio > 1) = 1.000 in both, P(ratio >= 1.03) = 0.91 and 0.83), so the rule's CI-above-1 test is robust and the 1.03 point test is not tight. d1 B=125 is clear (batch-level CI 1.059 to 1.100). (b) Raw-feature transfer AUC without per-batch z is 0.826 (3B to 7B-AWQ) and 0.849 (7B-AWQ to 3B) against 0.868 and 0.859 with it, so the per-batch z does remove a good part of the judge-swap drift (judge-feature SDs differ by 2 to 3 times between the two judges), which supports the entry's caveat 4; it was not run through the policy replay. (c) Drift attribution of the pass, using the entry's verdict-fold drift excess for the pre-registered best arm (1.015, 1.090, 0.990, 0.996): only d1 B=125 shows a clear drift-specific edge for that arm, and d2 B=62 passes with an excess of 0.990 (its no-drift control_z ratio is 1.049, above the drift ratio 1.039), so the pass in that cell is a label-count effect, as the entry's own reading already says.

Corrected verdict: PASS (unchanged). Regression gate 0 differences in 2,112 values (re-verified); best persistent / freeze_plain_k6 over batches 7 to 12 is 1.038 (1.030 to 1.047), 1.078 (1.045 to 1.126), 1.039 (1.017 to 1.070), 1.006 (1.002 to 1.010), so the pass rule holds in 3 of 4 cells and kill (i) in 1 of 4 (3 needed), kill (ii) in 0 of 4. Corrected reading: under a one-step judge swap with per-batch standardization, continued refitting after batch 6 beats learn-then-freeze by 3.8 to 7.8 percent in 3 of 4 cells, but only d1 B=125 shows a drift-specific gain for the best arm; persistence without a detector is safe at this drift size (7 to 15 percent over thr_agreement after the switch); no forgetting arm beats unbounded carry_plain by more than about 1.5 percent in any cell, so the spec's reading "keep fitting with a forgetting horizon, the best W is the spec" is not supported and a harsher drift (L8a) is still required before choosing a horizon.

## L7b 2026-10-18 pass (a one-shot designed calibration set does not reproduce the learner's edge; claim 2 survives the last fixed-rule family tried at a 12-batch horizon)
Hypothesis. L6a showed that the persistent learner's edge over every pre-registered fixed rule comes from which labels are in hand, not how many: freeze_plain_n (the learner's own first n labels, then frozen) keeps 0.988 to 1.001 of carry_plain while seeded, random and n_distinct > 1 calibration sets reach at most 0.945. The stated mechanism (the greedy stream is not range-restricted and sits near the decision boundary) was never isolated, and no fixed-rule family so far was a designed, non-adaptive calibration set. Falsifier (kills claim 2): a one-shot designed covering set, stratified over the agreement range or centred on the thr_agreement cutoff, reaches >= 0.99 x carry_plain in at least 3 of 4 cells; the Phase 1 recipe would then be that fixed rule, not an online learner.

What was run. CPU only, no generation, no GPU spend (E04 stop rule untouched); launched through bin/gpu_run.sh avrl/runs/L7b 'bash exp/L7b.sh' (gpu_status 15 MiB used), exit_code 0, 38 s wall (64 jobs at about 12 s each, xargs -P 24, then the summary). Layout identical to L6a: GSM8K, 3,000 prompts, E04 feature tables of Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ (ten features), label judge_error, 12 disjoint batches of m = 250 from default_rng(10000 + order seed), 16 order seeds, per-step B = 62 and 125, policy seed = order seed, 2 judges x 2 B x 16 = 64 jobs, cells never pooled. Scripts: exp/l7b_design.py (copy of l6a_amort.py, L6a arms verbatim; amortized() gains the pick kinds strat and bnd and every fit set records coverage statistics), exp/l7b_summarize.py, exp/L7b.sh, exp/l7b_verify.py. Results: runs/L7b/ (design_*.json x 64, result.json, ledger_table.md, summary.log, verify.log).
Six new arms, fixed in advance and never chosen on held-out seeds, each at n = 125, 250, 500 with the amort_rand_n calibration structure (k = ceil(n / B); batches 1..k-1 are pure calibration, the last n - (k-1)B calibration labels fall in batch k, ONE StandardScaler + balanced logistic fit on exactly n labels, reused free as top-B by score in batches k+1..12, calibration labels charged and counted as hits, one-class fit set falls back to thr_agreement): amort_strat_n takes equal numbers of picks from each of 10 quantile bins of the batch's label-free agreement; amort_bnd_n takes the contiguous lowest-agreement rank window of the needed size centred on the thr_agreement cutoff rank B (full batch: ranks 31 to 92 at B = 62, 63 to 187 at B = 125, i.e. B/2 to 3B/2 rounded). Two implementation choices that the spec left open, made before the first run: (a) agreement takes only 8 distinct values, so the 10 bins are the equal-size rank tenths (25 prompts each) of the thr_agreement order, ties broken by the policy-seeded stream, never by label, and the take % 10 remainder goes to rng-drawn bins; (b) the last calibration batch gets the smaller window centred on rank B. Selection exactly as L6a: best designed arm per cell on order seeds 0 to 7 by 12-batch hits, reported on 8 to 15 (verdict fold), swapped fold and pooled 16 as cross-checks, percentile bootstrap over order seeds (10,000 resamples).

Headline (verdict fold, 8 order seeds, all 12 batches, calibration charged; all-16-seed mean errors caught per 12 batches: carry_plain 614.2, 1129.9, 613.6, 1080.1 and thr_agreement 575.2, 1037.4, 551.9, 951.4 in the four cells in table order):

| cell | best designed arm (chosen on 0-7) | carry_plain / best (95% CI) | best / carry_plain | best / thr_agreement | swapped fold | pooled 16 | pass cell | kill cell |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3B B=62 | amort_strat_125 | 1.113 (1.101 to 1.123) | 0.898 | 0.958 | 1.110 | 1.111 | yes | no |
| 3B B=125 | amort_bnd_125 | 1.078 (1.057 to 1.095) | 0.928 | 1.010 | 1.079 | 1.079 | yes | no |
| 7B-AWQ B=62 | amort_strat_125 | 1.136 (1.105 to 1.167) | 0.880 | 0.974 | 1.132 | 1.134 | yes | no |
| 7B-AWQ B=125 | amort_strat_125 | 1.067 (1.054 to 1.081) | 0.937 | 1.064 | 1.071 | 1.069 | yes | no |

For comparison, the L6a best fixed arm (verdict fold) was 0.936, 0.943, 0.885, 0.933 of carry_plain in the same four cells: the designed family is no stronger than the L6a family (lower in three cells, 0.004 higher in 7B-AWQ B=125).

All 16 order seeds, 12-batch total / carry_plain (post-calibration ratio, batches k+1..12 with k = ceil(n / B), in brackets). The 95% CIs of the 12-batch ratios are within +-0.022 for every row except amort_bnd_125 and amort_bnd_250 (widest 0.737 to 0.878 for amort_bnd_125 at 3B B=62, an unstable arm across order seeds); the bracketed post-calibration ratios have CIs up to +-0.09 for amort_bnd and about +-0.025 or less for the other rows (full CIs in ledger_table.md):

Deficit decomposition (carry_plain minus the arm, as a share of carry_plain's 12-batch total, calibration batches 1..k / batches k+1..12, all 16 seeds; positive = arm behind). strat_125: 6.0/4.0, 2.5/5.4, 6.1/5.7, 2.9/3.6; strat_500: 27.4/0.9, 11.6/2.1, 30.3/0.9, 12.8/-0.3; bnd_125: 3.5/15.2, 2.1/4.7, 3.3/11.3, 2.6/6.2; bnd_500: 12.4/3.4, 9.7/1.5, 15.7/2.7, 11.7/-0.2 (cells in the table's order). strat_125 splits its loss about evenly between the two periods, bnd_125 loses mostly after calibration (4.7 to 15.2 percent against 2.1 to 3.5), and the n = 500 sets lose mostly while calibrating (labels paid near the base rate; 27.4 and 30.3 percent of the total for strat_500 at B = 62).

Fit-set coverage, n = 125, mean over 16 order seeds (global label-free z-agreement, population range -2.10 to 1.28, SD 1.00, n_distinct > 1 share 0.814; population error rate 0.473 for 3B, 0.438 for 7B-AWQ). freeze_plain_125 has 186 labels at B = 62 (the k*B labels in hand) and 125 at B = 125. At n = 500 strat is unchanged, bnd's range widens to 1.45 (B = 62) and 1.54 (B = 125) with SD 0.34 to 0.51, and freeze's SD rises by up to 0.08 and its error rate by up to 0.05:

| 3B B=125 | strat | -2.10 to 1.28 (3.38) | 1.00 | 0.452 | 0.818 |

Verification by the loop (exp/l7b_verify.py, separate code from the run and the summarizer; log in runs/L7b/verify.log). (1) Regression gate re-derived from the raw JSONs over ALL 19 L6a arms plus batch_base_errors, 64 file pairs: 1,280 vectors, 15,360 per-batch values, 0 differences (the run's own gate covers thr_agreement, carry_plain, amort_seed/rand/split_n, freeze_plain_n and batch_base_errors: 960 vectors, 11,520 values, 0 differences; the 5 other L6a reference arms, 320 vectors, 0 differences; 0 in-job self-check failures). (2) Verdict-fold headline recomputed with a different bootstrap seed (7): same best arm in all 4 cells, same ratios, CI lower ends 1.101, 1.057, 1.105, 1.054; carry_plain beats the chosen designed arm on 8 of 8 verdict-fold order seeds in every cell (per-seed carry_plain / best over all 16 seeds for the arm chosen on seeds 0 to 7, minimum 1.085, 0.995, 1.078, 1.037; the 0.995 is one 3B B=125 seed). (3) amort_strat_n and amort_bnd_n re-implemented from the spec in separate code (own pick logic, own fit and top-B loop) and compared with the job JSONs on 4 order seeds (0, 3, 9, 15) x 2 judges x 2 B x 6 arms: 96 vectors, 0 differences. (4) A development run of the same 64 jobs under bin/gpu_run.sh (deleted afterwards) produced an identical result.json (parsed equality), so the run is deterministic.

Post hoc diagnostics (loop-level, in verify.log, not in the verdict). (a) Rule fitted on the n labels of a design and scored on the rest of the 3,000 prompts, mean over 16 order seeds: held-out AUC 0.82 to 0.87 for strat and 0.70 to 0.87 for bnd (bnd lowest at B = 62, 0.70 to 0.80), and the standardized agreement coefficient is -0.18 to -0.42 for strat but -0.07 to +0.14 for bnd, i.e. the boundary window leaves agreement uninformative to the fit. (b) Top-B picks of the frozen n = 125 rule in its post-calibration batches: hit rate 0.797 (strat) vs 0.675 (bnd) at 3B B=62 and 0.784 vs 0.722 at 7B-AWQ B=62, with 19 percent and 9 percent of bnd picks at agreement >= 0.875 against 0.8 and 0.7 percent for strat (population share 36.6 percent); at B = 125 the bnd window is wide enough that bnd_125 matches strat_125 (hit rate 0.722 vs 0.716 at 3B, 0.675 vs 0.695 at 7B-AWQ). A fit set that spans only the middle of the agreement range lets the frozen rule extrapolate to high-agreement prompts it never saw, which is consistent with the range-restriction mechanism for bnd.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: PASS. The gate passed with 0 differences. carry_plain / best designed arm is 1.113, 1.078, 1.136, 1.067 with bootstrap CI lower bounds 1.101, 1.057, 1.105, 1.054, so the pass rule (>= 1.03 with CI lower above 1) holds in 4 of 4 cells (3 needed); the swapped fold (1.110, 1.079, 1.132, 1.071) and pooled 16 (1.111, 1.079, 1.134, 1.069) also pass 4 of 4. KILL (best designed >= 0.99 x carry_plain) holds in 0 of 4 cells in all three folds; the best designed arm is 0.880 to 0.937 of carry_plain and the chosen arms' post-calibration ratios are 0.924 to 0.966, so the sub-clause "calibration cost only" (post-calibration ratio >= 0.99 in 4 of 4 cells) is not triggered (0 of 4).

What the pass does and does not license. Licensed: at a 12-batch horizon, with calibration labels charged, no one-shot designed calibration set that covers the agreement range (amort_strat_n, an equal-population stratification) or is centred on the thr_agreement cutoff (amort_bnd_n) at n = 125, 250 or 500 gets within 6.3 percent of the persistent learner, and the designed sets do not reliably beat plain thr_agreement either (best / thr_agreement 0.958 and 0.974 at B = 62, 1.010 and 1.064 at B = 125). The edge is therefore not available to coverage alone: freeze_plain_n spans the full agreement range like strat (range 3.2 to 3.4 against 3.4) but is concentrated (SD 0.66 to 0.91 against 1.00) and error-rich (label error rate 0.68 to 0.82 against 0.43 to 0.48), while bnd is boundary-focused but range-restricted (range 1.2 to 1.5, SD 0.32 to 0.51). So the L6a statement that the learner's stream is "not range-restricted" holds for its min-to-max coverage, but what separates it from the designed sets is the combination (full range, concentrated near low agreement, high share of errors), which no label-free design here reproduces. Not licensed: (i) a statement about long horizons. The steady-state gap shrinks with n: strat_500 reaches 0.964, 0.969, 0.966 and 1.004 of carry_plain after calibration (bnd_500 0.870, 0.978, 0.897, 1.002) in the four cells, and the remaining deficit of the n = 500 sets sits in the calibration batches (9.7 to 30.3 percent of the 12-batch total). A post hoc extrapolation that holds each arm's post-calibration mean hits per batch flat (verify.log item 6; not a measurement) puts strat_500 / carry_plain at 0.854 to 0.951 at 29 batches, 0.913 to 0.978 at 60 and 0.940 to 0.991 at 120, so only 7B-AWQ B=125 approaches 0.99 and only at a very long horizon; L7c, not this run, decides it. (ii) A statement about designs outside this family (equal-width range stratification, one-shot uncertainty sampling, stratification on features other than agreement).

What it changes about the thesis claims. Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget) loses its last unexplored fixed-rule family at the 12-batch horizon: the learner is 6.7 to 13.6 percent ahead of the best designed arm in 4 of 4 cells, comparable to its 6.0 to 12.9 percent lead over the L6a family. The kill reading "calibrate once on a covering set" is rejected here, and the Phase 1 allocator stays online at this horizon. The gain that remains to be explained is the calibration period: the learner earns hits while it learns because its picks are greedy, whereas every one-shot design pays its first k batches near the base rate; whether that cost is worth paying for a cheaper steady state is a horizon question, not a coverage question.

Caveats. (1) No drift by construction (most favourable to a once-fitted rule); the 16 order seeds permute the same 3,000 prompts, so CIs are optimistic and each fold has 8 seeds. (2) amort_strat_n is an equal-population stratification, so it is uniform sampling with lower coverage variance and coincides with amort_rand_n to within 0.006 of carry_plain in every cell (0.900 vs 0.898, 0.921 vs 0.924, 0.882 vs 0.888, 0.935 vs 0.936 at n = 125); a design that oversamples sparse agreement levels or both tails was not run, and the "stratified over the agreement range" family is therefore only tested in its proportional form. (3) freeze_plain_n has k*B >= n labels (186 / 310 / 558 at B = 62) against exactly n for the designed arms, which flatters the freeze comparison at B = 62 by up to 12 percent more labels (the L6a Critic's point about confounding applies unchanged). (4) amort_bnd_125 at B = 62 is unstable across order seeds (3B CI 0.737 to 0.878) and was not investigated per seed. (5) The mechanism diagnostics are post hoc and descriptive. (6) One population, one task (GSM8K judge_error labels from E04), offline replay on weak-judge labels; the 3B judge barely passed the E04 validity gate.

Follow-ups (ranked by expected information value; L7c, L8a, L8b, L8c as queued in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md stay unchanged).
1. L7c, long-horizon replication on fresh prompts (GPU, still BLOCKED on Rustom's explicit override of the E04 stop rule): the post hoc extrapolation above reaches 0.99 for a one-shot n = 500 design only in 7B-AWQ B=125 and only near 120 batches (0.94 to 0.96 in the other three cells), so the horizon is the one place where a one-shot designed set could still reach 0.99 of carry_plain, and the 12-batch PASS does not measure it. Medium to high value, nonzero GPU spend.
2. L8a, sign-flip drift (CPU, about 10 min), unchanged: the only CPU test left that can separate a persistent learner from a one-shot rule.
3. Low value, only if cheap: a one-shot design that oversamples both agreement tails (equal-width bins) and a two-stage fixed set (random half, then top-uncertainty half, frozen once), to close the two untested designed families named in caveat 2 and the not-licensed item (ii).

## L7b 2026-10-18 pass
(Loop-level entry written after re-deriving the headline from the 64 raw JSONs in runs/L7b and the 64 matching runs/L6a files. The run-level entry above, line 658, carries the long audit text, the deficit decomposition and the coverage table. Section "New finding" below is not in the run-level entry and narrows one of its mechanism statements.)

Hypothesis. L6a's persistent learner (carry_plain) beat every pre-registered fixed rule (seeded, random and n_distinct > 1 calibration sets at n = 125, 250, 500, calibration charged and counted as hits) by 6 to 13 percent over 12 batches, but no fixed rule was a designed, non-adaptive calibration set. Falsifier (kills claim 2): a one-shot designed set, stratified over the agreement range (amort_strat_n) or centred on the thr_agreement cutoff (amort_bnd_n), reaches >= 0.99 x carry_plain on the verdict fold in at least 3 of 4 cells, which would make the Phase 1 recipe "calibrate once on a designed set" instead of an online learner.

What was run. CPU only, no generation, no GPU spend (E04 stop rule untouched); launched with bin/gpu_run.sh avrl/runs/L7b 'bash exp/L7b.sh' (gpu_status 15 MiB used), exit_code 0, 38 s wall. Task GSM8K, 3,000 prompts, label judge_error from the E04 feature tables of Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ, ten cheap + judge features, 12 disjoint batches of m = 250 from default_rng(10000 + order seed), per-step budget B = 62 and 125 (25 and 50 percent), policy seed = order seed, order seeds 0 to 15, 64 jobs (2 judges x 2 B x 16 seeds), cells never pooled. Six new pre-registered arms amort_{strat,bnd}_{125,250,500} with the amort_rand_n calibration structure (k = ceil(n / B), one StandardScaler + balanced logistic fit on exactly n labels, reused free as top-B in batches k+1..12, one-class fit falls back to thr_agreement); strat = equal numbers from each of 10 equal-population rank bins of the batch's label-free agreement, bnd = contiguous lowest-agreement rank window centred on the thr_agreement cutoff. All L6a arms kept verbatim as references. Selection as L6a: best of the 6 designed arms per cell chosen on order seeds 0 to 7, reported on 8 to 15 (verdict fold), swapped fold and pooled 16 as cross-checks, percentile bootstrap over order seeds (10,000 resamples).

Numbers (all 16 order seeds, cumulative errors caught over 12 batches, calibration charged; each cell is ratio to carry_plain / ratio to thr_agreement; absolute means in the first two rows):

Verification by the loop (recomputed from the raw JSONs in this session with separate inline code, bootstrap seed 12345; not taken from the run report). (1) Regression gate: thr_agreement, carry_plain, amort_seed/rand/split_{125,250,500}, freeze_plain_{125,250,500} and batch_base_errors against runs/L6a on order seeds 0 to 15 in all 4 cells: 11,520 per-batch values, 0 differences, 0 missing files. (2) All 64 files have 12 values per arm and every value between 0 and B. (3) Verdict-fold best arms (amort_strat_125, amort_bnd_125, amort_strat_125, amort_strat_125), ratios, bootstrap CIs (lower ends 1.101, 1.057, 1.106, 1.054), best / carry_plain, best / thr_agreement, post-calibration ratios, swapped-fold best arms (amort_strat_125, amort_bnd_250, amort_strat_125, amort_strat_125) and ratios all match result.json to within 0.001. (4) The verdict-fold arm chosen on seeds 0 to 7 is below carry_plain on 16 of 16, 15 of 16, 16 of 16 and 16 of 16 order seeds, and even the per-seed oracle best of the six designed arms reaches carry_plain in only 2 of 64 (cell, seed) pairs. (5) The post hoc best arm over the union of the L6a and L7b families on all 16 seeds is still only 0.939, 0.945, 0.897, 0.936 of carry_plain (amort_seed_250, amort_seed_250, amort_split_125, amort_rand_125), so the designed family adds nothing to the strongest fixed baseline.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: PASS. The gate passed with 0 differences. carry_plain / best designed arm is 1.113, 1.078, 1.136, 1.067 with CI lower ends 1.101, 1.057, 1.106, 1.054, so the pass rule (>= 1.03 with CI lower above 1) holds in 4 of 4 cells (3 needed) and in 4 of 4 on the swapped fold and pooled 16. KILL (best designed >= 0.99 x carry_plain) holds in 0 of 4 cells in every fold; the best designed arm is 0.880 to 0.937 of carry_plain. The "calibration cost only" sub-clause (post-calibration ratio >= 0.99 in 4 of 4 cells) fired in 0 of 4; the best post-calibration ratio of any n = 500 design is 0.964, 0.978, 0.966, 1.004.

New finding (equal-label post-calibration comparison, not in the run-level entry). At B = 125 the freeze arm holds exactly n labels, so designed arms and freeze_plain_n can be compared like for like on batches k+1..12 (pooled 16 seeds, post-calibration hits ratio to freeze_plain_n): amort_strat_n is 0.998, 0.993, 0.987 (3B, n = 125, 250, 500) and 1.017, 1.018, 1.003 (7B-AWQ); amort_bnd_n is 1.007, 1.007, 0.995 and 0.987, 0.996, 1.001. All twelve values lie in 0.987 to 1.018. So once calibration is paid, a frozen rule fitted on a stratified random or boundary-window label set is as good as a frozen rule fitted on the learner's own error-rich stream (error rate 0.68 to 0.78 against 0.43 to 0.47); the label error rate and concentration of the fit set do not matter beyond about 2 percent. At B = 62, where freeze has 186, 310, 558 labels, strat is 0.968 to 0.983 of freeze and the shortfall is the label-count gap; only bnd (0.83 to 0.92) and amort_seed (down to 0.46) are worse, which is the range-restriction failure again. Consequences: (a) the run-level statement that what separates the learner from the designed sets is "the combination (full range, concentrated near low agreement, high share of errors)" is not supported; the data say the fit set composition does not drive post-calibration quality, in line with the L6a Critic's correction 1. (b) The learner's edge over a one-shot designed set at 12 batches is two things only: it earns hits while it collects labels (strat_125 catches 0.58, 0.45, 0.56, 0.44 of B in the calibration batches against 0.78, 0.68, 0.76, 0.68 for carry_plain, which is the 2.5 to 30 percent of the 12-batch total that the strat arms lose in calibration, rising with n) and it keeps refitting past n labels (after calibration carry_plain is 4 to 7 percent ahead of strat_125 and 4 to 6 percent ahead of freeze_plain_125, 1 to 5 percent ahead of strat_250 and freeze_plain_250, and 0 to 4 percent ahead of strat_500 and freeze_plain_500). (c) The 12-batch kill rule has little power against a design that does not earn while calibrating: at B = 62, n = 125 already costs about 6 percent of the 12-batch total in calibration alone, so the 0.99 bar was unreachable by construction for random-like picks. The post-calibration ratio is the informative quantity, and it says the gap is small and shrinking in n, which is a horizon question.

What it changes about the thesis claims. Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget) survives the last unexplored fixed-rule family on this machine, on one stationary offline replay at a 12-batch horizon with calibration charged: the learner is 6.7 to 13.6 percent ahead of the best designed arm in 4 of 4 cells. The mechanism is narrower than "better labels": earn-while-labelling plus continued refitting, both of which a longer stationary stream dilutes. So the surviving content of claim 2 is "online allocation beats calibrate-once at horizons short relative to the calibration cost", and what holds it up at GRPO horizons is drift, not label quality (L7a gave a narrow PASS on a mild judge swap, L8a is the harsher test) and the horizon itself (L9a below). The Phase 1 allocator stays online (carry_plain, or learn-then-freeze from about 500 labels) with thr_agreement as the committed fallback. Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space) is untouched: L7b uses ten rollout and judge statistics and no prompt-space features, and E01 and E04 stay KILL for it on GSM8K (group-level majority-vote and answer-only judge proxies, 3B rung valid).

Caveats. No drift by construction (most favourable to a once-fitted rule); the 16 order seeds permute the same 3,000 prompts, so CIs are optimistic and each fold has 8 seeds; one population, offline replay on weak-judge labels, the 3B judge barely passed the E04 validity gate; amort_strat_n uses equal-population bins, so it is random sampling with lower coverage variance and tracks amort_rand_n within 0.006 of carry_plain in every cell (the proportional form of "stratified" is the only one tested; equal-width or tail-oversampled bins were not); freeze_plain_n has up to 12 percent more labels than a designed arm at B = 62; amort_bnd_125 at B = 62 is unstable across order seeds (3B CI 0.737 to 0.878); the equal-label comparison above is post hoc and descriptive, and the horizon extrapolations in the run-level entry are not measurements.

Follow-ups (ranked by expected information value; full specs in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under Proposed by the loop, iteration 9).
1. L9a, CPU long-horizon replay (about 10 min, no GPU, no E04 override needed): stationary iid-with-replacement streams of 60 batches drawn from the same 3,000-prompt table, same arms plus n = 1000, to measure the shrinkage that the L6a and L7b extrapolations only predict (strat_500 / carry_plain 0.94 to 0.99 at 120 batches). High value: the equal-label result makes the horizon the only remaining way a designed set can reach 0.99, and the answer sets the Phase 1 recipe (persistent versus learn-then-freeze at what n). L7c (GPU) would replace it with fresh prompts if Rustom overrides the E04 stop rule.
2. L8a, sign-flip drift (CPU, about 10 min), unchanged and still queued: the only CPU test that can separate a persistent learner from a one-shot rule once horizon is controlled. High value, run after or alongside L9a.
3. L9b, earn-while-calibrating designed rule (CPU, about 10 min): a mixed calibration design (greedy lowest-agreement picks plus a stratified exploration share, fixed in advance), the one non-adaptive family that keeps calibration earnings. Predicted (from the numbers above) to land at 0.93 to 0.95 of carry_plain, i.e. another PASS; low to medium value, mainly to retire the "designed set" line for good. L9c (prompt-population bootstrap of the verdict counts) is queued behind it.

**Critic note (L7b, adversarial review 2026-10-18; covers both L7b entries above).** The PASS verdict stands and so does every verdict count (verdict fold: PASS cells 4 of 4, KILL cells 0 of 4, post-calibration best / carry_plain >= 0.99 in 0 of 4; swapped fold 4, 0, 0; pooled 16 4, 0, 0; gate 0 differences). The corrections below are one wrong label-count figure, one off-by-one range, one scope statement that is stronger than the entry's own caveats, and two nits; none moves a pass or kill count. What I re-derived with separate code (exp/l7b_critic.py and l7b_critic2.py; bootstrap seed 999, 10,000 resamples): all 64 design_*.json exist with budget, order_seed and policy_seed matching the file names, 12 values for every arm, every value between 0 and B, 0 failed in-job self-checks, the 64 logs are one line each ending checks_all_true=True with no Traceback or warning, exit_code 0, run.log empty, and cmd.sh 22:40:27 to done.flag 22:41:05 matches the 38 s. Gate: 1,280 vectors and 15,360 per-batch values against runs/L6a (all 19 L6a arms plus batch_base_errors), 0 differences. Verdict fold: best arms (amort_strat_125, amort_bnd_125, amort_strat_125, amort_strat_125), carry_plain / best 1.113, 1.078, 1.136, 1.067 with my CI lower ends 1.101, 1.056, 1.106, 1.055 (result.json 1.101, 1.057, 1.105, 1.054, so the two entries' CI ends differing by 0.001 is bootstrap-seed noise, not an error), best / thr_agreement 0.958, 1.010, 0.974, 1.064, post-calibration ratios 0.952, 0.944, 0.924, 0.966, and result.json carries the same counts in all three folds. Even the oracle arm on the verdict fold (best of the six designed arms on seeds 8 to 15 itself) gives the same ratios in three cells and 1.074 (amort_bnd_250) in 3B B=125. I also recomputed every cell of the 10-row all-16-seed table (12-batch / carry_plain, post-calibration / carry_plain, / thr_agreement) plus the L6a reference rows, the 16-value deficit decomposition, the 12-row coverage table and the n = 500 coverage sentence, carry_plain and thr_agreement absolute means, strat_n versus rand_n (within 0.006 of carry_plain in all 12 (cell, n) pairs), the calibration-period hit rates (strat_125 0.579, 0.452, 0.558, 0.435 of B against carry_plain 0.778, 0.681, 0.760, 0.684), the twelve equal-label post-calibration ratios at B = 125 (0.987 to 1.018) and the B = 62 ranges (strat 0.968 to 0.983, bnd 0.83 to 0.92, amort_seed_500 down to 0.457), the post hoc best over the L6a and L7b families (0.939, 0.945, 0.897, 0.936), the per-seed oracle (2 of 64) and the chosen arm's below-carry counts (16, 15, 16, 16; the one 0.995 is 3B B=125 order seed 5, a selection-fold seed, so the verdict fold is 8 of 8 in every cell): all match the entries to within 0.001 except the items below. The verify.log diagnostics (held-out AUC, agreement coefficients, top-B hit rates, extrapolation table) match the ledger text. Determinism: I re-ran exp/l7b_design.py on 7B-AWQ B=125 order 9 and 3B B=62 order 5; arms, arm_info and all checks are identical to the stored JSONs. No leakage: the allocator columns are the ten cheap and judge columns; gold, majority_correct, proxy_error and pass_rate are in the feature table but not in the column list; the judge prompt is the question and the proposed final number only (exp/p0_judge_score.py: gold is merged in for a pair-level sanity AUC and never enters the prompt); gold enters only through judge_error = (judge_sel != gold); amort_strat_n and amort_bnd_n pick by label-free agreement rank with a seeded tie-break, never by label, and a label joins a fit set only after it is paid. Budget accounting is equal: every designed arm verifies exactly B prompts per batch (in batch k the n - (k-1) B calibration picks plus B minus that many ranked picks, 1 calibration label for n = 125 at B = 62), calibration labels count as hits, and carry_plain and thr_agreement verify B per batch.

Corrections.
1. "freeze_plain_n has up to 12 percent more labels than a designed arm at B = 62" (run-level caveat 3, loop-level caveats, and the notes field of result.json) is wrong. freeze_plain_n holds 186 / 310 / 558 labels at B = 62 against 125 / 250 / 500, i.e. 49 / 24 / 12 percent more; 12 percent is only the n = 500 case. The comparison to the freeze arm at B = 62 (and the "shortfall is the label-count gap" reading of the 0.968 to 0.983 strat / freeze ratios) is flattered by up to 49 percent more labels at n = 125, not 12. The verdict is untouched because freeze_plain_n is a diagnostic and never enters the selection, and the B = 125 comparisons hold exactly n labels.
2. Loop-level "New finding" (b): "after calibration carry_plain is 4 to 7 percent ahead of strat_125" should read 4 to 8 percent (carry_plain / strat_125 over batches k+1..12 is 1.055, 1.062, 1.080, 1.041; the 8.0 is 7B-AWQ B=62, from the entry's own post-calibration column 0.926). The other ranges in that sentence (4 to 6 percent for freeze_plain_125, 1 to 5 for the n = 250 pair, 0 to 4 for the n = 500 pair) reproduce.
3. Scope. The "What it changes" paragraphs say claim 2 "loses its last unexplored fixed-rule family" (run-level) and "survives the last unexplored fixed-rule family" (loop-level), and the PASS reading is "beats any static covering design". The entries' own caveat 2, not-licensed item (ii) and follow-up 3 say that equal-width or tail-oversampled stratification, one-shot uncertainty sampling and two-stage designs were not run, and amort_strat_n is proportional sampling (it matches amort_rand_n within 0.006 in every cell and n, so it adds no coverage information beyond L6a). What was actually tested is the pre-registered family: proportional 10-bin stratification, and a boundary rank window that fits a narrow slice of the agreement range and then extrapolates (agreement coefficient about zero, held-out AUC 0.70 to 0.87). That window arm is also erratic: for 3B B=62 amort_bnd_125 totals range from 270 (order seed 0, below uniform's 348) to 592 (seed 10, equal to carry_plain), 13 of 16 seeds are below thr_agreement, and nobody inspected it per seed (entry caveat 4). Corrected reading: PASS means the learner beats the two pre-registered designed calibration families at a 12-batch horizon with calibration charged; it does not rule out a covering design that oversamples sparse agreement levels or both tails, an uncertainty-sampled set, or a long horizon (L9a, L9b), so "last unexplored family" should read "the pre-registered designed family".
4. Nits. (a) The run-level statement that 12-batch CIs are within +-0.022 for every row except amort_bnd_125 and amort_bnd_250 misses amort_bnd_500 at 7B-AWQ B=62 (-0.025 / +0.021), and the post-calibration CIs for amort_bnd reach +-0.096 (bnd_125, 7B-AWQ B=62), not +-0.09. (b) The loop-level headline table prints CI ends from bootstrap seed 12345 (1.106, 1.168; 1.054, 1.082) while result.json and the run-level table print seed-0 ends (1.105, 1.167; 1.054, 1.081); both are valid draws and neither changes a pass cell.

Robustness remark (not a correction). The margin over the 1.03 line does not depend on the order-seed bootstrap alone: a batch-level bootstrap over the 96 seed-batch values of the verdict fold gives carry_plain / best CIs 1.083 to 1.148, 1.062 to 1.095, 1.103 to 1.172, 1.045 to 1.093, all above 1.03. It still cannot see prompt-sampling variance, because all 16 order seeds permute the same 3,000 prompts; L9c is the test for that, and the entry's optimistic-CI caveat stands.

Corrected verdict: PASS (unchanged). Gate 0 differences; carry_plain / best designed arm 1.113, 1.078, 1.136, 1.067 with CI lower ends above 1.054, so the pass rule holds in 4 of 4 cells (3 needed) and the kill rule in 0 of 4. Corrected reading: at a 12-batch horizon with calibration charged, the persistent learner is 6.7 to 13.6 percent ahead of the best of the two pre-registered designed calibration families in every cell; the Phase 1 allocator stays online, with thr_agreement as the fallback; claim 2 is not yet shown against designs that oversample the agreement tails or use uncertainty, nor at long horizons.

## L9a 2026-10-18 void (memorisation control triggered in 3 of 4 cells and its remedy cannot reach H = 60; the PASS / KILL rule applied with the control waived reads PASS in 3 of 4 cells and KILL in 0 of 4)
Hypothesis. Claim 2's 12-batch edge (carry_plain beats the best fixed arm by 1.06 to 1.14 in L6a and L7b) is a finite-horizon effect: 12 batches is the whole 3,000-prompt table, and the L7b equal-label result (frozen designed rule 0.987 to 1.018 of a frozen learner rule at equal n) leaves horizon as the only route by which a one-shot calibrated rule can reach the learner. Falsifier (kills claim 2 at long horizon under stationarity): on stationary iid-with-replacement streams of 60 batches of 250, the best fixed arm (amort_strat / split / seed at n in 125, 250, 500, 1000, chosen on stream seeds 0 to 7) reaches >= 0.99 x carry_plain in at least 3 of 4 cells (2 judges x B in 62, 125). Pass: carry_plain / best fixed >= 1.03 with the CI lower bound above 1 in at least 3 of 4 cells. VOID if the regression gate fails or the memorisation control cannot be satisfied.

What was run. CPU only, no generation, no GPU memory (E04 stop rule untouched). Launched with bin/gpu_run.sh avrl/runs/L9a 'bash exp/L9a.sh' (gpu_status 15 MiB used), wall 13.5 min (23:10:43 to 23:24:16 on spruce), exit_code 4 (the summarizer's code for "control void"; all 256 job commands exited 0). Timing job first (runs/L9a_timing, one H = 60 job at 7B-AWQ B = 125): 31 s. Scripts: exp/l9a_horizon.py (copy of exp/l7b_design.py; every copied function verbatim except carry_plain gaining an optional external label list L that is None in every pre-existing mode; modes iid, iid_nr, disjoint12), exp/l9a_summarize.py (copy of the l7b summarizer's structure), exp/L9a.sh. Four 64-job stages on 2 judges (E04 feature tables of Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ, ten features, label judge_error) x B in 62, 125 x seeds 0 to 15: hz_* (iid streams, batch i = 250 draws with replacement from the 3,000-prompt table, rng default_rng(30000 + seed), policy seed = stream seed, H = 60), gate_* (disjoint12 = the L7b layout with the full L7b arm set), nr_* (the pre-registered memorisation remedy), hz120_* (the same streams to H = 120, the spec's optional extension after the timing check; a prefix of the same stream, never enters the verdict). Fixed arms (12): amort_{strat,split,seed}_{125,250,500,1000}. References: carry_plain, thr_agreement, uniform; diagnostic: freeze_plain_n. A first pass of the hz and gate stages ran before the summarizer existed and was overwritten by this run (deterministic, identical files).
