Loop checks beyond the run's own verification. (1) The independent OOF recompute (GroupKFold-style folds by pid with fresh shuffle seeds 101 to 103, same logistic) reproduces cheap_r and the PC delta to within 0.001 and the bootstrap upper ends (0.0160, 0.0141) stay under the 0.03 line. (2) Every replay ratio in the table was recomputed from the per_seed lists of the 72 sim files and matches the run's figures to the printed digits (z values differ by at most 0.2 because I used ddof=1). (3) New: greedy_online / best charged recal rule across budgets with cheap_r, 10 / 25 / 50 percent: 3B corrupted 1.024 / 1.010 / 1.013, 3B judge_wrong 1.006 / 0.988 / 0.984, 7B corrupted 1.043 / 1.011 / 1.005, 7B judge_wrong 1.017 / 1.012 / 0.956. Applied to the agreement-free data, the L1b kill reading (best charged rule within 2 percent of or ahead of greedy_online at both 25 and 50 percent on all label sets) holds on all four cheap_r label sets. The cheap_r + PCs ratios are higher (1.05 to 1.07 at 25 percent) only because a 24-feature rule fit on 100 to 400 labels overfits; the PC learner is not better in absolute terms (1,432 vs 1,415). (4) New: identical-answer duplicates cannot be the main driver of the pid-layout edge. Only 24.9 percent (3B) and 26.4 percent (7B) of corrupted rollouts share (pid, answer) with a sibling, and labels are constant within (pid, answer) (0 inconsistent of 2,416 and 2,413 multi-row groups); corrupted rollouts instead cluster by prompt (a prompt with any corrupted rollout has 2.3 to 2.5 of them on average, against 6.2 to 6.35 accepted rollouts per prompt overall), i.e. prompt-level hardness, which a free group aggregate (agreement, judge_mean) carries. This is consistent with the run's reading (iii) but was not tested directly.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: KILL. The gate passes (0 differences, positives >= 200 everywhere, judge AUCs 0.654 and 0.702 reproduce), so the run is not void. PASS fails on both rungs ((a) PC delta 0.0097 / 0.0084 against 0.05, best cell delta 0.0013 against 0.02; (b) 0 of 4 splits with ts_online >= 1.05 x greedy_online and a 2 SD gap, on either feature set). The widened KILL rule holds on both rungs: (a') PC delta under 0.02 with CI upper end 0.0149 / 0.0152 under 0.03, and all three cell deltas under 0.01; (b') pooled ts/greedy 1.013 | 0.998 (3B) and 0.996 | 0.994 (7B) all <= 1.03, greedy(cheap_r + PCs)/greedy(cheap_r) 1.012 and 0.996 <= 1.03. The literal backlog rule fires neither kill nor pass: its AUC conditions are met for kill (both PC deltas under 0.01, the 3B one by 0.0003) but its third condition (learned <= 1.05 x best label-free threshold) fails at 1.27 to 1.37, because greedy_online combines 8 features against single-feature thresholds (claim 2 material, not prompt-space structure). The widening from 0.01 to 0.02 turned out to be immaterial for the structure arm: both PC deltas are also under the literal 0.01. One honest qualification: the PC delta is real, not zero (CI excludes zero on both rungs, and net of the permutation null it is about +0.012 to +0.013), but it is 5 to 6 percent of what free agreement provides and the replay shows no usable gain from it; 3B cheap_r shows a layout edge in two splits (1.021, 1.032, individual z 2.1 and 3.2) that is absent with PCs, on 7B, at cell30, and under the 1.05 bar.

What it changes about the thesis claims. Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space) is now not supported on GSM8K in both regimes tried: group-level proxies (E04) and rollout-level proxies with every group aggregate denied (L1a, cheap AUC 0.70). The revised thesis ("the allocator wins only where the proxy lacks a confidence signal") is killed on GSM8K at that cheap AUC; the only layout that helps is the prompt itself (pid buckets, 1.09 to 1.16), which is the channel group aggregates supply for free. It is a kill for this task, this judge family and this embedding: a trained reward model that reads the reasoning (L1c), a heterogeneous task, and a better representation than 16 PCs are untested, and L1a does not reopen the E04 kill (agreement was ablated, not absent from the task). Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget) gets a non-supporting point, not a verdict: in the agreement-free regime at absolute budgets about 5x those of L1b (about 2,970 vs about 560 labels at 25 percent), a charged 8-feature recalibrated rule reaches 99 percent of greedy_online (1.010 to 1.013 at 25 and 50 percent on the corrupted label, 0.96 to 1.01 on judge_wrong) and greedy_online reaches 0.96 to 0.97 of the free-label static ceiling. The learner's clear wins are over single-feature label-free rules (1.27 to 1.37), which is a weak baseline. The earlier persistent-learner passes (L6a, L7b) are untouched, but this result sits against the direction of the revised thesis: the edge is not larger in the regime without a confidence signal. Whether that is the feature regime or the budget scale is confounded and is the first follow-up. L1c is not justified as an override request on the L1a evidence alone.

Caveats. (1) One policy model (1.5B), one task, answer-only judges that never see the reasoning; the accept rate is fixed from the overall rollout accuracy. (2) Rollouts of a prompt are dependent; the effective sample is nearer 3,000 prompts than 24,000 rows, which is why CV is grouped and the bootstrap resamples pids. (3) Budget scale differs from L1b by about 5x, so the claim-2 ratios are not on L1b's footing. (4) 16 linear PCs plus a logistic or HGB may miss structure that a text-reading scorer would find (HGB gives the same +0.012). (5) The pre-registered label-free threshold family is four single-feature rules and is not the right claim-2 baseline; the charged multi-feature rule is. (6) The pid-layout edge is not a verdict arm.

Follow-ups (ranked by expected information value; specced in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under Proposed by the loop, iteration 11; all CPU, no override).
1. L11a, budget-scale matched rollout replay (about 6 min): subsample the sim prompts so the 25 percent budget is about 560 labels as in L1b and charge n_cal in 25 to 200. If greedy_online / best charged rule returns to 1.04 or more, budget scale explains the L1a vs L1b gap and claim 2 holds at small per-step budgets even without agreement; if it stays at 1.02 or less, the edge needs group-level features and the claim-2 formulation must be narrowed. Highest value because it separates the two confounded explanations of the only claim-2 point in the agreement-free regime.
2. L11b, oracle-difficulty ceiling (about 10 min): add gold-solution step count (an oracle covariate) to cheap_r and test whether the 16 PCs can predict it. A lift of 0.03 AUC or more with PC R^2 under 0.2 means the L1a kill is a representation kill and justifies asking Rustom for the L1c override with a text-reading scorer; a lift under 0.01 closes claim 3 on GSM8K for any representation and means L1c should not be requested.
3. L11c, sibling rule versus learner on the pid layout (about 8 min): a hand-written rule that boosts unverified siblings after a verified corrupted rollout, with no learning, against ts_online(pid). If it reaches 0.97 of the learner the pid edge is a fixed rule available through group aggregates and cannot support either claim; if it stays at 0.93 or below the learner finds something a rule does not, which would be a claim-2 mechanism worth a separate test.

Critic note (L1a, adversarial recheck from the raw files; applies to both L1a entries above). Verified with independent code: the regression gate (108 policy-budget cells recompared between runs/E04 and runs/L1a/gate, 0 differences), positives (4,484 / 3,958 / 2,053 corrupted), judge AUCs 0.65400 and 0.70196, every absolute catch count in the 25 percent table, every pooled and per-split ratio at 10 / 25 / 50 percent (cell100, both feature sets, both rungs, the secondary tables, cell30 and pid), the per-split z for 3B split 2 (3.2 to 3.4), the structure table (an independent GroupKFold recompute gives cheap_r 0.7045 / 0.7087 / 0.6906, PC delta +0.0078 / +0.0074 / +0.0057 and cell100 delta +0.0007 / +0.0010 / +0.0010 for 3B q, 7B q, 7B p05, all under the 0.02 and 0.01 lines), no forbidden aggregate column in the six tables, no gold in the judge prompt (p0_judge_score.py) or in the PCs and cells (MiniLM question embeddings only), equal budgets across arms, 72 sim files with 8 finite per-seed values each, and no warning or traceback in any log. The verdict logic in l1a_summarize.py matches the pre-registered thresholds, and KILL follows from the stored numbers. Corrections and qualifications. (1) Accept rule: the backlog rule is judge_p > 0.5, and on it the 3B rung has 920 accepted rollouts and 90 corrupted positives, which fails the 200-positive gate, so the 3B rung cannot meet the gate under the literal text. The run substituted the quantile rule q after the pre-run probe showed this. The Gates paragraph gives the reason, but the substitution is not among deviations (i) to (iv) and is an exception to "all fixed before the data were seen"; it is a gate-driven deviation. Also q does not accept 64.21 percent: it accepts 15,686 of 24,000 (65.36 percent) on 3B (545 rollouts tie at the cut and 3B judge_p takes only 131 distinct values) and 15,439 (64.33 percent) on 7B. The 3B cut sits at judge_p = 0.0002, a score the judge itself rejects, so the 3B corrupted label means wrong and in the top 65 percent of judge_p, not accepted by the judge. (2) What survives: under q the KILL holds on both rungs as reported. Under the literal rule only 7B-AWQ meets the gate, and it also meets (a') and (b') (PC delta +0.0054 [-0.0028, +0.0132], cell deltas at most +0.0019, ts/greedy 1.024 with cheap_r and 1.027 with PCs, greedy(cheap_r + PCs) / greedy(cheap_r) 0.975), but its (b') margin is only 0.003 to 0.006 under the 1.03 line and ts/greedy is 1.052 and 1.043 at 10 percent budget, so the literal-rule leg is the weaker one. Read "both rungs" as both rungs under rule q, plus 7B-AWQ under the literal rule. (3) Margin precision: (b') is a point-estimate rule and it is met, but with four overlapping split seeds the 3B cheap_r ts/greedy of 1.0127 has a split SD of 0.017 and a t interval of about [0.985, 1.040], so "at most 1.03" is not established at 95 percent for that one cell (its split 2 is 1.032, z 3.2); every other rung-q cell has a t-interval upper end of at most 1.022. The PASS direction is firmly excluded (largest single split 1.032 against 1.05). (4) The pid-layout arm is not a like-for-like population: the MIN = 3 filter drops prompts with fewer than 3 accepted rollouts, removing 346 (3B q), 394 (7B q) and 377 (7B p05) rollouts (2 to 4 percent; sim base rate 0.267 against 0.283 for 3B), which the entry does not state. Reading (iii), that the pid edge is "available for free through group agreement or pass rate" in real GRPO, is an untested inference (L11c is its test) and the pid arm pays for sibling labels out of the budget; it stays a mechanism check, not a verdict arm. (5) Minor wording: the 72 replay jobs took about 225 s (247 s is the cumulative time at replay end, as the loop entry words it); "about 2,970 labels at 25 percent" is 3B split 0 (means are about 2,935 for 3B and 2,836 for 7B); greedy / best charged rule at 25 and 50 percent on the corrupted label is 1.005 to 1.013, not 1.010 to 1.013 (7B at 50 percent is 1.005); the script's PC deltas (+0.0097, +0.0084) are 0.001 to 0.002 above an independent fold assignment, within the script's own CV-seed spread (3B per-seed 0.0089 to 0.0108) and far under the 0.02 line. Corrected verdict: KILL for claim 3 on GSM8K in the agreement-ablated rollout-level regime stands under the pre-registered thresholds as implemented with accept rule q, with qualifications (1) to (4); the literal backlog rule fires neither kill nor pass (PC deltas 0.0097 and 0.0084 and all cell deltas pass its 0.01 line, but learned / best label-free threshold of 1.27 to 1.37 fails its 1.05 line); L1a does not justify asking Rustom to override the E04 stop rule for L1c; the follow-ups are unchanged.

## L11a 2026-10-19 inconclusive (claim 2 in the agreement-free rollout regime: at the L1b budget scale the ratio criterion is met on both rungs, the pre-registered pooled-SD z > 2 criterion is not, and the edge is corrupted-label only)
Hypothesis: the L1a vs L1b gap in greedy_online / best charged recalibrated fixed rule (1.010 to 1.013 at 25 and 50 percent in the agreement-free rollout regime, against 1.037 to 1.072 in group-level L1b) is explained by absolute budget scale, not by feature regime. L1a verified about 2,970 rollouts at 25 percent, so a 100 to 400 label calibration charge was cheap; L1b had about 560, where the charge is most of the budget. If scale is the driver, shrinking the L1a sim population to about 2,250 rows (B about 560 at 25 percent) lifts the ratio to 1.04 or more on both judge rungs. Falsifier: the ratio stays at or below 1.02 at 25 percent on both rungs, meaning the learner's edge over a fair multi-feature charged rule needs group-level features.

Run: CPU only through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L11a 'bash exp/L11a.sh' (GPU not used, E04 stop rule untouched), exit_code 0, 171 s wall, no traceback in any of the 208 sim logs. Data: runs/L1a/tab_{3B,7B}_q_{corrupted,judgewrong}.parquet (24,000 Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K rollouts over 3,000 prompts; accept rule q; 3B-Instruct primary judge rung, 7B-Instruct-AWQ confirmation rung). Policy: cheap_r features only (8, no group aggregate), layout cell30, K = 1, 8 policy seeds, budgets 10 / 25 / 50 percent of n_sim, R = max(budget // 8, 40), n_cal in (25, 50, 100, 200, 400) filtered by n <= budget // 2. exp/l11a_sim_alloc.py is exp/l1a_sim_alloc.py plus argv[7] (target n_sim) and argv[8] (subsample seed): whole pids of the sim half are drawn at random until the row count is closest to the target, so the fit set (n_fit 3,168 to 3,986 per split, identical across subsamples, verified) and the static / static_matched arms are unchanged. Grid: n_sim 2,250 (pre-registered verdict scale), 1,125 and 4,500 (descriptive) x 2 rungs x 2 labels x split seeds 0 to 3 x subsample seeds 0 to 3 = 192 jobs (16 clusters per cell), plus a full-scale reference (target 0, cell30, split 0 to 3, 16 jobs; an addition to the spec so the dose-response has a same-layout top point). Best charged recal rule: n_cal chosen on policy seeds 0 to 3, ratio reported on seeds 4 to 7 (L2a protocol), 64 runs per cell; hindsight-best n_cal shown as a conservative check. Files: runs/L11a/result.json, summary.md (every table), gate/gate_check.json, sim_*.json (208). Scripts: exp/l11a_sim_alloc.py, l11a_gate_check.py, l11a_summarize.py, L11a.sh.

Gates. Regression gate (target 0, split 0, cheap_r, cell100, all four L1a tables vs runs/L1a/sim_*_cheap_cell100_s0.json): 172 policy-budget cells plus n_fit, n_sim, n_buckets and hits compared, 0 differences. Extra reproduction: target 0 at cell30 split 0 matches runs/L1a/sim_*_cheap_cell30_s0.json on all four tables (39 cells each), 0 differences. Realised n_sim at target 2,250: 2,249.75 (3B) and 2,250.06 (7B-AWQ), B = 562 at 25 percent.

Verification by the loop (not from the run report): I recomputed the primary cell from the raw per_seed lists of the 32 sim_*_n2250_*.json files with separate code (selection of n_cal on seeds 0 to 3, ratio and pooled-SD z on seeds 4 to 7): 3B 1.061, z 0.49, n_cal 100, greedy 265.7 vs recal 250.4; 7B-AWQ 1.071, z 0.50, n_cal 100; ts_online and static ratios reproduce. I also read the pre-registered rule in the summarize script (docstring) and confirmed the verdict logic, the diff of the sim script against l1a_sim_alloc.py (only the subsample block, two argv reads, NCALS and four output fields), and that n_fit is constant within each split (checked on 3B corrupted).

Primary cell (corrupted label, 25 percent budget, n_sim 2,250, B = 562; 16 split x subsample clusters):

| quantity | 3B | 7B-AWQ |
|---|---|---|
| errors in sim population (corrupted / n_sim) | 637 / 2,250 | 580 / 2,250 |
| greedy_online errors caught (seeds 4-7) | 265.7 | 248.9 |
| best charged recal rule (n_cal 100, chosen on seeds 0-3) | 250.4 | 232.4 |
| **greedy_online / best charged recal** | **1.061 [1.034, 1.092]** | **1.071 [1.034, 1.112]** |
| pooled-SD gap z (the pre-registered statistic) | 0.49 | 0.50 |
| hindsight-best n_cal on the eval seeds | 1.058 (n_cal 200) | 1.071 (n_cal 100) |
| clusters with ratio > 1 (of 16) | 81% | 88% |
| per-split ratios (4 splits) | 1.085, 1.021, 1.093, 1.047 | 1.113, 1.111, 1.003, 1.066 |
| post hoc, not the rule: z with within-cluster SD / paired t over 16 clusters / paired t over 4 splits (df 3) | 0.89 / 4.08 / 3.73 | 1.07 / 3.70 / 2.93 |

Baselines at the same cell (mean errors caught, all 8 seeds, 3B | 7B-AWQ): uniform 158.8 | 144.4; best label-free threshold 195.7 | 203.4 (thr_judge_low); static_matched 242.1 | 222.4; best recal (sel) 250.8 | 232.9; greedy_online 265.6 | 247.9; ts_online 267.9 | 248.6; static with free labels (upper bound) 280.1 | 264.0; oracle 562.0 | 558.2. Ratios of greedy_online: / ts_online 0.992 | 0.997 (z -0.08, -0.02); / static (free labels) 0.948 | 0.939; / static_matched 1.097 | 1.115; / uniform 1.672 | 1.717; / best label-free threshold 1.359 (z 2.20) | 1.224 (z 1.56). Labels used: B = 562 of 2,250 rollouts.

Dose-response, corrupted label, greedy_online / best charged recal rule (selected on seeds 0-3, reported on 4-7; cluster-bootstrap 95% CI; 3B | 7B-AWQ). The full row is 4 split clusters on 12.1k rows, so its CI is wide and its n_cal pool reaches 400.

| n_sim | B at 25% | 10% budget | 25% budget | 50% budget |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1,125 | 281 | 1.098 \| 1.072 (B 112) | 1.100 [1.071, 1.127] \| 1.092 [1.070, 1.118] | 1.036 \| 1.038 (B 562) |
| 2,250 | 562 | 1.137 \| 1.185 (B 225) | 1.061 [1.034, 1.092] \| 1.071 [1.034, 1.112] | 1.023 \| 1.023 (B 1,125) |
| 4,500 | 1,125 | 1.067 \| 1.109 (B 450) | 1.024 [1.009, 1.040] \| 1.044 [1.026, 1.061] | 1.014 \| 1.015 (B 2,250) |
| full (about 12.1k) | about 3,040 | 1.004 \| 1.033 | 1.019 [0.997, 1.042] \| 0.997 [0.990, 1.003] | 1.016 \| 1.000 |

Read against B (post hoc): matched-B pairs agree on 3B and 7B at B of about 1,125 (25% of 4,500: 1.024 | 1.044; 50% of 2,250: 1.023 | 1.023) but not at B = 562 (25% of 2,250: 1.061 | 1.071; 50% of 1,125: 1.036 | 1.038), so the budget fraction matters too, by about 0.025 to 0.035 at B = 562. The curve peaks at B of about 225 to 280 (1.09 to 1.19), is 1.06 to 1.07 at B = 562 on the 25 percent budget (1.04 at 50 percent), 1.02 to 1.04 at B = 1,125 and about 1.00 to 1.03 at B of 1,200 or more (full scale). It does not show the L2b dip below 1.0 at B <= 100 (L2b: 0.94 at B = 100, about 1.0 at 125, 1.03 to 1.09 at 250, 1.07 to 1.08 at 500) because the fixed arm here is the charged recal rule and not the free agreement threshold, so the two curves are not on the same footing.

Judge_wrong label (all 24,000 rollouts, secondary): the edge does not replicate. Ratio at 25 percent, 1,125 | 2,250 | 4,500 | full: 3B 0.975 | 0.983 | 1.004 | 0.992; 7B-AWQ 1.014 | 0.953 | 0.950 | 0.967. Across all 24 judge_wrong cells it is 0.92 to 1.04; at 25 percent and 2,250 rows all 4 splits are below 1 on both rungs (per-split 3B 0.988, 0.986, 0.962, 0.995; 7B-AWQ 0.938, 0.978, 0.964, 0.931), split-level paired t -2.45 (3B) and -4.37 (7B-AWQ), df 3. A free single-feature threshold also beats greedy_online there (greedy / best label-free threshold 0.954 for 3B thr_judge_low, 0.914 for 7B-AWQ thr_boundary at 25 percent and 2,250 rows).

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: INCONCLUSIVE. The regression gate passed (a failure would have voided the run). PASS needs ratio >= 1.04 with the pooled gap above 2 SD on BOTH rungs: the ratio part holds on both rungs (1.061, 1.071) and the z part fails on both (0.49, 0.50), so PASS does not fire. KILL needs ratio <= 1.02 on both rungs: 1.061 and 1.071 are far from it, so KILL does not fire. By the spec's own rule, anything else is inconclusive and the dose-response decides what to report. Why z fails: the pooled-SD statistic divides a gap of 15.3 hits (3B) and 16.5 hits (7B-AWQ) by sqrt(sd_a^2 + sd_b^2) with run SDs of 19 to 25 hits, and that SD is dominated by spread across the 16 sub-populations, which is shared by both arms and cancels in a paired comparison. At this gap the unpaired z could only exceed 2 if the ratio were about 1.25, so the criterion could not have passed at any plausible effect size at this scale; that is a defect in how the spec measured the gap, and I am not changing the verdict because of it. The paired statistics I added after seeing z (labelled post hoc above) point the same way as the CI: the bootstrap CI excludes 1 on both rungs and the split-level paired t is 3.73 and 2.93. They are not clean evidence either: the 4 subsamples inside a split share one fit set and one sim pool, so the 16 clusters are not independent and the split-level df 3 is the honest count; the n_cal selection reuses the same populations as the evaluation (policy seeds differ), and the hindsight-best n_cal gives the same ratio, so selection is not what carries the result.

What it changes. (1) The falsifier of the scale explanation is not met: at B about 560 the ratio on the corrupted label is 1.06 to 1.07, back in L1b's 1.037 to 1.072 range, and it falls monotonically with B to about 1.0 at B of 3,000. The L1a vs L1b gap is mostly absolute budget scale on this label, not a feature-regime effect; the L1a reading (1.01) was a statement about B of 3,000, not about the agreement-free regime. (2) The edge is smaller and narrower than the thesis wording. It is 6 to 7 percent at B = 562, 2 to 4 percent at B = 1,125 and 0 to 2 percent at B of 3,000; it exists only on the corrupted label; ts_online (cell30 layout) equals greedy_online in all 24 corrupted cells (ratio 0.982 to 1.023, |z| < 0.3), so no prompt-space layout contributes; and a fixed rule with free labels (static) is at or above the learner in every corrupted cell (greedy / static 0.93 to 0.97 at 25 percent, 0.74 to 0.94 at 10 percent, 0.97 to 1.00 at 50 percent), so "any fixed rule" is false as worded. The defensible form is "a persistent learner beats the best fixed rule that must pay for its own calibration labels, by an amount that shrinks as the calibration charge becomes small relative to the budget". That reads as a label-cost accounting effect and is the mechanism L12c below tests directly. (3) Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): supported conditionally on the corrupted label, the quantity the claim names, at B of about 280 to 1,100 against the strongest charged multi-feature rule tried (CI above 1 on both rungs, the formal z criterion unmet); not supported at B of about 3,000, not supported on judge_wrong (0.92 to 1.04, mostly below 1), and not supported against any free-label rule. The Phase 1 allocator spec is unchanged (persistent learner, L5a), but the Phase 1 comparison should be reported against a charged recal rule at the same total verifier calls and against the free-label static ceiling, and the expected edge over a charged rule at 500 or more total labels is single digits of percent (about 0 to 2 percent at 3,000). (4) Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): still not supported. ts_online / greedy_online is 0.982 to 1.023 in every corrupted cell at cell30 (and greedy_online beats ts_online by 5 to 14 percent at 50 percent budget on judge_wrong from 2,250 rows up), the pid layout was not run, and no embedding feature was used; E01, E04 and L1a stand. (5) Claim 1 is untouched.

Caveats. One population (3,000 GSM8K train prompts, one 1.5B policy, answer-only judges); all sub-populations are draws from the same sim half of that population (pairwise overlap is large: a 2,250-row draw is about 19 percent of a 12,000-row sim half, the 4 draws within a split overlap each other, and the 4 splits' sim halves overlap each other), so the dose-response is a scale test on one population, not a fresh-population test. cell30 gives 23 buckets, so the layout arm is coarse; cell100 was not run at the subsampled scales. The absolute gap at B = 562 is 15 to 17 hits out of about 250, so a modest change in the fixed arm's n_cal grid or its feature set could move the ratio by several points; the best-charged-rule family here is the n_cal logistic only. The L11a primary pass bar was set before the data and I kept it; the paired statistics were added after the data and cannot be used to promote the verdict.

Follow-ups, ranked by expected information value (full specs in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under 'Proposed by the loop', Iteration 12).
1. L12c (CPU, about 6 min): size-matched uncharged fixed arm. Fit the same logistic on n_cal random labels from the fit half with the labels not charged, and compare with the charged rule and with greedy_online across B. If greedy_online / uncharged is at or below 1.00 at every B, the learner's whole edge is calibration cost accounting and claim 2 must be restated that way. Highest value: it turns the dose-response curve into a mechanism and fixes the Phase 1 comparison.
2. L12b (CPU, about 6 min): boundary-distance feature on the judge_wrong label. The learner's score is a linear function of judge_logit, while the free thr_boundary rule (distance to the accept boundary, a non-monotone function of judge_logit) wins on judge_wrong; add |judge_logit - boundary| (given to both learned and charged arms) and see whether the judge_wrong negative is a representation limit of the linear learner or a property of the label. Medium value, outcome genuinely open.
3. L12a (CPU, about 5 min): disjoint-subsample paired re-test. Cut each split's sim half into non-overlapping 2,250-row blocks (about 5 per split) so the clusters share only the fit set, and pre-register a paired criterion (cluster-level paired t over disjoint blocks and the bootstrap CI) in place of the pooled-SD z. Lower value than 1 and 2 because the post hoc numbers predict a pass, but it repairs the one defect in this run's verdict rule. A fresh-population test of the B curve (L4c style, GPU, needs Rustom's explicit override of the E04 stop rule) is now the main untested caveat; its value rose because the effect is quantified at 6 to 7 percent on one population, but it stays blocked.

Critic note (L11a, adversarial recheck from the raw files). Verified with independent code: the primary cell from the 32 sim_*_n2250_* files (3B 1.0609 with z 0.494, 7B-AWQ 1.0711 with z 0.497; n_cal 100 selected on seeds 0 to 3; greedy 265.66 vs recal 250.41 and 248.88 vs 232.36; hindsight 1.058 (n_cal 200) and 1.071 (n_cal 100)), the per-split ratios (3B 1.085, 1.021, 1.093, 1.047; 7B-AWQ 1.113, 1.111, 1.003, 1.066), the 16-cluster paired t (4.08, 3.70), the split-level paired t (3.73, 2.93), clusters above 1 (81 and 88 percent), the judge_wrong 25 percent / 2,250 per-split ratios and t (-2.45, -4.37), the cluster bootstrap CIs (to 0.002), the dose-response and secondary tables against result.json and summary.md, the ts_online range over the 24 corrupted cells (0.982 to 1.023, max |z| 0.297), the greedy / static ranges, the judge_wrong range over 24 cells (0.920 to 1.043), the 208 sim json files (8 finite per_seed values each, means consistent, 16 distinct greedy rows per cell, n_fit constant within each split, all 23 cell30 buckets populated in every subsample), and the logs (no traceback or warning in the 208 sim logs or the 4 gate logs; the 212 case-insensitive "error" hits are the header phrase "errors caught"). The run's gate recompares to 0 differences in 156 policy-budget cells (39 per table at cell100 split 0, and 39 per table at cell30 split 0). New check: the run's gate uses target 0, which bypasses the new subsample block, whereas the backlog gate says a subsample fraction of 1.0 must reproduce runs/L1a/sim_*_cheap_cell100_s*.json; I ran exp/l11a_critic_gate.sh through bin/gpu_run.sh (runs/L11a_critic, CPU, exit_code 0, target 1,000,000 keeps every pid, 4 tables x splits 0 to 3, cell100): 688 comparisons against runs/L1a, 0 differences, so the subsample code path is transparent and the literal gate passes on all four splits. No leakage: the allocator features are judge_logit, token and answer-format features of the policy's own answer, and log_qlen; gold enters only the label and the global accept-rate scalar 0.6421, and the group aggregates in FORBIDDEN are asserted absent from the table and the feature list. Budget accounting is equal: every arm stops at int(frac x n_sim) verifications, recal_charged counts the hits of its calibration labels, greedy_online counts every verified item, and the fit-set classifier is used only by the free-label static ceiling. The verdict logic matches the pre-registered thresholds: gate passes; ratio at least 1.04 on both rungs (1.061, 1.071) but pooled-SD z 0.49 and 0.50 are not above 2, so PASS does not fire; ratio is not at most 1.02, so KILL does not fire; INCONCLUSIVE.

Corrections and qualifications (none changes the verdict). (1) "The ratio criterion is met" is a point-estimate statement. Both cluster-bootstrap lower ends (1.034) sit below the 1.04 bar, and the cluster bootstrap and the 16-cluster t treat clusters as independent although the 4 subsamples in a split share one fit set and one sim pool. At the honest unit (4 splits, df 3) the 95 percent t intervals of the ratio are about [1.008, 1.115] (3B) and [0.992, 1.155] (7B-AWQ), and the t statistic against the 1.04 bar is 1.27 and 1.30. So the 7B-AWQ ratio is not distinguishable from 1.0 at 95 percent (t 2.93 against 3.18), and neither rung is established at 1.04. The sentence in "What it changes (3)" that claim 2 is "supported conditionally ... (CI above 1 on both rungs ...)" should read "suggestive on the corrupted label at B of about 280 to 1,100 (point ratios 1.02 to 1.10), not established". A stricter reading moves further from PASS and does not approach KILL (point ratios 1.06 to 1.07 against 1.02), so INCONCLUSIVE is robust. (2) "It falls monotonically with B" contradicts the entry's own table: the 3B 10 percent series is 1.098 (B 112), 1.137 (225), 1.067 (450), 1.004 (1,218), and the ratio is not a function of B alone (B = 562 gives 1.061 at 25 percent of 2,250 and 1.036 at 50 percent of 1,125). Correct to "falls with B above about B = 250 and depends on the budget fraction as well". (3) "It does not show the L2b dip below 1.0 at B <= 100" is untestable here: the smallest corrupted-label B is 112 (1.098 | 1.072), against L2b's 0.98 to 1.02 at B = 125 with a different fixed arm. (4) "static (free labels) is at or above the learner in every corrupted cell" has one tie, 3B full scale at 50 percent (greedy / static 1.001, 2,398.2 vs 2,396.0). (5) "172 policy-budget cells plus n_fit, n_sim, n_buckets and hits" is a labelling slip: 172 is the total number of comparisons (156 policy-budget cells, 39 per table, plus 16 header fields); gate_check.json has the same label. (6) The "sim half" is a three-quarter split (fit = a quarter of the cell groups); the corrupted sim populations are 11.5k to 12.8k rows per split at cell30, and the quoted overlap percentages are unaffected. (7) Two departures from the backlog text are not listed as deviations: whole pids are drawn at random from the whole sim set rather than "within sim cells" (all 23 buckets stay populated, so I expect no effect), and the backlog's bootstrap over subsample seeds became a bootstrap over the 16 split x subsample clusters. (8) The 3B corrupted label uses accept rule q, whose 3B cut is judge_p = 0.0002 (L1a critic note, item 1), so it means wrong and in the top 65 percent of judge_p, not accepted by the judge; under the literal judge_p > 0.5 rule the 3B rung has 90 positives and fails the 200-positive gate, so the 3B evidence here is conditional on rule q. (9) n_cal = 400, the top of the grid, is the selected value in 3B and 7B-AWQ at 4,500 rows and 50 percent and in 7B-AWQ full scale at 25 and 50 percent, so the best charged rule there may be understated and the ratio slightly overstated; the 2,250 verdict cell is interior (selected 100, hindsight 200 or 100).

Corrected verdict: INCONCLUSIVE stands. Gate passes (also at fraction 1.0 on all four splits); ratio at least 1.04 on both rungs only as point estimates (1.061, 1.071) with pooled-SD z 0.49 and 0.50, so PASS does not fire; KILL does not fire. Claim 2 status: suggestive but not established on the corrupted label at B of about 280 to 1,100, about 0 to 2 percent at B of 3,000, absent on judge_wrong. The follow-up ranking is unchanged; L12a (disjoint blocks with a paired criterion) matters more than the entry credits, because the honest unit here is 4 splits.

## L12c 2026-10-19 inconclusive
Hypothesis: the persistent learner's 6 to 7 percent edge over the best charged recalibrated rule (L11a, corrupted label, B = 562) is pure calibration-cost accounting. The charged rule spends n_cal of its B labels on random picks (about 28 percent hit rate) instead of top-ranked picks, so the same class-balanced logistic fitted on n_cal labels that are NOT charged (drawn from the fit half) should be at or above greedy_online. Falsifier (a genuine learning component): greedy_online / best uncharged size-matched rule stays at 1.03 or more with the bootstrap CI lower end above 1 on both rungs at 25 percent budget and n_sim 2,250. The experiment attacks the 'beats any fixed rule at equal budget' wording of claim 2, not just its size.

What was run: CPU only through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L12c 'bash exp/L12c.sh' (GPU not used, E04 stop rule untouched), exit_code 0, 80 s grid wall (first and largest job 14 s), 104 sim jobs. Data: the L1a tables (24,000 Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K rollouts over 3,000 prompts); rungs 3B-Instruct (primary judge) and 7B-Instruct-AWQ (confirmation); corrupted label only (accept rule q); cheap_r features (8), layout cell30, K = 1, 8 policy seeds, budgets 10 / 25 / 50 percent. Grid: n_sim 1,125 / 2,250 / 4,500 x split 0 to 3 x subsample 0 to 3 (16 clusters per cell) plus full scale (target 0, split 0 to 3, 4 clusters). New arms recal_free_<n>, n in 25, 50, 100, 200, 400: n rows drawn at random from the fit half (rng seeded [policy seed, 12]), same StandardScaler + balanced LogisticRegression as recal_charged, rank the whole sim population, spend all B on the top-ranked picks, no labels charged and none counted as hits; a one-class draw falls back to a random order. Best free and best charged n are chosen on policy seeds 0 to 3 and reported on seeds 4 to 7 (64 runs per arm per cell). Files: runs/L12c/result.json, summary.md, gate/gate_check.json, gate/gate_check_post.json, sim_*.json (104). Scripts: exp/l12c_sim_alloc.py, L12c.sh, l12c_gate_check.py, l12c_summarize.py.

Verification by the loop (not from the run report). (1) I recomputed the primary cell from the raw per_seed lists of the 32 sim_*_n2250_* files with separate code: greedy / best free 0.9655 (3B, n 400) and 0.9763 (7B-AWQ, n 400), greedy / best charged 1.0609 and 1.0711 (n 100), percentile CI [0.951, 0.979] and [0.958, 0.994], split-level paired t (df 3) -5.23 (p 0.0136) and -4.66 (p 0.0186). All match result.json. (2) Gate recomputed independently: all 104 sim files against the same-named runs/L11a files, 4,360 policy-budget cells of the pre-existing arms plus n_sim, n_fit, n_fit_pos and hits, 0 per-seed differences. (3) Diff of l12c_sim_alloc.py against l11a_sim_alloc.py: only the recal_free branch, a NOFREE flag (argv[9]), the free_ns output field and the extended policy loop; no existing arm touched. (4) l12c_summarize.py (mtime 00:39:41) was written before the first job output (00:40:23), so the headline share definition below was fixed before the data. (5) The sanity check recal_free_400 <= static + 2 SE holds in all 24 cells it covers (free_400 minus static ranges from -46.8 to -1.4 hits, always below the static ceiling). (6) greedy_online uses no fit-half labels: it refits the same logistic on its own picks every R = max(B / 8, 40) labels and every pick counts as a hit, so it is a cold-start learner; the free arms are the only ones given outside labels.

Primary cell (corrupted label, 25 percent budget, n_sim 2,250, B = 562, 16 clusters; ratios and the selected-rule rows on eval seeds 4 to 7, absolute baseline rows on all 8 seeds):

The pre-run prediction held: free_100 lies between charged_100 and the static ceiling (3B 250.8 < 254.0 < 280.1; 7B 232.9 < 234.7 < 264.0), with greedy / free_100 = 1.046 and 1.056. The best free n is 400, not 100.

Dose-response of greedy_online / best free recal across B (selected rule, eval seeds; split-level df 3 so p values are indicative; full-scale rows have 4 clusters). B in parentheses:

The ratio is below 1.00 at every 10 and 25 percent cell, reaches parity (0.98 to 1.02) at B of about 1,100 or more, and exceeds 1.00 only at the large-B 50 percent cells (1.006 to 1.019), where the free rule's 400 labels are a small share of B and its selected n sits at the grid edge. Descriptive crossing to 1.0 near B of 1,130 on both rungs. greedy_online / best charged ranges from 0.997 to 1.185 over the 24 cells and is below 1.01 only in three full-scale cells (7B-AWQ 25 and 50 percent, 3B 10 percent).

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: INCONCLUSIVE. The regression gate passed with 0 differences (also in my independent recomputation), so the run is valid. PASS needs both clauses on both rungs. Clause 1 (greedy / best free <= 1.00 with CI upper <= 1.02) holds: 0.965 [0.951, 0.979] and 0.976 [0.958, 0.994]. Clause 2 (the charge cost explains at least 80 percent of the edge over the charged rule), at the headline definition fixed before the run (equal n at the charged rule's selected n = 100), fails on both rungs: -0.05 and 0.11 on eval seeds, 0.22 and 0.12 on all 8 seeds. KILL needs greedy / best free >= 1.03 with CI lower end above 1: the ratio is below 1, so KILL does not fire. By the spec's rule anything else is inconclusive.

How fragile clause 2 is (post hoc, for the reader; I am not promoting the verdict). The charged curve is flat between n = 100 and n = 200 (250.8 vs 250.1 on 3B, 232.9 vs 232.4 on 7B-AWQ; L11a's hindsight-best n for 3B was 200), so which n counts as 'the charged rule' is not resolved by the data, and the share is a steep function of that choice: n = 100 gives 0.0 to 0.2, n = 200 gives 0.80 to 1.25, best-vs-best gives 1.4 to 1.6 (all would clear 0.80 except the headline). Read honestly: the per-label charge at a fixed n is small where the charged rule sits (3 and 2 hits of a 15 to 16 hit edge), but the charge is what stops the fixed rule from using the 200 to 400 labels it would need to match the learner. The correct summary is that the edge is a label-supply effect, not a per-label tax: with 200 free labels a fixed rule ties the learner (3B 265.1 vs 265.6, 7B 251.9 vs 247.9), with 400 it beats it by 2.4 to 3.6 percent, and the learner is worth about 170 to 210 free labels at B = 562 (30 to 37 percent of B; 32 to 44 percent of B at the 10 percent budgets). The two readings of the share clause disagree, so the formal verdict stays inconclusive; the ratio result is clean and does not depend on the definition.

What it changes about the thesis claims. (1) Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget) is not supported as worded: a size-matched fixed rule given 400 free labels from other prompts catches 2.4 to 3.6 percent more than the learner at B = 562 on both rungs (split-level p 0.019 and 0.014, df 3), 4 to 32 percent more at the 10 percent budgets, and the static rule with about 4,000 labels sits 5 to 7 percent above it. The wording 'any fixed rule' must be dropped. What survives is narrower: against fixed rules that must pay for their own calibration labels the learner is ahead by 6 to 7 percent at B = 562 (suggestive, not established: split t 3.73 and 2.93 at df 3, 7B-AWQ p 0.061), with the edge shrinking to parity as B or the free-label supply grows. (2) Mechanism: the cost-accounting hypothesis in its per-label form is mostly refuted (charge cost at the charged rule's own n is 3 and 2 hits of 15 to 16), but the label-supply form is supported: the learner generates its own calibration set as a by-product of picking, at no waste, and that is worth about 200 labels at B = 562. This agrees with L6a and L7b that the learner's own stream is the cheap source of calibration labels, but it sits in tension with L6a's reading that which labels are in hand matters more than how many: here 200 random free labels are enough to tie the learner. The regimes differ (L6a: E04 group-level features with agreement, 12-batch horizon, calibration charged inside the horizon; L12c: agreement-free rollout regime, one horizon, free labels), so this is a difference to resolve, not a contradiction; L13b measures it directly. (3) Phase 1: report the comparison at equal total verifier calls including calibration against the charged recal rule, and also report free-label arms (free_200, free_400, static) as references, because in a GRPO loop earlier verified labels on other prompts do exist and a fixed or warm-started rule can use them; the case for a persistent learner in Phase 1 is now a label-scarcity case. The cold-start learner is no longer the obvious Phase 1 default if labeled data from earlier steps are reusable; whether online learning adds anything once labels exist is the open question (L13a). (4) Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): not tested by L12c, and still not supported. The learner here is the layout-free greedy_online, and ts_online (cell30 layout) is within 0 to 1 percent of it (267.9 vs 265.6 on 3B, 248.6 vs 247.9 on 7B-AWQ); E01, E04, L1a and L11a stand. (5) Claim 1 untouched.

Caveats. (1) recal_free draws individual rows from the fit half while recal_charged draws rows from the sim population, so free minus charged at equal n mixes the charge with any population shift (spec-mandated; the fit and sim halves are random splits of the same prompts and base rates look alike, 28.1 percent vs 28.3 percent positives in 3B split 0, but the shift is not isolated; L13b adds a sim-population uncharged control). (2) The selected free n is the top of the grid, so the free rule's ceiling is higher (static 280.1 and 264.0) and the true ratio is probably lower than 0.965 and 0.976; the grid edge does not change the direction. (3) The equal-n share is noisy (CIs from -0.95 to 0.39 at n = 100; cost on the eval seeds alone was -0.8 hits for 3B against +3.2 on all 8 seeds) and n-sensitive as above. (4) The 16 clusters share fit sets within a split, so the honest unit is 4 splits (df 3); the full-scale rows have 4 clusters. (5) One population (3,000 GSM8K train prompts), one 1.5B policy, answer-only judges, corrupted label only (judge_wrong was not run; L11a found no edge there), 3B depends on accept rule q (L11a critic note 8). (6) The free rule is a decomposition arm, not a deployable competitor when no labeled data exist; its relevance depends on whether earlier verified labels are reusable. (7) greedy_online's refit period R = max(B / 8, 40) was never tuned in this regime, so the learner may be understated (L13c). (8) The free arms use the fit-half labels of the same prompts' sibling distribution, not a fresh-population draw; the fresh-population test (L5c style) stays blocked on the E04 override.

Follow-ups, ranked by expected information value (full specs in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under 'Proposed by the loop', Iteration 13).
1. L13a (CPU, about 6 min): warm-started persistent learner against the free fixed rule at the same free labels. Initialise greedy_online with the recal_free_n logistic (same draw, n = 200 primary) and refit on free plus seen labels. This is the clean test of whether online learning adds anything once labels exist, which L12c could not answer because its free rule never learned. Highest value: pass (warm / free_200 >= 1.03, CI lower end above 1, both rungs) restores a learning component and says Phase 1 should warm-start; kill (<= 1.01) says the learner's edge is label supply only.
2. L13b (CPU, about 5 min): per-label value of the learner's own stream. Freeze greedy_online after n labels and compare precision at k on the unseen sim rows with a rule fitted on n random free fit-half labels and on n random sim labels. Converts the 'worth about 200 free labels' figure into a per-label informativeness and isolates the population-shift caveat. Medium-high value; avoids the pick-hit accounting asymmetry by scoring ranking quality, not hits.
3. L13c (CPU, about 5 min): refit-period and regularisation headroom for greedy_online (R in B/32, B/16, B/8, B/4; C in 3 values). If a tuned cold-start learner reaches the free_400 rule, the free-label comparison flips at B = 562 and claim 2 can keep a restricted 'any fixed rule' form at that scale; if not, the learner is capped near free_200 to free_300. Medium value, cheap, uses the L12c arms unchanged. L12a (disjoint-block paired re-test of the charged ratio) drops in value because the charged-rule edge is no longer the headline; L12b (boundary feature on judge_wrong) is unaffected.

Critic note (L12c, adversarial recheck from the raw files). Verified with independent code (separate from l12c_summarize.py): the primary cell from the 32 sim_*_n2250_* files (greedy / best free 0.9655 [0.951, 0.979] on 3B and 0.9763 [0.958, 0.994] on 7B-AWQ, n 400 selected on seeds 0 to 3; greedy / best charged 1.0609 and 1.0711, n 100; split-level t -5.23, -4.66, 3.73, 2.93; headline equal-n share -0.051 [-0.945, 0.389] and 0.114 [-0.475, 0.383]; best-vs-best 1.623 and 1.366; all-8-seed shares 0.22 and 0.12 at n 100, 0.97 and 1.25 at n 200; worth 207 and 170 free labels), the whole charge-cost-by-n table, all 24 dose-response ratios and CIs (max abs difference from result.json 0.0), and the regression gate (all 104 sim files against the same-named runs/L11a files: 4,360 policy-budget cells of pre-existing arms plus 11 header fields each, 0 per-seed differences; the 4 gate-mode files against L11a: 174 cells, 0 differences; the 4,776 in gate_check_post.json is 4,360 plus 4 header fields x 104). The verdict logic in l12c_summarize.py matches the pre-registered thresholds as stated (clause 1 true on both rungs, clause 2 false at the equal-n headline, KILL false), and the summarize script (00:39:41) predates every job output (first at 00:39:55), so the headline definition was fixed before the data. No leakage: the 8 cheap_r features are judge_logit, token, answer-format and question-length columns with no gold-derived field; fit and sim halves are disjoint by cell30 and cell30 is constant within every pid (max 1 cell per pid in both tables); greedy_online and recal_charged read labels only of rows they picked or charged; recal_free reads only fit-half labels. Budget B = int(frac x n_sim) is identical for every arm; the only asymmetry is the intended one (free arms get n extra labels outside B, so they use B + n total verifier calls). All 104 logs and the run log are clean (no traceback, warning or NaN), every arm has 8 per-seed values, exit_code 0, free_ns = 25..400 in every file. Clause 1 is robust at the honest unit: all 4 splits have greedy below free_400 on both rungs (per-split ratios 3B 0.963, 0.958, 0.957, 0.985; 7B-AWQ 0.972, 0.988, 0.966, 0.981) and the t95 interval over the 4 split ratios is [0.945, 0.986] and [0.961, 0.992], inside the <= 1.00 and <= 1.02 bars.

Corrections and qualifications (none changes the verdict). (1) The sentence after the dose-response table is wrong in two places. "The ratio is below 1.00 at every 10 and 25 percent cell" and "exceeds 1.00 only at the large-B 50 percent cells (1.006 to 1.019)" are contradicted by the table's own 7B-AWQ full-scale 25 percent cell (1.003 [0.990, 1.019]). Correct to: below 1.00 at every 10 percent cell and at every 25 percent cell except 7B-AWQ full scale (1.003, CI includes 1); above 1.00 at the 50 percent cells from 2,250 rows up, with the CI excluding 1 only for 7B-AWQ at 4,500 rows (1.013 [1.008, 1.019]) and full scale (1.019 [1.015, 1.023]). (2) "Reaches parity (0.98 to 1.02) at B of about 1,100 or more" and "Descriptive crossing to 1.0 near B of 1,130 on both rungs" are not supported. The two full-scale 10 percent cells sit at B 1,218 and 1,211 with ratios 0.933 and 0.963, and the 1,132 / 1,139 crossing printed by l12c_summarize.py is an artifact: its loop looks for the ratio FALLING through 1.0 in B-sorted data and finds it between the 50 percent / 1,125 cell (1.006) and the 10 percent / 1,218 cell (0.933), which is a change of budget fraction, not of B. The ratio depends on the budget fraction as well as B (L11a critic item 2 made the same point for the charged ratio): at 10 percent it is at most 0.963 at every scale including B of about 1,200; at 25 percent it is 0.953 / 0.966 at B 281, 0.965 / 0.976 at 562, 0.979 / 0.989 at 1,125 and 0.990 / 1.003 at about 3,040; at 50 percent it passes 1.0 between B 562 (0.994 / 0.998) and 1,125 (1.006 / 1.008). The same parity phrase appears in the lit/avrl backlog Iteration 13 header and should be read with this correction (I did not edit the backlog). (3) "A fixed rule given 200 free labels ties the learner" and "200 random free labels are enough to tie the learner" hold on 3B only (greedy / free_200 1.002, split t 0.14). On 7B-AWQ greedy / free_200 is 0.984 with all 4 splits negative (-4.2, -2.0, -6.6, -3.1 hits; t -4.05, p 0.027, df 3), so 200 free labels already beat the learner there, consistent with the entry's own 170-label worth figure. (4) "32 to 44 percent of B at the 10 percent budgets" holds only for the 1,125 to 4,500 row scales (31.6 to 43.8 percent: about 38 to 98 labels at B 112 to 225, 142 to 164 at B 450); at full scale 10 percent the learner is worth 177 (3B) and 184 (7B-AWQ) labels, 14.5 and 15.2 percent of B. The worth in absolute labels grows with B at 25 percent (207 / 170 at B 562, 268 / 222 at 1,125, 323 / 393 at about 3,040), so "about 200 labels" is a statement about B = 562 only. (5) The share clause is under-powered, so "fails on both rungs" should read "fails at the point estimate and is unresolved at the honest unit". The bootstrap CIs on the share treat 16 clusters as independent, but the 4 subsamples of a split share the fit half and the free-label draw. At df 3 the equal-n charge cost at n = 100 (eval seeds, hits per split) is 7.2, -22.2, 18.2, -6.3 on 3B (t95 interval [-28.6, 27.0]) and 12.3, 7.2, -8.2, -3.8 on 7B-AWQ ([-13.3, 17.0]); the 80 percent thresholds (12.2 and 13.2 hits) lie inside both. At n = 200 the intervals are [-14.2, 37.5] and [9.8, 31.9] against thresholds of 11.6 and 13.5 hits, also inside (the n = 200 eval-seed share on 3B is 0.802, on the edge). The formal rule applies to the point estimate, so INCONCLUSIVE is unchanged, but neither a pass nor a fail of clause 2 is established under any definition with 4 independent splits. (6) Table labelling in the primary-cell table: the greedy_online row (265.7, 248.9) is on eval seeds 4 to 7 while the prose and the ts_online row use all 8 seeds (greedy 265.6 and 247.9); like for like ts_online / greedy_online is 1.009 and 1.003 (all 8) or 1.013 and 0.999 (eval), so "within 0 to 1 percent" is 0 to 1.3 percent. (7) Mechanism direction: caveat 1 leaves the sign of the fit-half vs sim-population shift open. The charged rule calibrates on rows of the very cells it ranks, the free rule on rows from a disjoint quarter of the cells, so any shift can only hurt the free arm and make free minus charged an underestimate of the true charge. "Per-label form mostly refuted" should read "not detected, with a biased-low cost estimate", and the sim-population uncharged control in L13b is needed before that sentence is used. (8) "What it changes (1)" says claim 2 is not supported as worded because a rule with 400 free labels beats the learner. That holds only if the 400 fit-half labels are free: that rule uses B + 400 verifier calls (about 1.7 B at B = 562). At equal total verifier calls the operative comparison is still the charged rule (1.061 and 1.071, split-level p 0.034 and 0.061, suggestive). An INCONCLUSIVE outcome carries no pre-registered change to claim 2, so (1) and (2) of that paragraph are post hoc readings, valid for the label-reuse regime only. (9) Minor: the n = 200 all-8-seed share CIs in the "How fragile" row (0.97 [0.71, 1.28] and 1.25 [1.01, 1.75]) are not in result.json; my bootstrap with the summarize seed gives [0.71, 1.27] and [1.01, 1.72], the same to resampling noise.

Corrected verdict: INCONCLUSIVE stands. Gate passes (0 per-seed differences), clause 1 holds on both rungs (0.965 [0.951, 0.979], 0.976 [0.958, 0.994]; robust at the honest unit), KILL cannot fire (ratio below 1), and clause 2 is false at the pre-fixed equal-n headline (-0.05, 0.11) but definition-dependent (true at n = 200 and best-vs-best) and unresolved at 4 independent splits, so PASS is not established. Claim 2 status after correction: the learner is below a 400-label free-label rule at B = 562 (4 of 4 splits, both rungs) and below a 200-label free rule on 7B-AWQ, ties it on 3B, and is 6 to 7 percent above the charged rule (suggestive); the crossover to parity with the 400-label rule depends on the budget fraction (about B 3,000 at 25 percent, between 560 and 1,100 at 50 percent, not reached at 10 percent by B of 1,200), not on B alone at about 1,130.

## L11b 2026-10-19 inconclusive
Hypothesis: the L1a kill of claim 3 (16 sentence-embedding PCs add only +0.0097 on 3B and +0.0084 on 7B-AWQ OOF AUC to cheap_r on the corrupted label) may be a representation kill, not proof that GSM8K prompts carry no structure. Oracle prompt-level difficulty covariates the allocator would not have (gold-solution step count = number of <<...>> annotations in the GSM8K gold answer, count of numbers in the question, gold answer magnitude) are added to cheap_r. Claim 3 is closed on GSM8K for any representation only if they add under 0.01 AUC on both rungs; the L1a kill is a representation kill only if they add 0.03 or more (CI lower end 0.015) while the 16 PCs explain under 0.2 of n_steps variance.

What was run: CPU only through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L11b 'bash exp/L11b.sh' (GPU not used, E04 stop rule untouched), exit_code 0, launcher wall 125 s (oracle step 41 s, 48 replay jobs by 125 s). Data: the L1a tables built from 24,000 Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K rollouts over 3,000 prompts; rungs 3B-Instruct (primary judge, 15,686 rows, 2,470 pids, 4,484 positives = 28.6 percent) and 7B-Instruct-AWQ (confirmation, 15,439 rows, 2,491 pids, 3,958 positives = 25.6 percent); corrupted label only (accept rule q); cheap_r (8 features, includes log_qlen). Join of runs/p0/feats.parquet to data/gsm8k_train.parquet + gsm8k_test.parquet on question text: 3,000 of 3,000 matched, 0 duplicates, parsed gold equals feats.gold. Structure stage reuses exp/l1a_structure.py: pid-grouped 5 folds x 3 CV seeds, balanced logistic on standardized features, 200 pid-bootstrap resamples (OOF predictions held fixed), 20-permutation null at CV seed 0 (oracle vector of a pid replaced by a random donor pid), HistGradientBoosting (100 iterations, lr 0.1, no early stopping) as the nonlinear check, ridge / target-encoding / HGB R^2 at the pid level (3,000 prompts). Variants: +3 oracle (orc), +n_steps and n_numbers_q only (orc_ng, the leak-free subset, added by the run before launch), each covariate alone. Secondary replay: exp/l1a_sim_alloc.py unchanged, cell100 and cell30 layouts, K = 1, 8 policy seeds, splits 0 to 3, budgets 10 / 25 / 50 percent, feature sets cheap_r, +orc, +orc_ng (48 jobs). Files: runs/L11b/result.json, summary.md, oracle_3B_q_corrupted.json, oracle_7B_q_corrupted.json, r2.json, oracle_pid.parquet, gate/gate_check.json, sim_*.json (48), critic.log. Scripts: exp/l11b_oracle.py, L11b.sh, l11b_gate_check.py, l11b_summarize.py, l11b_critic.py.

Verification by the loop (not from the run report). (1) Gate: cheap_r OOF AUC 0.7045 and 0.7090 reproduced; no-oracle replay vs runs/L1a: 10 files, 390 policy-budget cells, 0 per-seed differences. (2) Join and covariate recomputed with separate code (critic.log): 3,000 matched, 0 duplicates in the 8,792-row GSM8K union, n_steps by counting '<<' equals the regex count, mean 3.162, range 0 to 9, 1.4 percent of prompts have 0 steps. (3) AUC delta recomputed with my own folds (different seeds, one logistic per fold, own code): cheap_r 0.7042 / 0.7087, +n_steps 0.7200 / 0.7260, delta +0.0158 / +0.0172 against the reported +0.0155 / +0.0171. (4) Leak diagnostic reproduced: log_abs_ans equals log_abs_gold in 71.5 percent (3B) and 74.4 percent (7B-AWQ) of rows, label rate 0.0007 and 0.0001 there and 1.000 elsewhere, so the table's corrupted label is, on these rows, almost exactly 'answer differs from gold' (policy wrong), not judge-specific fooling. (5) The replay ratios (2 rungs x 2 layouts x 3 budgets x 2 policies) were recomputed from the raw per_seed lists of the 48 sim files and match summary.md. (6) The pass and kill logic in l11b_summarize.py matches the spec text (pass: logistic delta >= 0.03, CI lower >= 0.015, above the permutation null max, PC R^2 < 0.2, gate passing; kill: delta < 0.01 on both rungs for the logistic and the HGB arm). The file was re-saved at 01:03:16, after the main run finished (done.flag 01:02); I could not diff the earlier version, and the thresholds in the current file are the pre-registered ones. (7) Side checks I added: a static top-k precision check, and a comparison of fixed against online arms across feature sets (both in critic.log).

The orc_ng ratio is 1.067 to 1.111 at 10 percent and 1.018 to 1.029 at 50 percent. Per-split ratios at 25 percent are 1.045 to 1.087 in all 16 split cells (splits share prompts, so this is consistency, not independent replication). Fixed rules gain as much from the covariate as the learner does (static, free labels, 3B cell30 at 25 percent: 1534 -> 1614, +5.2 percent; greedy_online 1480 -> 1576, +6.5 percent), but the greedy_online / best charged recal rule (n_cal 100, 200, 400, pooled) rises from 1.003 to 1.011 to 1.023 to 1.041 at 25 percent and from 1.001 to 1.049 to 1.020 to 1.088 at 10 percent, with no change at 50 percent (0.999 to 1.015). Fixed rules gain nothing from gold magnitude (3B cell30 25 percent, static: 1614 with or without it), while greedy_online and ts_online do (1851 and 1734 against 1576 and 1560), so the 'orc' replay inflation of 1.25 to 1.45 is the online learner exploiting a label-revealing column that a static linear fit does not; a static linear top-k check shows no such exploit (cheap_r + log_abs_gold precision at top 10 percent equals cheap_r: 0.654 and 0.591).

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: INCONCLUSIVE. The gate passed, so the run is valid. PASS fails on its first clause: the logistic oracle delta is +0.0147 and +0.0166, below 0.03, with CI lower ends 0.0089 and 0.0107, below 0.015 (the second clause, PC R^2 for n_steps 0.0458 < 0.2, holds). KILL fails: both logistic deltas are above 0.01 and their CIs and the permutation nulls (max +0.0024, +0.0014) exclude zero by a wide margin. By the spec's rule anything else is inconclusive. One interpretive fork, flagged for the reader: the spec names the HGB arm as a nonlinear check and applies it explicitly only in the kill clause, so I applied PASS to the logistic delta. If HGB deltas were eligible for PASS, the leak-free 7B-AWQ arm (+0.0343 [0.0272, 0.0412], null max 0.0058) meets the numbers while 3B (+0.0234) does not, and the leaked full-oracle HGB arm meets them on both rungs; I do not count either. The leaked arm is label leakage by construction (log_abs_gold next to the rollout's own log_abs_ans reveals answer == gold). The 7B-AWQ leak-free number sits on a weak baseline: HGB cheap_r 0.6821 is 0.027 below logistic cheap_r 0.7090, and HGB + orc_ng (0.7163) is still below logistic + orc_ng (0.7258), so the HGB delta mostly measures a poorly fitted nonlinear baseline. The run report's statement that the literal and leak-free readings agree is true only under that logistic-primary rule. L14a below settles the fork with a properly fitted nonlinear baseline.

What it changes about the thesis claims. (1) Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): not closed and not reopened. The kill clause failing means GSM8K prompts do carry a prompt-level signal beyond judge and rollout features: gold step count adds +0.0155 / +0.0171 AUC (at least 6x the permutation null max), which is 1.6x and 2.0x the 16-PC gain, and the PCs barely read it (R^2 0.046; log_qlen alone reaches 0.152 and is already in cheap_r). The PCs and the oracle are nearly additive (combined +0.0242 / +0.0249 over cheap_r, below 0.03), so the PCs carry something other than step count. The size is small: +0.015 to +0.017 AUC, +5.8 to +8.0 percent errors caught by greedy_online at 25 percent for the leak-free oracle. The pass line (0.03) was not met, so the L1a kill is not shown to be a representation kill, but the sentence 'no prompt-level structure beyond the rollout features' in the E01, E04 and L1a scopes must be softened to 'none that the 16 PCs, the cell encodings or group-level features read; an oracle step count adds about 0.015 AUC'. (2) What kind of structure: a global difficulty gradient (more solution steps, more wrong answers, corr 0.23 to 0.24 with the label, mostly policy-wrong rows), usable as a linear covariate. It is not layout structure: ts_online with the cell30 or cell100 layout stays at 0.989 to 1.011 of greedy_online even with the oracle covariates, and fixed rules gain as much as the learner. The 'edge from clusters in prompt space' form of claim 3 is closed further, the 'feature-borne difficulty signal' form is small and unreached. (3) The E04 override request for L1c is not justified by this evidence: the spec's own reading for an inconclusive outcome is that the result does not by itself justify it, and the readable part of the ceiling is unmeasured until L14b. (4) Claim 2: not tested. Descriptive only: richer covariates lift the learner's ratio over the best charged recal rule at 10 and 25 percent (1.003 to 1.011 -> 1.023 to 1.041 at 25 percent), consistent with L11a's reading that the learner's edge over charged rules grows with feature informativeness, but the oracle is not deployable and the splits share prompts; L12c's status of claim 2 stands. (5) Phase 1 hazard: an online learner trained on its own selected picks exploited a label-revealing feature (greedy_online +25 to +45 percent over cheap_r at 10 and 25 percent, of which 6 to 11 points are the legitimate step-count gain) that a static fit on thousands of labels did not; any feature in the Phase 1 allocator must be audited for gold-derived information, because the learned allocator will find leaks fixed rules cannot.

Caveats. (1) Step count is one oracle covariate; operation types, solution length in tokens, and per-prompt error propensity from siblings were not tested, so +0.015 is a ceiling for this covariate, not for prompt-level structure. (2) One task, one 1.5B policy, answer-only judges, corrupted label only, and the label is mostly policy-wrong, so this measures difficulty, not judge-specific fooling. (3) The bootstrap resamples pids over fixed OOF predictions, so the CIs omit model-fitting variance (L1a machinery); 20 permutation draws bound the null only roughly. (4) The pooled-SD z of the replay (+1.0 to +2.9) is the same weak criterion that left L11a inconclusive; the ratios are consistent across splits but splits share prompts. (5) The HGB arm is unregularised, untuned and starts 0.02 to 0.03 AUC below the logistic baseline. (6) The summarizer was re-saved after the run (see verification item 6); the pre-registered thresholds were checked by reading, not by diff.

Follow-ups, ranked by expected information value (full specs in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under 'Proposed by the loop', Iteration 14). L13a stays the highest-value claim-2 item (L13a to L13c unchanged by this result).
1. L14a (CPU, about 6 min): fitted nonlinear baseline. Re-run the nonlinear check with a regularised, depth-limited HGB and a logistic with pairwise interactions, both required to match logistic cheap_r within 0.005 AUC before the oracle delta counts. This settles the one place the verdict depends on interpretation and could flip it: pass (delta >= 0.03, CI lower >= 0.015 on a rung with a well-fitted baseline) reopens claim 3 as an interaction structure and makes the override question live; kill (<= 0.02 on both rungs) turns L11b into a clean 'small, linear, 0.015 ceiling' result.
2. L14b (CPU, about 10 min): how much of the +0.0155 / +0.0171 step-count ceiling a text-only reader recovers (full 384-d MiniLM embedding, TF-IDF, cue words, nested OOF predictor of n_steps, added as one column). This converts the oracle ceiling into an attainable number and decides whether a text-reading scorer (L1c) is worth an override request at all. Pass: recovers >= 0.012 and beats the 16-PC gain by 0.002 on both rungs. Kill: <= 0.005.
3. L14c (CPU, about 10 min): feature-informativeness dose-response of the learner's edge over charged rules at the L11a budget scale (n_sim 2,250, B about 562): cheap_r, +16 PCs, +orc_ng, +both. Tests the descriptive finding above (edge over the best charged rule grows from 1.00 to 1.04 with covariate information) at the scale where L11a found 1.061 and 1.071, and says whether Phase 1 should spend effort on features before allocator tuning. An oracle-layout replay (step-count buckets for ts_online) is not proposed: ts_online / greedy_online is already 0.989 to 1.011 with the covariates.

Critic note (L11b, adversarial recheck from the raw files). Recomputed independently of the run's code (exp/l11b_critic.py, runs/L11b/critic.log): the join and step count, the n_steps delta (+0.0158 and +0.0172 on my own folds against +0.0155 and +0.0171), the leak diagnostic, the replay ratios from raw per_seed lists, and the fixed-versus-online comparison. No arithmetic error found in result.json or summary.md. Qualifications: (a) 'literal and leak-free readings agree' in the run report holds only because PASS was applied to the logistic delta, see the fork above; (b) the spec's 'over cheap_r' contrast for the HGB arm is against HGB cheap_r, which is the weak baseline, so the HGB deltas are not comparable to the logistic ones; (c) the run report's 'no gain at 50 percent (ratios 0.96 to 1.03)' mixes the leak-inflated orc arm (0.96 to 1.00) with the leak-free orc_ng arm, whose 50 percent ratios are 1.017 to 1.030 on all eight rung x layout x policy rows, small but positive; (d) the orc replay is void as a difficulty reading and should not be cited.

Critic note 2 (L11b, second adversarial recheck from the raw files). Independent code: exp/l11b_critic2.py and exp/l11b_critic2_replay.py, outputs runs/L11b/critic2.log and critic2_replay.log (own join with a '<<' count, own shuffled-pid GroupKFold with fold seeds 201 to 203, sklearn roc_auc_score). Verified, no error found. Join 3,000 of 3,000, 0 duplicate questions in the 8,792-row union, parsed gold equals feats.gold, n_steps mean 3.162, range 0 to 9, zero-step share 0.0137, and my n_steps and n_numbers_q equal the run's table columns. My logistic AUCs: cheap_r 0.7047 / 0.7093, n_steps delta +0.0157 / +0.0172, orc_ng delta +0.0155 / +0.0169 (run +0.0150 / +0.0167; per-seed spread 0.0153 to 0.0156 and 0.0168 to 0.0170), 16 PCs +0.0091 / +0.0082 (run +0.0097 / +0.0084); the unregularised HGB reproduces (cheap_r 0.6893 / 0.6805, orc_ng delta +0.0229 / +0.0338 against +0.0234 / +0.0343). Every cell of the Numbers table matches result.json and oracle_*.json; the secondary replay table (uniform, cheap_r, orc_ng ratio and z, orc ratio, ts_online / greedy_online) reproduces from the 48 raw sim files to the printed digits. Budget accounting is equal: n_fit, n_fit_pos, n_sim, n_buckets, hits, split_seed, label, group_col and the feature-free arms (uniform, oracle, the four thresholds, all per_seed lists) are identical across cheap, orc and orc_ng in 32 file pairs, 0 mismatches; the feature lists are as stated (orc_ng drops only log_abs_gold) and the judge proxy is answer-only, so no gold reaches the proxy; the gold-derived columns are the intended oracle covariates, and the only label-revealing one (log_abs_gold) is excluded from the headline. 1,872 policy x budget cells have 8 finite per_seed values; no traceback, warning or NaN in the 48 sim logs, oracle.log or run.log; exit_code 0. Verification item 6 and caveat 6 are resolved: the launcher's own summary.log (01:02:25, before the 01:03:16 re-save of l11b_summarize.py) already printed headline INCONCLUSIVE, literal INCONCLUSIVE, leak-free INCONCLUSIVE with PC R^2 0.0458, so the post-run edit did not change the verdict. The verdict follows from the pre-registered thresholds: the logistic delta (+0.0147 / +0.0166) is below 0.03 with CI lower ends (0.0089 / 0.0107) below 0.015, so no PASS; it is above 0.01 on both rungs (the 7B CI lower end 0.0107 is itself above 0.01), so no KILL. The sample margins are adequate for this verdict (CI upper ends 0.0202 / 0.0231 sit below the 0.03 line, the permutation nulls are at most +0.0024), and per split the leak-free greedy_online gain at 25 percent is above 1.045 in all 16 split cells, with paired t over the 4 splits of 8.0 to 29.8 at df 3 in each rung x layout cell. Corrections and qualifications (none changes the verdict). (1) The sentence 'so the HGB delta mostly measures a poorly fitted nonlinear baseline' is not supported by a fitted-baseline spot check (single untuned configurations, 3 fold seeds, no bootstrap or null, so not an L14a substitute). Baselines within 0.005 of logistic cheap_r: 3B depth-3 HGB with 400 iterations (baseline +0.0043) gives orc_ng delta +0.0225; 3B pairwise-interaction logistic with C 0.1 and 0.01 (baseline +0.0041, +0.0050) +0.0202, +0.0201; 7B depth-3 HGB with 200 iterations (baseline +0.0002) +0.0269; 7B pairwise logistic (baseline +0.0030, +0.0032) +0.0236, +0.0237. Outside the band: 3B HGB 200 iterations (baseline +0.0093) +0.0217; 7B HGB 400 iterations (baseline -0.0076) +0.0283. So the nonlinear delta over a matched baseline is +0.020 to +0.028, which is 0.005 to 0.012 above the logistic +0.015 to +0.017, and below 0.03 in all eight configurations. About 40 to 65 percent (7B) and 60 to 90 percent (3B) of the unregularised HGB's excess over the logistic delta survives a matched baseline, so the weak-baseline reading explains part of the HGB excess, not most of it, and a modest interaction component is likely real. Consequences: the HGB-eligible reading still does not reach PASS in this check, so INCONCLUSIVE is unchanged; but L14a's kill reading ('clean small linear 0.015 ceiling', delta <= 0.02 on both rungs) is borderline on 3B (0.0201, 0.0202) and not met on 7B here (0.0236 to 0.0283), so the likelier L14a outcome is inconclusive between 0.02 and 0.03, and 'claim 3: feature-borne difficulty signal is small and linear' should read 'small, with a possible modest interaction component of up to about +0.01'. (2) 'ts_online with the cell30 or cell100 layout stays at 0.989 to 1.011 of greedy_online even with the oracle covariates' holds at 25 percent only. With orc_ng the ratio is 0.977 to 0.992 at 10 percent (lowest 7B cell100), 0.989 to 1.011 at 25 percent and 0.999 to 1.014 at 50 percent, so the layout is never above the layout-free learner by more than 1.4 percent and sits 1 to 2 percent below it at 10 percent; the conclusion that layout adds nothing stands. (3) 'the spec's own reading for an inconclusive outcome' misattributes: the pre-registered text ends 'Anything else is inconclusive' and gives no inconclusive reading. The override reading is attached to PASS only, so the L1c request is unjustified by default (PASS not met), not by a stated inconclusive reading. (4) 'Fixed rules gain as much from the covariate as the learner does' is loose. For orc_ng over cheap_r at 25 percent, cell30, 3B / 7B, static gains +5.2 / +4.9 percent, greedy_online +6.5 / +5.7, ts_online +6.6 / +7.6, and recal_charged_100 / 200 / 400 +4.3 / +4.2 / +4.5 percent (3B) and +1.3 / +2.8 / +3.6 percent (7B), so fixed rules gain roughly 65 to 85 percent (static) or less (charged) of the learner's gain, consistent with the entry's own greedy_online / best charged rise from 1.003 to 1.023. The Phase 1 hazard observation does hold: random-label linear rules do not use gold magnitude (orc / orc_ng 1.000 to 1.014 for static and recal_charged at 10 and 25 percent) while greedy_online and ts_online do (1.17 to 1.29 and 1.11 to 1.18, at 10 and 25 percent, cell30, both rungs); how a linear online fit exploits it is not shown. Corrected verdict: INCONCLUSIVE stands under the pre-registered thresholds (gate passes; logistic delta +0.0147 and +0.0166 fails PASS at 0.03 and KILL at 0.01; R^2 clause holds). Claim 3 stays neither closed nor reopened on GSM8K; the L1c override request is not justified; the n_steps ceiling is +0.015 to +0.017 linear and up to about +0.020 to +0.028 over a matched nonlinear baseline in an unbootstrapped spot check, still under the 0.03 line, which L14a should test properly before the 'linear ceiling' wording is used. I did not edit the backlog.

## L13a 2026-10-19 pass
Hypothesis: L12c showed that a fixed rule given 200 free fit-half labels ties the cold-start greedy_online learner and a rule given 400 free labels beats it (greedy / free_400 0.965 on 3B, 0.976 on 7B-AWQ at 25 percent budget, n_sim 2,250, B = 562, corrupted label), so the learner's edge over charged rules looked like label supply. The free rule never learns, so L12c could not say whether online refitting adds anything once labels exist. A learner warm-started with the same n free labels beats the fixed rule fitted once on them only if refitting on the learner's own stream adds something beyond the labels in hand. Falsifier of 'label supply only': warm_online_200 / recal_free_200 >= 1.03 with the bootstrap CI lower end above 1 on both rungs (headroom from L12c: the free-label static ceiling is 1.057 and 1.048 above recal_free_200, cold greedy_online 1.002 and 0.984 of it, so 1.03 means closing about 55 to 60 percent of the f200-to-static gap). Pre-registered PASS: gates pass and, at 25 percent, n_sim 2,250, ratio >= 1.03 with CI lower end > 1 on BOTH rungs and the ratio above 1.00 in all 4 splits on both rungs. KILL: ratio <= 1.01 with CI upper end <= 1.02 on both rungs. Else inconclusive; VOID if a gate fails.

What was run: CPU only through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L13a 'bash exp/L13a.sh' (GPU not used, E04 stop rule untouched, GPU shown free at launch), exit_code 0, launcher wall 89 s (both gates 16 s, first and largest job 16 s, grid done at 87 s), 104 sim jobs. Data and grid identical to L12c: L1a tables (24,000 Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K rollouts over 3,000 prompts), rungs 3B-Instruct (primary) and 7B-Instruct-AWQ (confirmation), corrupted label only, cheap_r (8 features), cell30 layout (not used by the new arms), K = 1, 8 policy seeds, budgets 10 / 25 / 50 percent, n_sim 1,125 / 2,250 / 4,500 x split 0 to 3 x subsample 0 to 3 (16 clusters) plus full scale (target 0, split 0 to 3, 4 clusters). New arms warm_online_<n>, n in 100, 200, 400: the greedy online learner (no layout) whose initial score is the recal_free_<n> logistic (same fit-half draw rng [policy seed, 12], same StandardScaler + balanced LogisticRegression, so each warm arm is paired with recal_free_n per policy seed), refit every R = max(B // 8, 40) labels (the cold learner's schedule) on the n free labels pooled with all seen sim labels, unweighted, max_iter 500; hits counted on picks only, the n free labels count nothing; a one-class free draw would fall back to the cold random score (none occurred in any file). Between refits the picks are the unseen rows in descending score order from one full-population argsort (the same call recal_free uses), which makes the no-refit identity exact even under tied scores; the critic below checked this against a literal per-pick implementation. No selection step: n = 200 is fixed in advance and all 8 policy seeds are used. A smoke test before launch caught one bug in my first draft (the per-arm result row was assigned inside the else branch, so warm arms were missing from the output); it was fixed before the launch, and the launched run is the first and only full run. Files: runs/L13a/result.json, summary.md, critic.log, gate/gate_check.json, gate/gate_check_ident.json, gate/gate_check_post.json, sim_*.json (104). Scripts: exp/l13a_sim_alloc.py, L13a.sh, l13a_gate_check.py, l13a_summarize.py, l13a_critic.py.

Gates (all pass). Gate 1 (regression): the nowarm flag reproduces runs/L12c for n2250 and full scale, split 0, both rungs, every arm, 254 policy-budget cells, 0 per-seed differences. Gate 2 (identity): with refits disabled warm_online_n equals recal_free_n per policy seed for n = 100, 200, 400 at all three budgets on both rungs, 18 cells, 0 differences, 0 one-class free draws (so the tie rule in the spec was not needed). Post gate: all pre-existing arms of all 104 grid files equal runs/L12c per seed (6,440 policy-budget cells, 0 differences) and all three warm arms are present with refits on.

Primary cell (corrupted label, 25 percent budget, n_sim 2,250, B = 562, 16 clusters x 8 policy seeds = 128 runs per arm, all 8 seeds, nothing tuned):

| quantity | 3B | 7B-AWQ |
|---|---|---|
