Secondary, descriptive. warm_online_200 / recal_free_200 is above 1 in all 24 rung x scale x budget cells (range 1.023 to 1.048), with the CI lower end above 1 in all 24 (lowest 1.004), all 4 splits above 1.00 in all 24 (lowest split ratio 1.0006), split-level p < 0.05 in 15 of 24 and < 0.10 in 20 of 24 (the full-scale cells have 4 clusters, one per split). At the 10 and 50 percent budgets at n_sim 2,250: 3B 1.028 [1.008, 1.046] and 1.038 [1.026, 1.052], 7B-AWQ 1.038 [1.023, 1.052] and 1.031 [1.022, 1.041]. n = 100 gives 1.049 to 1.124 in all 24 cells and n = 400 gives 0.991 to 1.031 (CI lower end above 1 in 20 of 24), so the benefit of warm start over the same-n fixed rule shrinks as n grows. warm_online_200 / recal_free_400 is 0.980 [0.957, 1.001] (3B) and 1.012 [0.993, 1.030] (7B-AWQ) at 10 percent, 1.004 and 1.024 at 25 percent, 1.013 [1.004, 1.023] and 1.019 [1.010, 1.030] at 50 percent. warm_online_200 / static is 0.937 to 1.013 and exceeds 1 in only 4 of 24 cells (all at 50 percent): the warm learner reaches the free-label ceiling and does not meaningfully pass it. warm_online_n is nearly flat in n at 25 percent (3B 275.3, 276.4, 280.6; 7B-AWQ 259.9, 262.2, 261.7): the stream dominates the initial n.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: PASS. Gates pass; at 25 percent, n_sim 2,250, warm_online_200 / recal_free_200 is 1.042 [1.032, 1.055] and 1.041 [1.030, 1.051], both >= 1.03 with CI lower end above 1, and the ratio is above 1.00 in all 4 splits on both rungs. KILL is not near (ratio far above 1.01). The pre-registered reading: persistent online learning adds value on top of available labels; claim 2 is restated as 'a warm-started persistent learner beats a fixed rule given the same labels', and the Phase 1 allocator should warm-start from earlier verified labels. The critic's label-count control below qualifies what the 'learning component' is.

What it changes about the thesis claims. (1) Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than a fixed rule at equal budget): a surviving form now exists and is pre-registered-supported: at equal budget and equal initial free labels the persistent learner is 4 percent ahead of the fixed rule on both rungs (CI above 1, 4 of 4 splits, 24 of 24 cells, 1.023 to 1.048). It closes about 75 and 85 percent of the gap between the 200-label fixed rule and the free-label static ceiling. The wording 'any fixed rule' stays wrong: it does not beat a fixed rule given more labels in the same pool (free_400 ties on 3B at 1.004 and is 2.4 percent behind on 7B-AWQ; static 0.987 and 0.993; a fixed rule given 200 + B free labels is level, see the critic note). (2) Mechanism: the L12c 'label supply' reading is refined, not refuted. Online refitting adds value beyond the initial label supply, but the critic's label-count control shows the size of the gain is what extra free labels would buy: warm_online_200 / (fixed rule with 200 + B = 762 free labels) is 0.993 [0.983, 1.002] on 3B and 1.011 [0.997, 1.024] on 7B-AWQ. L13a therefore shows the learner turns its picks into a calibration set at no waste, and it does not show a coverage or label-concentration benefit beyond label count. (3) Phase 1: the default allocator should be warm-started from earlier verified labels and keep refitting on its own stream; the cold-start learner is the fallback when no earlier labels exist (cold greedy_online is 1.002 and 0.984 of a 200-label fixed rule, so cold start loses its edge once 200 labels exist). Report in Phase 1: warm start, fixed rule at the same n, a fixed rule at n + B labels, static ceiling. (4) Claim 3: untouched (the new arms use no layout; ts_online is unchanged and not examined here). Claim 1 untouched.

Caveats. (1) One task (GSM8K), one 1.5B policy, answer-only judges, corrupted label only, cheap_r only. (2) The free labels come from the disjoint fit half of the same split, exchangeable with the sim half; in Phase 1 earlier-step labels come from a policy that has since changed, a shift this experiment does not model, so 1.04 is an optimistic estimate for Phase 1 and drift may shrink or reverse it. (3) The 4 subsamples of a split share a fit set and sim prompts, so the honest independent unit is the 4 splits: split-level p is 0.018 and 0.001 at df 3 at the primary cell; the 24-cell consistency is not 24 independent replications. (4) The 3B point estimate clears the 1.03 line by 0.012 and one of its splits is 1.025; the pre-registered split clause is 'above 1.00', which holds with margin. (5) The refit pools unweighted labels from the learner's own top-ranked picks, which are not random; no weighting was tried. (6) The eight-feature logistic saturates near the static ceiling, so a warm learner cannot show more than that edge here; a richer model class could change both arms. (7) n = 100 and 400 and the other budgets and scales are descriptive, not pre-registered.

Follow-ups (not run; I did not edit the backlog). 1. L15a (CPU, about 5 min): drip-fed random-label control. A rule that refits every R labels on the n free labels plus random (not picked) uncharged sim labels at the same count and then picks top-ranked rows. If it ties warm_online, picks are worth random labels one for one and the learner's gain is pure label supply; if warm_online beats it, picks are more informative than random labels (the coverage or concentration effect that D below cannot separate). 2. A drift version of L13a (CPU): free labels from the other rung's judge or a different split's policy rollouts, to bound how much of the 4 percent survives a stale label set. L13b and L13c are unchanged by this result.

Critic note (L13a, adversarial recheck from the raw files). Independent code: exp/l13a_critic.py, output runs/L13a/critic.log (own bootstrap seed 7 with 20000 resamples, scipy ttest_rel over the 4 split means, own population construction, a literal per-pick implementation of the warm learner). (A) Primary cell recomputed from the raw per_seed lists of the 32 sim_*_n2250_* files: ratio 1.0423 [1.0315, 1.0546] (3B) and 1.0408 [1.0303, 1.0508] (7B-AWQ) against result.json 1.0423 and 1.0408 (max abs diff 0.0 in the ratio, the split ratios and the split t; CI ends within 0.0003 as expected from a different bootstrap seed); split t 4.677 (p 0.0185) and 15.740 (p 0.0006); gap closed 0.7483 and 0.8508; paired cluster differences 16 of 16 and 15 of 16 positive, all 8 policy seeds positive on both rungs. (B) Invariants over all 104 files: 8 finite per_seed values per arm, 0 <= hits <= min(B, errors in sim), warm_refit true, no one-class draws, three warm arms at three budgets, and every L12c arm unchanged: 0 problems. (C) A literal per-pick reimplementation (run_online(greedy=True) structure, warm initial score, argsort over the unseen subset per pick, refit every R on n free + seen labels) reproduces the raw warm_online_200 per_seed lists exactly for 3B and 7B-AWQ at n2250 sp0 sub0 at all three budgets (6 of 6, 8 of 8 seeds each, max abs diff 0), so the block-order shortcut introduced no tie-order difference there. (D) Side check, not pre-registered: label-count control (replicates recal_free_400 per seed exactly on both rungs, so the draw is right). A fixed rule given m free labels, 16 clusters x 8 seeds, 25 percent budget: 3B free_400 275.2, free_600 277.7, free_762 (200 + B) 278.3 against warm_online_200 276.4, giving warm / free_600 0.995 [0.986, 1.004] and warm / free_762 0.993 [0.983, 1.002]; 7B-AWQ free_400 256.1, free_600 259.3, free_762 259.3 against warm_online_200 262.2, giving warm / free_600 1.011 [1.000, 1.022] and warm / free_762 1.011 [0.997, 1.024]. Qualification: the warm learner holds 200 free labels at the start and 200 + B at the end, so its 4 percent gain over the 200-label rule is matched, within the CI, by a fixed rule handed 600 to 762 free labels at once; this does not separate 'picks are as good as random labels' from 'picks are better than random labels' (L15a does), and it shows that the PASS reading 'a learning component beyond label supply' must be read as 'beyond the initial label supply', since the stream's contribution is quantitatively a label-count effect. The verdict PASS stands under the pre-registered thresholds. No arithmetic error found in result.json or summary.md.

## L13a 2026-10-19 pass
Hypothesis: L12c showed that a fixed rule given 200 free labels ties the cold-start greedy_online learner and one given 400 free labels beats it, so the learner's edge over charged rules looked like label supply. The free rule never learns, so L12c could not say whether online refitting adds anything once labels exist. If refitting on the learner's own stream adds information, a learner warm-started with the same n free labels beats the fixed rule fitted once on them; if the edge is only that picks double as labels, warm start gives nothing over the fixed rule at the same n. Pre-registered PASS: gates pass and, at 25 percent budget and n_sim 2,250, warm_online_200 / recal_free_200 >= 1.03 with the bootstrap CI lower end above 1 on BOTH rungs and the ratio above 1.00 in all 4 splits on both rungs. KILL: ratio <= 1.01 with CI upper end <= 1.02 on both rungs. Otherwise inconclusive; VOID if a gate fails.

What was run (review of the run by the loop, from the raw files, not from the run report): CPU only through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L13a 'bash exp/L13a.sh' (GPU not used, E04 stop rule untouched), exit_code 0, launcher wall 89 s, 104 sim jobs (largest 16 s). Data: the L1a tables (24,000 Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K rollouts over 3,000 prompts), rungs Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct judge (primary) and 7B-Instruct-AWQ judge (confirmation), corrupted label only (judge accepts a wrong answer), cheap_r (8 features), layout cell30, K = 1, 8 policy seeds, budgets 10 / 25 / 50 percent, n_sim 1,125 / 2,250 / 4,500 x split 0 to 3 x subsample 0 to 3 (16 clusters) plus full scale (target 0, split 0 to 3, 4 clusters). New arms warm_online_<n>, n in 100, 200, 400 (n = 200 fixed in advance, no selection step, all 8 seeds). Primary cell: 25 percent budget, n_sim 2,250, B = 562, 16 clusters x 8 seeds = 128 runs per arm. Files: runs/L13a/result.json, summary.md, critic.log, gate/*.json, sim_*.json (104); scripts exp/l13a_sim_alloc.py, L13a.sh, l13a_gate_check.py, l13a_summarize.py, l13a_critic.py.

Checks I ran myself. (1) Gates read from gate/gate_check*.json: gate 1 (nowarm vs runs/L12c, n2250 and full, split 0, both rungs) 254 policy-budget cells, 0 differences; gate 2 (refits off, warm_online_n == recal_free_n, n 100/200/400, 3 budgets, both rungs) 18 cells, 0 differences, 0 one-class draws; post gate (all 104 files, pre-existing arms vs L12c) 6,440 cells, 0 differences. (2) Primary cell recomputed from the raw per_seed lists of the 32 sim_*_n2250_* files with fresh code (own bootstrap seed 2026, 20,000 resamples, scipy ttest_rel over the 4 split means): 3B ratio 1.0423 [1.0313, 1.0546], 7B-AWQ 1.0408 [1.0303, 1.0509], per-split ratios and split t identical to result.json (4.68 and 15.74); the 10 and 50 percent cells at n_sim 2,250 and the baseline means in the table also match. 128 paired runs per rung: warm_200 > free_200 in 78 percent (3B) and 79 percent (7B-AWQ) of runs, below in 21 and 18 percent, mean paired difference +11.2 and +10.3 hits (SD 15.6 and 14.7). (3) Diff of l13a_sim_alloc.py against l12c_sim_alloc.py: only the run_warm function, the flag handling and the extended policy loop; the refit uses y only for rows already picked (y[seen]) plus the n fit-half labels, no unseen label is read, and hits count picks only. (4) Clarification of the first L13a entry's wording: the 'fit half' is a random quarter of the cell30 cells (17 to 26 percent of the rows: n_fit 2,663 to 3,986 against 11,466 to 12,776 sim rows before subsampling, over the 8 rung x split files), so the free labels come from different prompts and different layout cells than the sim population, exchangeable only in expectation. (5) The adversarial critic (exp/l13a_critic.py, runs/L13a/critic.log) reproduced the headline with independent code and reproduced the warm_online_200 per_seed lists exactly with a literal per-pick implementation (6 of 6 cells); I did not re-run its label-count control (section D), I read its output and note that it reproduces recal_free_400 per seed exactly, which validates the draw it extends.

Secondary, descriptive: warm_200 / free_200 is 1.028 / 1.038 (3B) and 1.038 / 1.031 (7B-AWQ) at 10 / 50 percent budget and n_sim 2,250, and lies in 1.023 to 1.048 across all 24 rung x scale x budget cells, with the CI lower end above 1 and all 4 splits above 1.00 in 24 of 24 (split-level p < 0.05 in 15 of 24; the 24 cells are not independent replications because splits share prompts). warm_200 / static exceeds 1 in only 4 of 24 cells, all at 50 percent: the warm learner reaches the free-label ceiling and does not meaningfully pass it. warm_online_n is nearly flat in n at 25 percent, so the stream dominates the initial labels.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: PASS. All three gates pass; at the primary cell warm_online_200 / recal_free_200 is 1.042 and 1.041, both >= 1.03, CI lower ends 1.032 and 1.030 (above 1), and the ratio is above 1.00 in 4 of 4 splits on both rungs. KILL is far away (ratio nearly 4 percentage points above the 1.01 line). The 3B point estimate clears 1.03 by 0.012 and one 3B split is 1.025, which satisfies the pre-registered clause (above 1.00) with margin but is not a comfortable margin on the 1.03 line.

What it changes about the thesis claims. Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): a pre-registered-supported surviving form now exists, 'a warm-started persistent learner beats a fixed rule given the same initial labels' (about 4 percent at B = 562, both rungs). The wording 'any fixed rule' is still false: a fixed rule handed more free labels ties (warm_200 / free_400 1.004 on 3B) or beats it (warm_200 / static 0.987 and 0.993), and the critic's label-count control shows the warm gain is what 600 to 762 free labels would buy. So the 'learning component' is best read as 'beyond the initial label supply': the learner turns its own picks into calibration labels at zero charge, and no coverage or concentration effect beyond label count is demonstrated. Together with L12c this fixes the honest claim-2 story: the learner's edge over rules that must pay for calibration (6 to 7 percent, L11a and L12c) and over a same-label fixed rule (4 percent, L13a) is a free-label-supply effect; it is not an edge over a rule with a large free label bank. Phase 1: warm-start the allocator from earlier verified labels and keep refitting; cold start is the fallback (cold greedy_online is 1.002 and 0.984 of a 200-label fixed rule). Caveats: the free labels here are same-policy and same-population, so 1.04 is optimistic for Phase 1 where earlier labels are stale (L15a tests this); one task, one 1.5B policy, answer-only judges, corrupted label only; splits share prompts so the honest unit is 4 splits. Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): untouched by L13a (the new arms use no layout); the cold ts_online / greedy_online ratio of 1.009 and 1.003 in this very cell again shows the cell30 layout adds under 1 percent, consistent with the E04 and L1a kills on GSM8K. Claim 1 untouched.

Follow-ups (written to lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md, iteration 15; ids differ from the first L13a entry's informal numbering: its 'L15a drip-fed control' is now L15b). L15a (CPU, about 6 min, highest value): stale-label warm start. Corrupt the n free labels (flip each with probability 0.25 or 0.5, or draw them from the shortest-question half of the fit rows) and compare warm_online_200 with the cold learner and with the fixed rule fitted on the same stale labels. It decides whether 'warm-start by default' survives the Phase 1 label-age problem and is the only remaining place where a learning component that is not a label-count effect (adaptation to stale labels) could show. L15b (CPU, about 5 min): drip-fed random-label control, a rule that refits on the learner's schedule using the n free labels plus the same number of uncharged random labels as the learner has picks, which separates 'a pick is worth a random label' from 'picks are better or worse' (partly answered by the critic's 762-label control, which makes this a refinement and partly supersedes the stream_frozen part of L13b). L15c (CPU, about 6 min, lowest value): disjoint-prompt block replication of the primary cell (8 blocks of 375 prompts, df 7), because the 4 splits reuse the same 3,000 prompts and the 3B margin over 1.03 is 0.012; a flip is unlikely given 24 of 24 cells above 1 but it removes the shared-prompt caveat.

Critic note 2 (L13a, second adversarial recheck from the raw files; applies to both L13a entries above, the experimenter's first and the loop's review). Independent code: exp/l13a_critic2.py, output runs/L13a/critic2.log (own bootstrap seed 99 with 20000 resamples, exact 256-resample bootstrap over the 4 splits, own population construction, a second literal per-pick implementation of the warm learner). Verified, no error in the headline: (a) every primary-cell number of the loop's table (absolute means of 11 arms, errors in sim 637 and 580, B 562, ratios 1.0423 and 1.0408 with CI ends within 0.0012 of the ledger under a different seed, per-split ratios, split t 4.677 and 15.740, warm/free_400, warm/cold, warm/static, gap closed 0.748 and 0.851, static/free_200, cold/free_200, ts/greedy, warm_100/free_100, warm_400/free_400, 16 and 15 clusters up, 78/21 and 79/18 percent of runs up/down, paired SD 15.6 and 14.7) recomputed from the 32 raw n2250 files; (b) the 24-cell claims (ratio 1.023 to 1.048, CI lower end above 1 in 24, lowest 1.004, all 4 splits above 1.00 in 24, lowest 1.0006, split p < .05 in 15 and < .10 in 20, n = 100 range 1.049 to 1.124, n = 400 range 0.991 to 1.031 with CI lower end above 1 in 20, warm/static above 1 in exactly 4 cells all at 50 percent) and all 24 rows of summary.md against the raw files (max ratio difference 0.0005); (c) no leakage: fit and sim share 0 prompts in all 8 rung x split pairs (pid overlap 0, n_fit and n_sim equal the files), the 8 allocator features are answer-derived (judge keyed by (pid, answer), log_abs_ans from the model's answer), the only gold-derived input is the single accept-rate scalar 0.6421 that fixes the population and is the same for every arm, and the refit reads labels only of rows already picked plus the n free fit-half labels; (d) budget accounting is equal: B = int(fraction x n_sim) for every arm, the oracle equals min(B, errors) in all 312 file-budget cells, free labels are uncharged for both the free and warm arms and hits count picks only; (e) a second literal per-pick reimplementation reproduces warm_online_200 per_seed exactly on 12 cells the first critic did not use (3B sp3 sub3, 3B sp2 sub1, 7B sp1 sub2, 7B sp3 sub0, three budgets each, 12 seed lists of 8 seeds, max abs diff 0); (f) the label-count control re-run with separate code reproduces the first critic's values (warm_200 / free_762 0.9931 [0.9834, 1.0019] on 3B and 1.0111 [0.9972, 1.0240] on 7B-AWQ; free_600 0.9952 and 1.0111); (g) no warning, traceback, NaN or kill in any of the 104 sim logs, the gate logs, run.log or summary.log, every sim log carries all three budget rows, exit_code 0; (h) exp/l13a_summarize.py was last saved at 01:27:46, after the launch but before the summary step ran (about 01:29:20), so the results come from the file as it stands, and its verdict rule equals the pre-registered thresholds (PASS: ratio >= 1.03, CI lower end > 1, 4 of 4 splits above 1.00, both rungs; KILL: ratio <= 1.01 and CI upper end <= 1.02, both rungs; VOID on any gate). Corrections and qualifications (none changes the verdict). (1) Gate counts are mislabeled: 'policy_budget_cells_compared' 254 is 20 metadata comparisons (n_fit, n_sim, n_buckets, hits, free_ns for 4 files) plus 234 policy-budget cells, and 6,440 is 520 metadata comparisons plus 5,920 policy-budget cells; the differences are 0 either way. (2) The data description 'L1a tables (24,000 rollouts over 3,000 prompts)' is the pre-filter pool. The fit and sim populations are the judge-accepted rollouts only: 15,686 rows over 2,470 prompts for 3B (corrupted rate 0.286) and 15,439 rows over 2,491 prompts for 7B-AWQ (0.256), so 'errors in sim population 637 / 2,250' is corrupted rewards among accepted rollouts. (3) The Hypothesis paragraph's L12c figure 'greedy / free_400 0.976 on 7B-AWQ' is the L12c headline on eval seeds 4 to 7; on all 8 seeds, the basis L13a uses everywhere, it is 0.968 (3B is 0.965 on either basis). Also a ratio of exactly 1.03 closes 53 percent (3B) and 62.5 percent (7B-AWQ) of the free_200-to-static gap, not 'about 55 to 60'. (4) Honest unit. The cluster bootstrap treats 16 subsample clusters as independent although the 4 subsamples of a split share one fit set. At the 4-split level the primary cell is 3B 1.0423 with exact split bootstrap [1.028, 1.059] and t interval [1.011, 1.075], 7B-AWQ 1.0408 with [1.038, 1.044] and [1.034, 1.047]. The lower ends are above 1 on both rungs, so the pre-registered clauses hold on either unit and the verdict stands, but on 3B the point estimate is only about 1.3 split-level standard errors above the 1.03 line (mean split ratio 1.0429, se 0.0100), the interval contains 1.03, and the leave-one-split-out pooled ratio falls to 1.034. The PASS is mechanically met and statistically modest on 3B; it is firm on 7B-AWQ. (5) The sentence 'free_400 is 2.4 percent behind on 7B-AWQ' rests on the cluster CI [1.011, 1.035]; at the split level warm_200 / free_400 on 7B-AWQ is [0.998, 1.050] (split ratios 1.002, 1.023, 1.032, 1.039), so only 'ties or is slightly behind' is supported, and warm_200 / free_762 is within noise of 1 on both rungs on either unit. (6) The ledger holds two entries titled '## L13a 2026-10-19 pass' (the experimenter's and the loop's review of the same single run, 104 files). They are one experiment, not two replications, and should be counted once. Corrected verdict: PASS stands under the pre-registered thresholds (gates pass; 3B 1.042 [1.032, 1.055] and 7B-AWQ 1.041 [1.030, 1.051], 4 of 4 splits above 1.00 on both rungs, KILL not near). The reading that may be quoted is: a warm-started persistent learner beats a fixed rule given the same initial labels by about 4 percent at B = 562, firm on 7B-AWQ and modest on 3B at the split level, and the gain is quantitatively what 600 to 762 free labels given at once would buy, so 'a learning component beyond label supply' must be quoted as 'beyond the initial label supply' only; L15c (disjoint-prompt blocks) remains the clean test of the 3B margin.

## L15a 2026-10-19 pass
Hypothesis: L13a's PASS (warm_online_200 / recal_free_200 about 1.04 at 25 percent budget) used free labels that are same-policy and same-population. In Phase 1 the earlier verified labels come from a stale policy checkpoint. A fixed rule fitted once on stale labels cannot repair itself, while the learner refits on its own stream and can. Two outcomes were possible. (a) The learner repairs staleness: warm_stale_200 / greedy_online (cold) >= 1.00 and warm_stale_200 / recal_free_200 (same stale labels) >= 1.06 on both rungs, a learning component that is not a label-count effect. (b) Stale labels poison the pooled refit: warm_stale_200 / greedy_online <= 0.97 with the CI upper end below 1 on either rung, which would make the L13a 1.04 a same-distribution artifact and default Phase 1 to cold start or label-age weighting. Pre-registered PASS: gates pass and, under flip25 at 25 percent budget, n_sim 2,250 (B = 562), on BOTH rungs warm_stale_200 / greedy_online >= 1.00 and warm_stale_200 / recal_free_200 (stale) >= 1.06 with the bootstrap CI lower end above 1. KILL: warm_stale_200 / greedy_online <= 0.97 with the CI upper end below 1 on EITHER rung. Anything else inconclusive; VOID if a gate fails. flip25 is the pre-registered primary; flip50 and lowlen are descriptive.

What was run: CPU only through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L15a 'bash exp/L15a.sh' (GPU not used, 15 MiB shown at launch, E04 stop rule untouched), exit_code 0, launcher wall 34 s (both gates 3 s, first job 2 s, grid done at 33 s), 128 sim jobs. One smoke job before launch (3B flip25 split 0 subsample 0, 8.9 s), no bug found; the launched run is the first and only full run. Data and population identical to L13a: runs/L1a tables, rungs 3B (primary) and 7B-AWQ, corrupted label, cheap_r (8 features), cell30, K = 1, 8 policy seeds, budgets 10 / 25 / 50 percent, n_sim 2,250 x split 0 to 3 x subsample 0 to 3 (16 clusters per rung and variant), STALE in {flip25, flip50, lowlen, none}. Arms: uniform, static, greedy_online (cold), recal_free_200, warm_online_200, warm_online_200_w25. STALE corrupts only the 200 free fit-half labels, through one function (free_draw) that both recal_free_200 and warm_online_200 call, so they stay paired per policy seed: none = L13a unchanged; flip25 / flip50 = each free label flipped independently with probability 0.25 / 0.5 using rng [policy seed, 15] on the labels (features untouched); lowlen = the 200 free rows drawn (same rng [policy seed, 12]) from the half of the fit rows with the lowest log_qlen, labels unchanged. w25 = warm_online_200 whose refits give the stale free labels sample_weight 0.25 (stream labels 1) through logisticregression__sample_weight; the initial pre-refit fit has no stream labels and is left unweighted, so w25 equals warm_online_200 until its first refit, and the StandardScaler stays unweighted (class_weight balanced multiplies with the sample weights). No selection step: all 8 policy seeds in every ratio, 10000-resample 16-cluster percentile bootstrap (one shared index), split-level paired t at df 3, and for the honest unit (4 splits, the 4 subsamples of a split share one fit set) a t interval on the 4 split ratios and the exact 256-resample split bootstrap. The summarizer was re-run once on the same files after a text-only edit that made the reading strings equal the spec wording; no number changed. Files: runs/L15a/result.json, summary.md, summary.log, jobs.txt, gate/gate_check.json, gate/gate_check_ident.json, gate/gate_check_post.json, sim_*.json (128). Scripts: exp/l15a_sim_alloc.py, L15a.sh, l15a_gate_check.py, l15a_summarize.py.

Gates (all pass). Gate 1 (STALE=none files vs runs/L13a n2250 split 0 sub 0, both rungs, uniform / static / greedy_online / recal_free_200 / warm_online_200 at three budgets plus n_fit, n_sim, n_buckets, hits): 38 comparisons, 0 differences. Gate 2 (norefit, all four STALE variants, both rungs): warm_online_200 == recal_free_200 per policy seed at all three budgets and w25 == warm_online_200, 54 comparisons including 6 checks that the corruption is applied (recal_free_200 differs from the clean L13a value in 3 of 3 budget cells for every flip25, flip50 and lowlen file), 0 differences, no one-class free draw anywhere. Post gate: 128 files present, 1,856 comparisons, 0 differences (the 32 none files equal L13a on all five L13a arms; uniform, static and greedy_online equal L13a in every variant, so nothing but the free-label arms moved).

Results, flip25, 25 percent, n_sim 2,250, B = 562, 16 clusters x 8 seeds = 128 runs per rung (mean errors caught).
3B: warm_stale 269.0, cold greedy 265.6, recal_free_200 (stale) 236.3, warm clean (L13a) 276.4, w25 270.1. warm_stale / cold 1.013 [1.002, 1.023] (split t interval [0.983, 1.042], exact split bootstrap [0.999, 1.030], per-split 1.011 / 0.994 / 1.038 / 1.007, 3 of 4 above 1.00, split t(df 3) 1.38 p 0.261). warm_stale / recal_free_200 (stale) 1.138 [1.113, 1.165] (split t interval [1.060, 1.221], per-split 1.088 / 1.145 / 1.120 / 1.208, split t 6.46 p 0.008). warm_stale / warm clean 0.973 [0.961, 0.984]. w25 / cold 1.017 [1.009, 1.024] (split t interval [0.997, 1.037]). recal_free_200 (stale) / cold 0.890.
7B-AWQ: warm_stale 257.1, cold 247.9, recal_free_200 (stale) 222.9, warm clean 262.2, w25 258.1. warm_stale / cold 1.037 [1.026, 1.049] (split t interval [1.014, 1.060], exact split bootstrap [1.024, 1.049], per-split 1.018 / 1.036 / 1.053 / 1.040, 4 of 4 above 1.00, split t 4.86 p 0.017). warm_stale / recal_free_200 (stale) 1.153 [1.121, 1.183] (split t interval [1.061, 1.247], per-split 1.148 / 1.073 / 1.199 / 1.195, split t 5.38 p 0.013). warm_stale / warm clean 0.980 [0.974, 0.987]. w25 / cold 1.041 [1.032, 1.052] (split t interval [1.027, 1.055]). recal_free_200 (stale) / cold 0.899.
Control (none, same cell): warm / cold 1.040 [1.031, 1.051] on 3B and 1.058 [1.045, 1.072] on 7B-AWQ, warm / free 1.042 and 1.041 (the L13a result, reproduced exactly).
Descriptive rows at 25 percent. flip50: warm_stale / cold 0.942 [0.926, 0.957] on 3B (0 of 4 splits above 1.00, split t interval [0.904, 0.980]) and 0.972 [0.962, 0.982] on 7B-AWQ (split t interval [0.941, 1.002]); warm_stale / recal_free_200 1.455 and 1.560 (the fixed rule collapses to 0.647 and 0.623 of cold); w25 / cold 0.969 [0.957, 0.979] and 0.996 [0.987, 1.005]; w25 / warm_stale 1.028 and 1.025. lowlen: warm_stale / cold 1.037 [1.028, 1.046] and 1.043 [1.028, 1.057]; warm_stale / recal_free_200 1.070 and 1.110; warm_stale / warm clean 0.997 and 0.987; w25 / cold 1.033 and 1.043. Other budgets under flip25 (warm_stale / cold): 10 percent 1.036 and 1.059, 50 percent 1.004 [0.999, 1.008] and 1.013 [1.009, 1.017]; warm_stale / recal_free_200 1.093 / 1.118 at 10 percent and 1.118 / 1.110 at 50 percent. w25 / warm_stale under flip25 is 1.004 [0.999, 1.009] on both rungs; under none it costs 0.993 [0.988, 0.999] on 3B and 0.998 [0.994, 1.001] on 7B-AWQ.

Verdict: PASS. Gates pass; under flip25 at 25 percent budget and n_sim 2,250, on BOTH rungs warm_stale_200 / greedy_online >= 1.00 (1.013 on 3B, 1.037 on 7B-AWQ) and warm_stale_200 / recal_free_200 (stale) >= 1.06 with the bootstrap CI lower end above 1 (1.138 [1.113, 1.165] and 1.153 [1.121, 1.183]); KILL not near (lowest warm_stale / cold CI upper end 1.023). Pre-registered reading: a learning component that is not a label-count effect exists (adaptation to stale labels), Phase 1 warm-starts from earlier verified labels by default and keeps refitting, and claim 2's surviving form gains a drift-robustness clause.

Qualifications (none changes the verdict, all bear on how far the reading may be quoted). (1) The two PASS clauses are not equally strong. The 1.06 clause against the fixed rule is met by a wide margin because the fixed rule is itself damaged by the stale labels (0.89 and 0.90 of cold) and cannot refit; that ratio shows repair, not a gain over having no stale labels. The discriminating clause is warm_stale / cold >= 1.00, which holds on 7B-AWQ at every level of the evidence (4 of 4 splits, split t interval [1.014, 1.060]) but on 3B is marginal: 1.013, split t interval [0.983, 1.042] contains 1.00, exact split bootstrap lower end 0.999, one split below 1.00, and at 50 percent budget the 3B ratio is 1.004 [0.999, 1.008]. (2) Stale labels at 25 percent flip cost part of the warm-start advantage: warm_stale / warm clean is 0.973 and 0.980, and the advantage over cold shrinks from 1.040 to 1.013 on 3B (about a third kept) and from 1.058 to 1.037 on 7B-AWQ (about two thirds kept). So 'warm start by default' survives as 'warm start is not worse than cold and usually a few percent better at 25 percent label noise', not as 'warm start keeps its full L13a edge'. (3) The result is noise-level dependent and the break-even was not located. Under flip50 (free labels carry no signal) the warm start is poisoned: warm_stale / cold 0.942 on 3B has the CI upper end 0.957, which would meet the KILL rule's numbers if flip50 had been the primary; it was pre-registered as descriptive, so it does not change the verdict, but it bounds the drift-robustness clause to moderate label corruption. w25 recovers to 0.969 (3B) and 0.996 (7B-AWQ) of cold at flip50, so label-age weighting at 0.25 helps (about +2.5 to +2.8 percent over unweighted warm) but does not fully repair pure noise on 3B; at flip25 and on clean labels the weight is within about 1 percent of unweighted (w25 / warm 1.004 at flip25, 0.993 and 0.998 at none), so these runs do not show a reason to prefer it when the labels are only mildly stale. (4) lowlen (covariate shift in the free rows, labels correct) is harmless: warm_stale / warm clean 0.997 and 0.987, warm / cold 1.037 and 1.043. (5) Staleness is simulated by symmetric label flips independent of the features and by a length-biased draw of fit-half rows, not by labels from a real earlier checkpoint; a real stale policy shifts the label-feature relation in a structured way, so this is a stress test of the mechanism, not a measurement of Phase 1 staleness. (6) The 16 clusters share fit sets within a split; the pre-registered clauses are reported on the bootstrap unit as specified and the split-level intervals above are the honest unit. (7) Single run, one pre-registered flip rate, 8 policy seeds, no replication on disjoint prompt blocks (L15c).

## L15a 2026-10-19 pass
Hypothesis: L13a's PASS (warm_online_200 / recal_free_200 about 1.04 at 25 percent budget) used free labels that are same-policy and same-population. In Phase 1 the earlier verified labels come from a stale checkpoint. A fixed rule fitted once on stale labels cannot repair itself, while the learner refits on its own stream and can. (a) Repair: warm_stale_200 / greedy_online (cold) >= 1.00 and warm_stale_200 / recal_free_200 (same stale labels) >= 1.06 on both rungs, which would be a learning component that is not a label-count effect. (b) Poisoning: warm_stale_200 / cold <= 0.97 with the CI upper end below 1 on either rung, which would make the L13a 1.04 a same-distribution artifact and default Phase 1 to cold start or label-age weighting. Primary: flip25 (each of the 200 free labels flipped with probability 0.25), 25 percent budget, n_sim 2,250 (B = 562); flip50 and lowlen descriptive; VOID if a gate fails.

What was run (review of the run by the loop, from the raw files, not from the run report): CPU only through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L15a 'bash exp/L15a.sh' (GPU not used, E04 stop rule untouched), exit_code 0, launcher wall 34 s, 128 sim jobs (first job 2 s). Data: L1a tables (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K rollouts over 3,000 prompts), rungs Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct judge (primary) and 7B-Instruct-AWQ judge (confirmation), corrupted label (judge accepts a wrong answer), cheap_r (8 features), cell30, K = 1, 8 policy seeds, budgets 10 / 25 / 50 percent, n_sim 2,250 x split 0 to 3 x subsample 0 to 3 (16 clusters per rung and variant), STALE in {flip25, flip50, lowlen, none}. Arms: uniform, static, greedy_online (cold), recal_free_200, warm_online_200, warm_online_200_w25. Checks I ran myself. (1) I read exp/l15a_sim_alloc.py as a diff against l13a_sim_alloc.py: the corruption enters through one function (free_draw) called by both recal_free_200 and warm_online_200, flips use rng [policy seed, 15] on the labels only, w25 passes logisticregression__sample_weight with 0.25 on the 200 free rows and 1 on stream rows, and the other arms are untouched; L15a.sh does not write done.flag or exit_code (gpu_run.sh wrote both, exit_code 0). (2) Gates read from gate/gate_check*.json: gate 1 38 cells, 0 differences; gate 2 54 cells (warm == recal_free per seed with refits off under all four STALE variants on both rungs, w25 == warm, and the corruption verified active in 3 of 3 budget cells for every flip and lowlen file), 0 differences, no one-class free draw; post gate 128 files, 1,856 cells, 0 differences. (3) Independent recomputation with new code, exp/l15a_critic.py, output runs/L15a/critic.log (own bootstrap seed 7 with 20,000 resamples, scipy ttest_rel over the 4 split means): primary-cell ratios, CIs, per-split ratios and split t reproduce result.json to the third decimal (CI ends within 0.0003); invariants over all 128 files (8 finite per_seed values per arm, 0 <= hits <= min(B, errors in sim), STALE header matches the file name, refits on, six arms at three budgets): 0 problems; the literal PASS / KILL rule applied to the raw files gives PASS. No arithmetic error found in result.json or summary.md.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: PASS. All three gates pass with 0 differences. Under flip25 at 25 percent and n_sim 2,250, on BOTH rungs warm_stale_200 / greedy_online >= 1.00 (1.013, 1.037) and warm_stale_200 / recal_free_200 (stale) >= 1.06 with the bootstrap CI lower end above 1 (1.113 and 1.121). KILL does not fire (the lowest warm_stale / cold CI upper end is 1.023, far from 0.97). The PASS is mechanical and, on the discriminating clause, thin on 3B: warm_stale / cold is 1.013, the split-level t interval [0.983, 1.042] contains 1.00, one of 4 splits is below 1.00, and at 50 percent budget the 3B ratio is 1.004.

What it changes about the thesis claims. (1) Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): the L13a surviving form (a warm-started persistent learner beats a fixed rule fitted once on the same initial labels) now has a robustness clause: at 25 percent symmetric label noise the warm start is still not worse than cold (1.013 and 1.037) and is 14 to 15 percent ahead of the fixed rule on the same stale labels. The wording 'any fixed rule' stays false: the clean-label static ceiling is 1.04 and 1.03 above warm_stale (280.1 and 264.0), and L13a's critic showed a fixed rule with 762 free labels matches the clean warm learner. (2) The L15a reading 'a learning component that is not a label-count effect' is NOT established by this run and should not be quoted. The 1.06 clause is easy because the fixed rule is damaged by the noise (0.89 and 0.90 of cold) and cannot refit, so the 1.14 to 1.15 ratio measures repair of a damaged rule, not a gain over no stale labels; no stale fixed rule with more labels was run, and symmetric flips mostly attenuate a logistic score without reordering rows (a heuristic, not tested here: flip25 leaves about (1 - 2 x 0.25)^2 = a quarter of the signal per label), so more stale labels could buy the fixed rule back what the stream buys the learner. The operational reading that does survive is that the stream's fresh labels wash out moderate staleness: the stale 200 are about 26 percent of the refit set at B = 562, w25 / warm_stale is 1.004 (weighting is neutral at flip25), and the learner keeps a third (3B) to two thirds (7B-AWQ) of the clean warm-start edge over cold. (3) Phase 1: keep 'warm-start from earlier verified labels and keep refitting' as the default for moderately stale labels and for covariate-shifted labels (lowlen, warm_stale / warm clean 0.997 and 0.987), but the default carries a noise limit: with uninformative free labels (flip50) the warm start is poisoned (0.942 and 0.972 of cold; at 25 percent budget the 3B CI upper end 0.957 would meet the KILL numbers had flip50 been the primary), and label-age weighting at 0.25 recovers only part of it (w25 / cold 0.969 and 0.996). The break-even noise level between 0.25 and 0.5 was not located, and symmetric flips are the benign kind of staleness. (4) Claim 3: untouched (no layout arm was run; cold ts_online / greedy_online stays 1.009 and 1.003 from L13a). Claim 1: untouched.

Corrections and qualifications (none changes the verdict). (a) The run entry above says the 1.06 clause is easy; I agree and add that the discriminating comparison is warm_stale / cold, which is 3 of 4 splits above 1 on 3B and 4 of 4 on 7B-AWQ. (b) Staleness is simulated by label flips and a length-biased draw, not by labels from a real earlier checkpoint. I checked whether cross-judge labels could serve as a structured stale set: the 3B and 7B-AWQ tables join on (pid, k) with 12,309 shared rollouts and the corrupted label agrees on all of them (the label is gold-based and each table holds only the rows its judge accepts), so a cross-judge label swap carries no label shift and was dropped as a follow-up. (c) The 16 clusters share fit sets within a split; the split-level intervals are the honest unit. (d) Single run, one pre-registered flip rate, 8 policy seeds, no replication on disjoint prompt blocks.

Follow-ups (written to lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md, iteration 16, ranked by expected information value). L16a (CPU, about 6 min, highest value): stale label-count control plus a noise ladder. Give the fixed rule 200, 400 and 762 (200 + B) stale flip25 labels and run warm_online_200 at flip 0.10, 0.25, 0.35, 0.40, 0.45; it decides whether 'not a label-count effect' can be said at all (pass: stale 762-label rule / warm_stale <= 0.99 with the CI upper end below 1 on both rungs; kill: >= 1.00 on either rung) and locates the break-even noise where warm_stale / cold crosses 1.00. L16b (CPU, about 8 min, second): structured staleness that changes the ranking rather than attenuating it: flip free labels with probability 0.5 only in rows with judge_logit above the fit-half median (relation reversal where the rule ranks highest), plus one-sided flips (positives dropped with probability 0.8, negatives raised with probability 0.35), with a drop-stale-after-first-refit arm; it tests whether the flip25 PASS is specific to rank-preserving noise and whether label-age weighting is needed under realistic drift. L16c (CPU, about 6 min, third): amend L15c so the disjoint-prompt block replication also carries the flip25 arms, because the 3B warm_stale / cold margin (1.013, split interval [0.983, 1.042]) is the weakest number in the PASS and df 7 on independent prompt blocks is the cheapest way to decide it. L15b is unchanged. Every GPU item remains blocked on the E04 stop rule and L15a gives no reason to request an override.

Critic note (L15a, adversarial recheck from the raw files; applies to both L15a entries above, the experimenter's and the loop's review, which are one run of 128 files and count once). Independent code: exp/l15a_critic2.py, outputs runs/L15a/critic2_A.log, critic2_B.log, critic2_C.log, critic2_D.log, critic2_E.log (own bootstrap seed 11 with 20000 resamples, scipy ttest_rel over the 4 split means, exact 256-resample split bootstrap, own population construction, own per-pick implementation). Verified, no error in the headline: (a) every primary-cell number recomputed from the 32 raw flip25 files per rung (mean errors of the seven arms, ratios, CI ends within 0.0006 of the ledger, per-split ratios, split t and p, split t intervals, exact split bootstraps, clusters and seeds up 12 / 16 and 6 / 8 on 3B, 15 / 16 and 8 / 8 on 7B-AWQ, warm_stale / static 0.960 and 0.974, free_stale / cold 0.890 and 0.899, share of the clean warm edge kept 0.315 and 0.640) and all 24 rows of the summary.md main table (5 ratios with CIs, 5 means, free_stale / cold): 0 mismatches, largest ratio or CI-end difference 0.0042 (a wide flip50 interval under a different bootstrap seed); the result.json verdict block equals mine (PASS, gate_ok true). (b) The literal rule applied to the raw files gives PASS and KILL is not met on either rung. (c) An independent per-pick reimplementation (ranking recomputed from scratch at every pick, own free_draw with the rng [seed, 12] permutation and the rng [seed, 15] flips at 0.25, own sample_weight 0.25 refit, own cold greedy) reproduces the raw per_seed lists of warm_online_200, warm_online_200_w25, recal_free_200 (stale) and greedy_online at the 25 percent budget for all 16 clusters on both rungs: 128 of 128 lists of 8 seeds identical, 0 differ, so the flips, the weights and the arms are what the entries say. (d) No leakage: fit and sim share 0 pids in all 8 rung x split pairs and n_fit and n_fit_pos equal the files; the 8 features are answer or judge derived (univariate AUC against the label 0.38 to 0.66, none near 1) and the label column is not an input; the realised flip fraction over 4 splits x 8 seeds averages 0.255 (range 0.23 to 0.285); gold-derived labels reach the allocator only through the 200 free fit-half labels and the labels of rows already picked. (e) Budget accounting is equal: every arm in a file shares B = int(0.25 x n_sim), free labels are uncharged and uncounted, hits stay within [0, min(B, errors in sim)] in all 128 files x 3 budgets x 6 arms (0 invariant problems). (f) No hidden failure: no Traceback, Warning, NaN or kill in the 128 sim logs, the 16 gate logs or run.log, every sim log carries its three budget rows, exit_code 0, gates 38 / 54 / 1,856 comparisons with 0 differences as stated. (g) The loop entry's side claims hold: the 3B and 7B-AWQ tables share 12,309 rollouts on (pid, k) with 0 label disagreements, and L13a ts_online / greedy_online at 25 percent is 1.0085 and 1.0028 (quoted as 1.009 and 1.003).

Corrections and qualifications. (1) Verdict label: PASS stands. It follows mechanically from the pre-registered thresholds (gates pass; warm_stale / cold 1.013 and 1.037 >= 1.00; warm_stale / recal_free_200 (stale) 1.138 [1.113, 1.165] and 1.153 [1.121, 1.183] >= 1.06 with the CI lower end above 1; KILL clause 1.013 and 1.037 against 0.97 with CI upper ends 1.023 and 1.049), and it also holds if the CI clause is read to cover warm_stale / cold on the pre-registered 16-cluster bootstrap (lower ends 1.002 and 1.026). It does not hold on 3B at the honest unit: exact split bootstrap lower end 0.999, split t interval lower end 0.983, split t p 0.261, so on 3B the discriminating clause is a statistical tie with cold, and only 7B-AWQ is firm (4 of 4 splits, exact split bootstrap [1.024, 1.049]). (2) The first entry's reading line ('a learning component that is not a label-count effect exists (adaptation to stale labels)') is not established and must not be quoted; I agree with the loop entry and add a quantitative check. Side check, not pre-registered (exp/l15a_critic2.py part E, runs/L15a/critic2_E.log; the m = 200 stale rule reproduces recal_free_200 exactly on both rungs; same flip25 populations, 25 percent budget, a fixed rule given m free labels from the same nested draws and flips, 16 clusters x 8 seeds). Stale labels: warm_stale / fixed rule with 400, 600 and 762 stale labels is 1.079, 1.039 and 1.020 [1.001, 1.041] on 3B and 1.091, 1.055 and 1.035 [1.012, 1.056] on 7B-AWQ, so the 1.06 clause is met only because the comparator holds 200 labels (it would fail against a 600-stale-label rule on both rungs), and a fixed rule with 762 stale labels reaches 0.993 (3B) and 1.002 (7B-AWQ) of cold. The loop entry's guess that more stale labels could buy the fixed rule back what the stream buys the learner is therefore only partly right: the learner stays 2.0 percent (3B) and 3.5 percent (7B-AWQ) ahead of the 762-stale-label rule on the cluster bootstrap (lower end 1.0006 on 3B), but the split-level t intervals [0.958, 1.084] and [0.973, 1.098] contain 1 (2 of 4 splits below 1.00 on 3B, 1 of 4 on 7B-AWQ). Clean labels: a fixed rule given 400, 600 and 762 clean labels scores 275.2, 277.7 and 278.3 (3B) and 256.1, 259.3 and 259.4 (7B-AWQ) against warm_stale 269.0 and 257.1, so warm_stale / it is 0.978 [0.964, 0.990], 0.969 and 0.967 [0.952, 0.980] on 3B (the learner is behind) and 1.004, 0.991 and 0.991 [0.974, 1.007] on 7B-AWQ (a tie). The stale-count and clean-count controls bracket the question, so 'not a label-count effect' is neither affirmed nor refuted by L15a; L16a (noise ladder plus its own pre-registered stale-label-count control) still has to be run and these numbers do not count as its run. (3) The loop entry's statement that 'the stale 200 are about 26 percent of the refit set at B = 562' is the share at the last refit only (200 / (200 + 560)). With R = 70 there are 8 refits at 70, 140, ..., 560 stream labels, with stale shares of 74, 59, 49, 42, 36, 32, 29 and 26 percent (mean 43 percent), and the first 70 picks come from the stale-only score, so the 'fresh labels wash out moderate staleness' argument is weaker than that sentence suggests. (4) Nominal sizes: n_sim 2,250 and B = 562 are the target and the rounded mean; per file n_sim ranges 2,246 to 2,253 (mean 2,249.75 on 3B, 2,250.06 on 7B-AWQ) and B at 25 percent ranges 561 to 563 (mean 562.0 and 562.1); the errors in sim figures 637 and 580 are means over 16 files. Every arm in a file shares that file's B, so nothing changes. (5) The ledger holds two entries titled '## L15a 2026-10-19 pass' for the same single run; count it once. Corrected verdict: PASS stands under the pre-registered thresholds (3B discriminating clause 1.013, a tie with cold at the honest unit; 7B-AWQ 1.037, firm). The reading that may be quoted is: at 25 percent symmetric label noise a warm start from stale labels is not worse than cold and is 14 to 15 percent ahead of a fixed rule fitted once on the same 200 stale labels, which is repair of a damaged 200-label rule by the stream's clean labels and not evidence of a learning component beyond label supply; the Phase 1 default of warm start plus refitting stands for moderate staleness with the noise limit (flip50 poisons it: 0.942 and 0.972 of cold), and the label-count question stays open until L16a.

## L16b 2026-10-19 spec amendment (recorded before any run, no verdict yet)
Experiment: L16b, the pre-registered kill test of the Phase 1 warm-start default under ranking-changing staleness (CPU replay through bin/gpu_run.sh, same machinery as L15a). Hypothesis: L15a's warm-start PASS (warm_stale_200 / cold greedy_online 1.013 on 3B, 1.037 on 7B-AWQ at flip25, 25 percent budget) used symmetric label flips, which attenuate a logistic score but mostly preserve its row ordering; real staleness changes the feature-to-error relation in the region the rule ranks highest. Falsifier of 'warm start is robust to realistic staleness': under the primary jlflip50, warm_online_200 / greedy_online <= 0.97 with the 16-cluster bootstrap CI upper end below 1 on either rung.

SPEC AMENDMENT (the backlog text, written in iteration 16, flips the rows ABOVE the fit-half median judge_logit). Checked before the run on the full tables runs/L1a/tab_{3B,7B}_q_corrupted.parquet: the AUC of judge_logit against the corrupted label is 0.4135 (3B) and 0.3796 (7B-AWQ), so corrupted rollouts concentrate at LOW judge_logit. In the four fit halves the error rate is 0.32 to 0.37 below the fit-half median versus 0.23 to 0.25 above it on 3B, and 0.31 to 0.34 versus 0.18 to 0.21 on 7B-AWQ. The region the learned rule ranks highest is therefore the BELOW-median half, and flipping above the median would damage the region the rule rarely picks from. The PRIMARY variant is therefore jlflip50 = each of the 200 free labels whose judge_logit is strictly BELOW the fit-half median judge_logit is flipped with probability 0.5; rows at or above the median keep their labels. The above-median version of the backlog text is kept only as a descriptive control (jlflip50_hi, strictly above the median flipped). Other descriptive variants: jlrev100 (every free label strictly below the median flipped, probability 1.0, a full relation reversal in the ranked region), fn80 (positives flipped to negative with probability 0.8), fp35 (negatives flipped to positive with probability 0.35), and the flip25 control (all labels, probability 0.25; must reproduce runs/L15a). The median is computed once from the whole fit half (fit['judge_logit']), not from the free draw. All flips use rng [policy seed, 15] on the labels only, never the features, inside free_draw(), so recal_free_200, warm_online_200, warm_online_200_w25 and warm_online_200_w0 stay paired per policy seed.

Implementation choices fixed now (not outcome-dependent). (1) New arm warm_online_200_w0: the stale labels carry weight 1 in the initial fit and in the picks before the first refit and weight 0 in every refit, through the same pipeline-step sample_weight path as w25 (class_weight='balanced' is still computed by sklearn over all rows of the refit including the zero-weight stale rows). Its refit guard looks at the labels that carry weight, so w0 skips a refit while the stream labels are one-class; for the warm and w25 arms the guard is unchanged. (2) Expected realised fraction of changed free labels for jlflip50 is about 0.24 (the below-median share of a fit half is 0.47 to 0.50, half of it flipped); gate 3 requires the pooled realised fraction per rung within 0.20 to 0.30 and zero changed labels on the keep side of the median. (3) Grid: corrupted label, rungs 3B and 7B-AWQ, cell30, cheap_r (8 features), K = 1, 8 policy seeds, budgets 10 / 25 / 50 percent, n_sim 2,250 x split 0..3 x subsample 0..3, 6 STALE variants x 2 rungs x 16 = 192 jobs; arms uniform, static, greedy_online (cold), recal_free_200, warm_online_200, warm_online_200_w25, warm_online_200_w0. (4) Gates, any failure is VOID with exit 3: gate 1 flip25 reproduces runs/L15a on six arms with 0 per-seed differences; gate 2 refits off, warm_online_200 and its w25 and w0 twins equal recal_free_200 per policy seed under every STALE variant; gate 3 corruption is active (realised changed-label fraction reported per variant, jlflip50 in 0.20 to 0.30, zero flips on the keep side).
Thresholds (unchanged from the spec). PASS: gates pass and under jlflip50 at 25 percent budget on BOTH rungs warm_online_200 / greedy_online >= 1.00 and warm_online_200 / recal_free_200 (stale) >= 1.06 with the bootstrap CI lower end above 1. KILL: under jlflip50 at 25 percent on EITHER rung warm_online_200 / greedy_online <= 0.97 with the CI upper end below 1; then Phase 1 starts cold, or warm with label-age weighting if warm_online_200_w25 / cold or warm_online_200_w0 / cold is >= 0.99 on both rungs. Anything else is inconclusive and the fn80, fp35 and jlrev100 rows decide the weight. A gate failure is VOID. Result entry follows after the run.

## L16b 2026-10-19 inconclusive (warm start under ranking-changing staleness: 7B-AWQ passes, 3B misses the 1.00 floor by 0.008, no kill on either rung)
What was run: CPU only through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L16b 'bash exp/L16b.sh' (GPU not used, 15 MiB shown at launch, E04 stop rule untouched), exit_code 0, launcher wall 56 s (gates 4 s, first job 2 s, grid done at 54 s), 192 sim jobs, one run, no crash, no rerun. Before launch: one smoke job (3B jlflip50 split 0 subsample 0, 2.7 s) and a 14-job smoke of gates 1 to 3 in a throwaway directory (deleted); no bug found. Scripts: exp/l16b_sim_alloc.py (copy of l15a_sim_alloc.py with the six STALE rules, the w0 arm and per-seed flip statistics), exp/L16b.sh, exp/l16b_gate_check.py, exp/l16b_summarize.py, exp/l16b_critic.py. The spec amendment above (primary flips the BELOW-median judge_logit rows) was recorded before any run and was applied as written.

Gates (all pass). Gate 1 (flip25 vs runs/L15a, six arms, plus n_fit / n_sim / n_buckets / hits): 44 comparisons in the early gate and, inside the post gate, all 32 flip25 files, 0 differences. Gate 2 (norefit, six variants, both rungs): warm_online_200 and its w25 and w0 twins == recal_free_200 per policy seed at three budgets, 120 comparisons including 12 checks that the corruption changes recal_free_200 (3 of 3 budget cells in every variant and rung), 0 differences, no one-class free draw anywhere. Post gate: 192 files, 3,200 comparisons, 0 differences (uniform, static and greedy_online equal L13a in every variant). Gate 3 (pooled over 16 files x 8 seeds x 200 labels per rung): jlflip50 realised fraction of changed free labels 0.2448 (3B) and 0.2441 (7B-AWQ), inside 0.20 to 0.30, with 0 flips at or above the median (6,268 and 6,248 flips, all below); jlflip50_hi 0.2322 and 0.2537 (all flips above); jlrev100 0.4822 and 0.4911 (every eligible label changed); fn80 0.2372 and 0.1983 (0.80 and 0.79 of positives); fp35 0.2417 and 0.2614 (0.34 and 0.35 of negatives); flip25 0.2550. No changed label on a non-eligible row in any variant.

Primary result, jlflip50, 25 percent budget, n_sim 2,250, B = 562, 16 clusters x 8 seeds = 128 runs per rung (mean errors caught).
3B: warm_stale 263.4, cold greedy 265.6, recal_free_200 (stale) 225.6, warm clean (L13a) 276.4, w25 266.6, w0 266.8. warm_stale / cold 0.992 [0.980, 1.003] (split t interval [0.961, 1.023], exact split bootstrap [0.976, 1.008], per-split 0.970 / 1.011 / 1.006 / 0.981, 2 of 4 splits above 1.00, split t(df 3) -0.86 p 0.455). warm_stale / recal_free_200 (stale) 1.168 [1.138, 1.199] (4 of 4 splits above 1.00, split t 15.47 p 0.001). w25 / cold 1.004 [0.997, 1.010] (3 of 4 splits above 1.00). w0 / cold 1.004 [0.996, 1.012] (3 of 4 above).
7B-AWQ: warm_stale 255.4, cold 247.9, recal_free_200 (stale) 232.1, warm clean 262.2, w25 258.4, w0 254.3. warm_stale / cold 1.030 [1.020, 1.041] (split t interval [1.015, 1.044], per-split 1.019 / 1.026 / 1.036 / 1.038, 4 of 4 above, split t 6.21 p 0.008). warm_stale / recal_free_200 (stale) 1.100 [1.075, 1.128] (4 of 4 above, split t 12.60 p 0.001). w25 / cold 1.042 [1.031, 1.053] (4 of 4 above). w0 / cold 1.026 [1.016, 1.036] (4 of 4 above).
Verdict: INCONCLUSIVE. PASS fails on 3B only because warm_stale / cold is 0.992 (< 1.00), the warm / free_stale >= 1.06 clause holds on both rungs with the CI lower end far above 1; 7B-AWQ meets every PASS clause. KILL does not fire on either rung (needs <= 0.97 with the CI upper end below 1; the 3B CI contains 1.00). Warm start under below-median label flipping is not worse than cold by more than about 1 percent on 3B and is 3 percent ahead on 7B-AWQ, and it is 10 to 17 percent ahead of the fixed rule fitted once on the same stale labels. At 10 percent it is 1.029 / 1.064 of cold and at 50 percent 0.994 / 1.009 (3B / 7B-AWQ), so the 3B shortfall sits at the large budgets.

Descriptive rows (25 percent budget, ratio to cold [bootstrap CI]; these decide the weight per the spec, they are not part of the PASS or KILL rule). warm_stale / cold: jlflip50_hi 1.005 [0.992, 1.018] and 0.996 [0.984, 1.009]; jlrev100 0.940 [0.921, 0.959] and 0.966 [0.950, 0.980]; fn80 0.954 [0.936, 0.971] and 0.994 [0.983, 1.008]; fp35 1.026 [1.016, 1.035] and 1.038 [1.026, 1.050]; flip25 control 1.013 and 1.037 (reproduces L15a). So an unweighted warm start loses to cold with the CI upper end below 1 on both rungs under a full relation reversal (jlrev100) and on 3B under positive deletion (fn80), but not under one-sided false positives or under the 50 percent below-median flip. Label-age weighting repairs this: w0 / cold is 1.004 / 1.026 (jlflip50), 1.001 / 1.010 (jlflip50_hi), 0.993 / 1.016 (jlrev100), 1.002 / 1.030 (fn80), 1.002 / 1.027 (fp35), 1.009 / 1.029 (flip25), so w0 is at least 0.99 of cold in every variant on both rungs; w25 / cold falls to 0.975 / 0.994 under jlrev100 and 0.966 / 1.011 under fn80, so w25 is not enough under the worst stress. At 25 percent w0 / warm_stale is 1.050 / 1.035 under fn80 and 1.056 / 1.052 under jlrev100 (3B / 7B-AWQ), 1.013 / 0.996 under jlflip50, and 0.977 to 1.014 in the mild variants (jlflip50_hi, fp35, flip25), so w0 costs up to 2 percent against the unweighted warm start when staleness is mild and gains 4 to 6 percent when it is severe; against w25 it is 1 to 2 percent lower on 7B-AWQ in the mild variants.
Reading to carry forward (descriptive, not pre-registered as a decision): the Phase 1 warm-start default is not withdrawn by the pre-registered test, and claim 2's surviving form (a warm-started persistent learner beats a fixed rule given the same initial labels) holds under ranking-changing staleness by 10 to 17 percent. The 'warm beats cold' part is not established for 3B under below-median staleness (0.992) and is clearly negative under full reversal, so the safe Phase 1 form is warm start with stale labels down-weighted to zero after the first refit (w0), which never fell below 0.993 of cold in any variant, rung or budget (minimum 0.993 at 25 percent, 3B jlrev100; 0.996 at 50 percent; at least 1.010 at 10 percent).

Critic pass (exp/l16b_critic.py, separate code, plain numpy, a different bootstrap generator, 20,000 resamples): the primary and descriptive ratios and split counts recomputed from the raw per_seed lists match result.json to 1e-9 (0 mismatches over 144 ratios), bootstrap CI ends agree within 0.004, recomputed outcome INCONCLUSIVE. Outputs: runs/L16b/result.json, summary.md, critic.log, gate/*.json.
Caveats. (1) The 3B miss is 0.008 under a threshold of 1.00 and the CI [0.980, 1.003] straddles it; split-level evidence is weak either way (2 of 4 splits above 1.00, df 3). (2) w0 is implemented with sample_weight 0 inside a pipeline whose class_weight='balanced' is still computed over all rows of the refit including the zero-weight stale rows, so it is not identical to cold greedy_online refits (w0 / cold is 1.004 and 1.026, not 1.000); its refit guard waits for a two-class stream. (3) Everything is a replay on the L1a tables with one corrupted-label construction; the flips are synthetic stand-ins for checkpoint staleness, and no real earlier checkpoint was used.


## L16b 2026-10-19 inconclusive
Hypothesis: L15a's warm-start PASS (warm_stale_200 / cold greedy_online 1.013 on 3B, 1.037 on 7B-AWQ at flip25, 25 percent budget) was earned under symmetric label flips, which attenuate a logistic score but mostly preserve its row ordering. Real staleness (an earlier checkpoint) changes the feature-to-error relation in the region the rule ranks highest. Pre-registered primary: jlflip50 (each of the 200 free labels whose judge_logit is strictly BELOW the fit-half median is flipped with probability 0.5; the spec amendment that moved the flip from the above-median half to the below-median half is logged earlier in this file, before the run, because judge_logit has AUC 0.4135 / 0.3796 against the corrupted label). PASS: gates pass and under jlflip50 at 25 percent budget on BOTH rungs warm_online_200 / greedy_online >= 1.00 and warm_online_200 / recal_free_200 (same stale labels) >= 1.06 with the bootstrap CI lower end above 1. KILL: warm_online_200 / greedy_online <= 0.97 with the CI upper end below 1 on EITHER rung. Anything else inconclusive; VOID if a gate fails. jlflip50_hi, jlrev100, fn80, fp35 and the flip25 control are descriptive and, per the spec, decide the weight when the verdict is inconclusive.

What was run (review of the run by the loop, from the raw files, not from the run report): CPU only through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L16b 'bash exp/L16b.sh' (GPU not used, E04 stop rule untouched), exit_code 0, launcher wall 56 s, 192 sim jobs. Data: L1a tables (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K rollouts over 3,000 prompts), rungs Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct judge (primary) and 7B-Instruct-AWQ judge (confirmation), corrupted label (judge accepts a wrong answer), cheap_r (8 features), cell30, K = 1, 8 policy seeds, budgets 10 / 25 / 50 percent, n_sim 2,250 x split 0 to 3 x subsample 0 to 3 (16 clusters per rung and variant), six STALE variants. Arms: uniform, static, greedy_online (cold), recal_free_200, warm_online_200, warm_online_200_w25, warm_online_200_w0. Checks I ran myself. (1) Read summary.md and result.json: gate 1 44 cells, gate 2 120 cells, post gate 192 files and 3,200 cells, all 0 differences; gate 3 realised changed-label fractions jlflip50 0.2448 (3B) and 0.2441 (7B-AWQ), all flips below the median, 0 on the keep side. (2) Read the head of exp/l16b_sim_alloc.py and grepped the STALE rule table and the w0 refit line (labels that carry weight in a refit are the stream labels only; the initial fit and the picks before the first refit use the stale labels): consistent with the spec; I did not diff the whole script. (3) Recomputed the 4 PASS / KILL ratios for jlflip50, jlrev100, fn80, fp35 and flip25 at 25 percent on both rungs, and w0 / cold in all 36 rung x variant x budget cells, from the raw per_seed lists with a fresh inline script (own join of the 16 files, own bootstrap, seed 0 with 10,000 resamples): every point ratio equals the ledger to 3 decimals, CI ends within 0.002, splits above 1 identical. (4) The spec amendment entry precedes the result entry in this file; I cannot check timestamps beyond file order.
