| 25 percent (B = 562, primary) | 0.992 [0.980, 1.003] | 1.030 [1.020, 1.041] | 1.004 [0.996, 1.012] | 1.026 [1.016, 1.036] |
| 50 percent (B = 1,125) | 0.994 [0.989, 0.999] | 1.009 [1.003, 1.015] | 1.001 [0.996, 1.006] | 1.008 [1.003, 1.015] |

Descriptive variants at 25 percent budget (ratios of means; the 3B / 7B-AWQ pair in each cell; CI in brackets only where it decides a reading):

| variant (realised fraction of labels changed, 3B / 7B) | warm_stale / cold | warm_stale / free_stale | w25 / cold | w0 / cold |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| jlflip50_hi, flips above the median (0.232 / 0.254) | 1.005 [0.992, 1.018] / 0.996 [0.984, 1.009] | 1.342 / 1.682 | 1.000 / 1.014 | 1.001 / 1.010 |
| jlrev100, every below-median label flipped (0.482 / 0.491) | 0.940 [0.921, 0.960] / 0.966 [0.950, 0.980] | 1.326 / 1.198 | 0.975 [0.958, 0.991] / 0.994 | 0.993 [0.974, 1.010] / 1.016 [1.002, 1.030] |
| fn80, positives dropped with p 0.8 (0.237 / 0.198) | 0.954 [0.936, 0.971] / 0.994 [0.983, 1.008] | 1.173 / 1.182 | 0.966 [0.949, 0.983] / 1.011 | 1.002 / 1.030 |
| fp35, negatives raised with p 0.35 (0.242 / 0.261) | 1.026 [1.016, 1.035] / 1.038 [1.026, 1.051] | 1.075 / 1.084 | 1.019 / 1.038 | 1.002 / 1.027 |
| flip25 control, reproduces L15a (0.255 / 0.255) | 1.013 [1.002, 1.023] / 1.037 [1.026, 1.049] | 1.138 / 1.153 | 1.017 / 1.041 | 1.009 / 1.029 |

Across all 36 rung x variant x budget cells: warm_stale / free_stale is at least 1.039; w0 / cold has minimum 0.993 (3B jlrev100 at 25 percent), next 0.996 (3B jlrev100 at 50 percent and 7B jlflip50_hi at 50 percent); w25 / cold has minimum 0.966 (3B fn80 at 25 percent).

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: INCONCLUSIVE. All three gates pass with 0 differences. PASS fails on exactly one clause: 3B warm_stale / cold is 0.992, below the 1.00 floor, with a CI [0.980, 1.003] that straddles it (2 of 4 splits above 1, split t p 0.455). The 1.06 clause holds on both rungs with the CI lower end far above it (1.138 and 1.075), and 7B-AWQ meets every PASS clause. KILL does not fire on either rung: it needs <= 0.97 with the CI upper end below 1, the 3B point is 0.992 with CI upper 1.003 and the 7B-AWQ ratio is 1.030. Two things the verdict label hides. (a) At 50 percent budget the 3B ratio is 0.994 [0.989, 0.999], a CI upper end below 1 but a 0.6 percent shortfall, nowhere near the 0.97 kill line; the 3B warm edge is positive at 10 percent (1.029) and gone by 25 percent. (b) The descriptive stress variants are not part of the kill rule, but the kill rule read literally on them (point <= 0.97 and CI upper below 1) fires at 25 percent in 3 of 4 cells: 3B jlrev100 (0.940), 3B fn80 (0.954) and 7B-AWQ jlrev100 (0.966 [0.950, 0.980]); it does not fire on fn80 for 7B-AWQ (0.994) or on any fp35, jlflip50_hi or flip25 cell at 25 percent. So the unweighted warm start survives the one pre-registered ranking-changing test and fails the two severe stresses on at least one rung.

What it changes about the thesis claims. (1) Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): not supported as worded, unchanged from L12c, L13a and L15a. The narrow surviving form (a warm-started persistent learner beats a fixed rule fitted once on the same initial labels) holds under ranking-changing staleness: warm_stale / free_stale is 1.168 and 1.100 under jlflip50, 4 of 4 splits on both rungs, and at least 1.039 in all 36 cells. The wording 'any fixed rule' stays false: the clean-label static ceiling sits 6.3 percent (3B) and 3.4 percent (7B-AWQ) above warm_stale, and the L13a critic showed a rule with 762 clean free labels matches the clean warm learner. The L15a caution stands and is not lifted: the free_stale comparator is damaged (0.849 and 0.936 of cold) and cannot refit, so the 1.10 to 1.17 measures repair of a damaged rule; whether a bank of 762 stale labels buys that back is untested under these variants (under jlflip50 the flips are systematic, so count may not average them out, which is what L17b tests). (2) The Phase 1 default: the pre-registered test does not withdraw 'warm-start from earlier verified labels', but it does not confirm 'warm beats cold' either. Under jlflip50 the stale labels erase the whole clean warm edge on 3B (share kept -0.20) and about half of it on 7B-AWQ (0.52; flip25 kept 0.31 and 0.64), the edge is a small-budget effect (10 percent budget 1.029 and 1.064, 50 percent 0.994 and 1.009), and under a full relation reversal or positive deletion the unweighted warm start loses 3 to 6 percent to cold. Label-age weighting repairs this: w0 (stale labels used for the initial fit and the first picks, dropped from every refit) never fell below 0.993 of cold in 36 cells, while w25 reaches 0.966. Operational reading, descriptive and not pre-registered as a decision: if staleness may be severe, Phase 1 should use w0, not an unweighted warm start. The price of w0 when the earlier labels are clean is NOT measured here (no STALE = none variant in the grid); at flip25 and the mild variants w0 gives up 0 to 2 percent against unweighted warm, and the clean-label case is the open question (L17a). (3) Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): untouched by this run. Every arm uses the same cheap_r features and the cell30 layout, no layout-free versus layout comparison was made, and nothing here changes the L1a / L11b state (not supported on GSM8K, representation question not closed).

Caveats. (a) Staleness is simulated by label flips keyed to judge_logit, the very feature the rule ranks by; that is an adversarial construction, closer to a worst case for a logistic rule than a smooth checkpoint drift, and no real earlier checkpoint was used (that needs GPU rollouts and stays blocked on the E04 stop rule; this run gives no reason to ask for an override). (b) The honest unit is 4 splits (df 3); the 16 clusters share fit sets within a split; the 3B primary is a tie at that unit (split t interval [0.961, 1.023]) and the 7B-AWQ primary is firm (4 of 4 splits, split t p 0.008). (c) The primary was moved from above-median to below-median by looking at the AUC of judge_logit on the full tables, before any run and not at outcomes; the above-median control gives 1.005 and 0.996 of cold, which would also have been inconclusive. (d) w0 keeps sklearn's class_weight='balanced' computed over all rows including the zero-weight stale rows, so w0 is not identical to cold refits (w0 / cold 1.004 and 1.026, not 1.000). (e) Single run, one corrupted-label construction, one policy, no replication on prompt-disjoint blocks. A separate critic pass by the run (exp/l16b_critic.py, 144 ratios, 0 mismatches) agrees with mine.

Follow-ups (written to lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md, iteration 17, ranked by expected information value; all CPU, every GPU item stays blocked on the E04 stop rule). L17a (about 8 min, highest): the price of the w0 insurance. Re-run the L16b machinery with STALE = none (clean earlier labels) plus flip25, jlflip50, jlrev100 and fn80, with arms cold, warm, w0 and a delayed drop wd50 (stale weight 1 until the stream holds 50 labels, then 0). It decides whether the Phase 1 default can be w0 outright (kill: w0 / warm_clean <= 0.97 with CI upper below 1 on either rung, i.e. w0 forfeits the 4 to 6 percent L13a edge and a staleness detector is needed) and is the only place the uncertain cost of the fix is measured. L17b (about 8 min, second): label-count control under ranking-changing staleness. Give the fixed rule 400 and 762 stale labels under jlflip50 and jlrev100 (merge with L16a's script if L16a runs); if systematic flips cannot be averaged out by count, recal_free_762 (stale) / warm_stale stays at or below 0.99 with the CI upper end below 1 on both rungs (pass) and the L15a caution is answered in claim 2's favour for the first time; at 1.00 or above on either rung (kill) the 1.10 to 1.17 above is a label-count effect again. L17c (about 6 min, third): amend L16c so the disjoint-prompt block replication carries jlflip50 with the warm, cold and w0 arms, because the 3B primary is a statistical tie at df 3 and df 7 on independent prompts is the cheapest way to say whether 0.992 is a tie or a small loss.


## L8a 2026-10-19 pass
Hypothesis. L7a's judge swap barely moved the feature-to-label relation (cross-judge transfer AUC 0.868 and 0.859 against 0.882 and 0.871 in-sample), so it could not tell a forgetting horizon from carrying stale labels, and gap statement (d) of the thesis, a non-stationary proxy relation under training, was untested where it could bite. If the relation itself changes after the switch (z of judge_top and judge_mean negated on every post-switch row, labels unchanged), the table-A rule becomes anti-informative on those two features while thr_agreement is untouched, so unbounded carry_plain should fall below thr_agreement until its 372 to 750 stale labels are outvoted, and a window or decay arm should recover sooner. Pre-registered rules. PASS: the f = +1 gate reproduces runs/L7a drift exactly AND the best forgetting arm beats unbounded carry_plain by >= 3 percent over batches 7 to 12 with the bootstrap CI lower bound above 1 in at least 3 of 4 cells AND is >= thr_agreement in batches 10 to 12 in those cells. KILL: (i) carry_plain >= 0.99 x the best forgetting arm in at least 3 of 4 cells, or (ii) the best persistent arm (excluding reset_at_switch) stays below thr_agreement in both batches 7 to 9 and 10 to 12 in at least 3 of 4 cells. VOID if the gate fails; otherwise inconclusive.

What was run. CPU only, no generation, no GPU spend (E04 stop rule untouched), through bin/gpu_run.sh runs/L8a 'bash exp/L8a.sh' (gpu_status under 2 GB at launch), exit_code 0, about 5 min for the 128 jobs, 14 min wall including setup. Task GSM8K, 3,000 prompts, E04 feature tables of Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-AWQ (ten features, label judge_error). Layout identical to L7a: permutation default_rng(10000 + order seed), 12 disjoint batches of 250, batches 1 to 6 from table A and 7 to 12 from table B, direction 1 = 3B then 7B-AWQ, direction 2 = 7B-AWQ then 3B (never pooled), per-step B = 62 and 125, policy seed = order seed, 16 order seeds, 4 cells x 16 seeds x {flip f = -1, gate f = +1} = 128 jobs. exp/l8a_flip.py is a copy of exp/l7a_drift.py with one flip argument (the judge_top and judge_mean columns of the per-batch z of every post-switch row times f) and one added arm, carry_plain_decay_hl100 (half-life 100 labels). Selection as L7a: best persistent arm per cell chosen on order seeds 0 to 7 by batches 7 to 12 hits, reported on seeds 8 to 15, swapped fold and pooled 16 as cross-checks, percentile bootstrap over order seeds (10,000 resamples). Persistent set: carry_plain, windows 125 / 250 / 500, decay hl250, decay hl100, carry_offset_lam1; freeze_plain_k3 / k6 and reset_at_switch are reference only.

Verification by me, not taken from the run report. (1) Gate recomputed from the raw JSONs with separate code: 64 (cell, seed) pairs of l8a gate JSONs against runs/L7a drift JSONs, every shared arm vector plus batch_base_errors, 9,216 values, 0 differences, 0 missing; the run's own verify.log agrees (also 0 in-job self-check failures over 128 jobs, 0 pre-switch flip-vs-gate differences, thr_agreement and restart_offset identical flip vs gate). (2) Diffed exp/l8a_flip.py against exp/l7a_drift.py: the only changes are the flip argument, the one multiplication line on rows of the post-switch table for the two judge columns, the hl100 arm and the extra JSON fields, so the surgical-change claim holds. (3) Headline recomputed from the 128 raw JSONs with an inline script (own selection on seeds 0 to 7, bootstrap seed 0, 10,000 resamples): best arm per cell, point ratios and kill / pass flags equal the summarizer (1.1224, 1.1896, 1.0506, 1.0911), CI lower ends 1.0863, 1.1730, 1.0256, 1.0799 against the summarizer's 1.0863, 1.1731, 1.0264, 1.0799 (differences from the bootstrap generator only). (4) No Traceback or error text in any of the 128 job logs. (5) The run's verify.log premise check: rule fit on table A of order seed 0 and scored on table B has transfer AUC 0.824 (d1) and 0.861 (d2) at f = +1, 0.499 and 0.692 at f = -1 (spec predicted 0.49 and 0.67), 0.658 and 0.773 with the two judge features zeroed, in-sample 0.872 and 0.866, so the flip is a real relation change in d1 (chance level) and a partial one in d2.

Headline (verdict fold: arm chosen on order seeds 0 to 7, reported on seeds 8 to 15; ratios of mean errors caught; CI over 8 order seeds). thr_agreement caught 47.7 / 45.4 / 46.2 per batch in batches 4-6 / 7-9 / 10-12 in d1 B=62, 86.0 / 78.3 / 78.8 in d1 B=125, 46.0 / 47.8 / 48.1 in d2 B=62.

| cell | best forgetting (chosen on 0-7) | best forgetting / carry_plain, 7-12 (95% CI) | carry_plain / best forgetting (kill i needs >= 0.99) | best forgetting / thr, 10-12 | best persistent / thr, 7-9 | best persistent / thr, 10-12 | carry_plain / thr, 7-9 and 10-12 (all 16 seeds) | reset_at_switch / best persistent (upper bound) | cell result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| d1 3B->7B-AWQ B=62 | window_125 | 1.122 (1.086 to 1.161) | 0.891 | 1.070 | 1.007 | 1.070 | 0.897 / 0.940 | 1.005 | pass |
| d1 3B->7B-AWQ B=125 | decay_hl100 | 1.190 (1.173 to 1.212) | 0.841 | 1.133 | 1.096 | 1.133 | 0.896 / 0.972 | 0.999 | pass |
| d2 7B-AWQ->3B B=62 | window_125 | 1.051 (1.026 to 1.077) | 0.952 | 1.040 | 1.018 | 1.040 | 0.950 / 0.985 | 1.018 | pass |
| d2 7B-AWQ->3B B=125 | window_250 | 1.091 (1.080 to 1.103) | 0.916 | 1.067 | 1.011 | 1.067 | 0.932 / 0.978 | 1.026 | pass |

Folds: verdict fold PASS 4 of 4, kill (i) 0 of 4, kill (ii) 0 of 4; swapped fold PASS 4 of 4 (best / carry_plain 1.148, 1.195, 1.060, 1.084), kill 0 and 0; pooled 16 PASS 4 of 4 (1.135, 1.192, 1.055, 1.088), kill 0 and 0.

Baselines and context (flip run f = -1, ratio to thr_agreement over all 16 seeds, batches 4-6 pre-switch / 7-9 / 10-12; the same arm at f = +1, the L7a swap, in the last column for 7-9 / 10-12):

| arm | d1 B=62 | d1 B=125 | d2 B=62 | d2 B=125 | f = +1 (d1 B=62) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| carry_plain (unbounded) | 1.079 / 0.897 / 0.940 | 1.096 / 0.896 / 0.972 | 1.107 / 0.950 / 0.985 | 1.135 / 0.932 / 0.978 | 1.159 / 1.142 |
| window_125 | 1.051 / 1.009 / 1.075 | 1.050 / 1.086 / 1.084 | 1.079 / 0.999 / 1.043 | 1.080 / 1.020 / 1.041 | 1.092 / 1.078 |
| window_250 | 1.078 / 0.945 / 1.050 | 1.072 / 1.082 / 1.117 | 1.093 / 0.968 / 1.055 | 1.112 / 1.011 / 1.067 | 1.127 / 1.110 |
| decay_hl100 | 1.069 / 0.970 / 1.034 | 1.071 / 1.095 / 1.133 | 1.095 / 0.973 / 1.017 | 1.120 / 1.010 / 1.064 | 1.128 / 1.121 |
| freeze_plain_k6 (reference) | 1.079 / 0.568 / 0.540 | 1.096 / 0.568 / 0.544 | 1.107 / 0.762 / 0.757 | 1.135 / 0.821 / 0.823 | 1.117 / 1.100 |
| reset_at_switch (upper bound) | 1.079 / 1.011 / 1.089 | 1.096 / 1.103 / 1.134 | 1.107 / 1.027 / 1.066 | 1.135 / 1.039 / 1.096 | 1.011 / 1.089 |

(Every entry is as printed in runs/L8a/summary.log; the f = +1 column is the L7a judge swap on d1 B=62.)

Loop-level post hoc numbers (computed by me from the raw JSONs, not pre-registered). (a) Price of a short horizon when the relation does NOT change: forgetting arm / carry_plain over batches 7 to 12 at f = +1, mean of the 4 cells: window_125 0.956, window_250 0.982, decay_hl100 0.984, window_500 0.995, decay_hl250 1.000, against the flip gains of 1.107, 1.099, 1.099, 1.054, 1.059; in the pre-switch stationary batches 4-6 the same ratios are 0.965, 0.986, 0.986, 0.999, 1.000. So a 250-label window or a 100-label half-life pays about 1.5 to 2 percent when nothing drifts and gains about 10 percent when the relation flips; window_125 pays 3.5 to 4.4 percent for 0.8 points more gain. (b) The optimum is at the edge of the grid in 3 of 4 cells (window_125 is the smallest window tested in d1 B=62 and d2 B=62, decay_hl100 the fastest decay in d1 B=125), so the best horizon is not identified, only bounded above by about 250 labels. (c) Over the whole six post-flip batches (7 to 12) the compromise horizons are at 0.998 / 1.099 / 1.012 / 1.039 (window_250) and 1.002 / 1.114 / 0.995 / 1.036 (decay_hl100) of thr_agreement in d1 B=62 / d1 B=125 / d2 B=62 / d2 B=125; at B=62 the learner's whole edge over the fixed rule is gone for six batches after the flip (best window_125 1.043 and 1.021), at B=125 it is 4 to 11 percent. (d) Batch 7, the first batch after the flip, is lost by every persistent arm in 2 of 4 cells (window_125 / thr 0.919 in d1 B=62, 0.964 in d2 B=62; carry_plain 0.808 to 0.952), so no detector can act before it. (e) Oracle ceiling for a drift detector over a fixed 250-label window or hl100 (oracle chooses the best of carry_plain, the window and reset_at_switch per scenario, batches 7 to 12, 50/50 flip and swap): 1.026 and 1.025 mean over 4 cells (single-cell range 1.000 to 1.053; 1.035 over window_125), i.e. below the 3 percent margin the earlier rules use. (f) The flip run's reset_at_switch is within 0.999 to 1.026 of the best persistent arm, so a short memory recovers essentially all of what a switch-aware restart recovers.

Verdict against the pre-registered thresholds: PASS. The gate has 0 differences. The pass rule (best forgetting / carry_plain >= 1.03 with CI lower above 1, and best forgetting >= thr_agreement in 10-12) holds in 4 of 4 cells in the verdict fold, the swapped fold and the pooled 16 (needed 3). Kill (i) fires in 0 of 4 cells (carry_plain / best forgetting 0.841 to 0.952, needs >= 0.99 in 3). Kill (ii) fires in 0 of 4 (the lowest best-persistent / thr is 1.007 in 7-9, d1 B=62; 0.999 in d2 B=62 pooled over 16 seeds, but 1.043 in 10-12 there, so the both-groups condition fails). The spec's null case did not fire: unbounded carry_plain is below thr_agreement in 7-9 in all 4 cells (0.896 to 0.950) and still below it in 10-12 in all 4 (0.940 to 0.985), and never returns to 1.03 within batches 7 to 12.

What it changes about the thesis claims. Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget). The drift-robustness axis is now tested where it could fail, and the result is mixed in a specific way. (1) Unbounded persistence is not safe under a relation change: the learner that carries its own labels drops 5 to 10 percent below the fixed agreement threshold for the first three batches and stays 1.5 to 6 percent below it for the next three, the L7a corrected reading ("persistence without a detector is safe") holds only for label-noise drift. (2) Learn-then-freeze is unsafe: freeze_plain_k6 falls to 0.54 to 0.82 of thr_agreement after the flip, so "optionally freeze after 2 to 9 batches" in the L7a Phase 1 spec is withdrawn for any setting where the proxy can move. (3) A forgetting horizon repairs most of it: with a 125 to 250 label window or a 100-label half-life the learner is 1.01 to 1.10 of thr_agreement in batches 7 to 9 and 1.04 to 1.13 in 10 to 12, within 0.1 to 2.6 percent of a switch-aware restart. This reverses the L7a statement that a forgetting horizon is "not supported", but only for the relation-change case; under the L7a judge swap the same arms lose 0 to 6 percent to carry_plain. (4) The margin over thr_agreement after a flip is small: 0.7 to 1.8 percent in 7-9 in 3 of 4 cells, and over all six post-flip batches 0.995 to 1.012 of thr at B=62 for the compromise horizons. The narrow surviving form is "a learner with a roughly 250-label forgetting horizon stays at or above the fixed agreement rule through a two-feature relation flip, and well above it from the fourth batch on"; the wording "any fixed rule" is untouched by this run and stays not supported for the reasons logged in L12c, L13a, L15a and L16b. thr_agreement is the only fixed rule compared here. The Phase 1 spec gains a number for E13: use a window of about 250 labels (or hl100) rather than unbounded carry; the price is 1.5 to 2 percent in no-drift and mild-drift settings. Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): untouched; the allocator uses rollout-level and judge-level features and no prompt embedding, and the E01, E04 and L1a kills on GSM8K stand.

Caveats. (1) The drift is synthetic: a known-time, single-step sign flip of two of ten features (judge_top, judge_mean); the other eight features stay truthful, which is why unbounded carry_plain is only 3 to 10 percent below thr_agreement and kill (ii) never had a strong chance; the transfer AUC at f = -1 is 0.499 in d1 and 0.692 in d2, so d2 is a partial relation change. Flipping judge_std and n_distinct as well (L7a diagnostic transfer AUC 0.17) is a harsher drift that this run did not test. (2) The optimum horizon is at the grid edge in 3 of 4 cells, so "best W" is only bounded. (3) 16 order seeds permute the same 3,000 prompts, so order-seed CIs are optimistic and each fold has 8 seeds; the d2 B=62 pass margin is thin (1.051 against 1.03, CI lower 1.026). (4) Direction confound: 7B-AWQ labels are cleaner than 3B (error rate 0.438 vs 0.473); directions are never pooled. (5) One population, offline replay on weak-judge labels, fixed rollouts (no policy drift). (6) The loop-level numbers (a) to (f) are post hoc and not pre-registered.

Follow-ups (ranked by expected information value; full specs in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md, 'Proposed by the loop', iteration 18; all CPU, every GPU item stays blocked on the E04 stop rule).
1. L18a, harsh flip (about 10 min, highest): flip judge_top, judge_mean, judge_std and n_distinct (transfer AUC 0.17 by the L7a diagnostic) and add window_62 and decay_hl50 to the grid. Kill (ii) had almost no chance in L8a because eight features stayed truthful; this is the regime where persistence plus any horizon can actually fail, and it extends the grid below the edge. Decides whether the drift-robustness reading survives the harshest synthetic relation change and whether a 62-label window beats 125.
2. L18b, horizon price and minimax horizon (about 15 min): the L8a grid extended downward and upward over five scenarios (no drift, swap, f = 0, f = -1, gradual ramp from +1 to -1 over batches 7 to 9) with the minimax-regret horizon chosen on seeds 0 to 7. Decides whether one fixed horizon within 3 percent of the best arm in every scenario exists (then the Phase 1 spec is that horizon) or whether the price of forgetting (1.5 to 4 percent) needs a detector or a horizon ensemble. Also adds the gradual-drift check the backlog L8b asked for.
3. L18c, drift detector, amended L8c (about 10 min, conditional): the oracle ceiling above is 1.025 over a fixed 250-label window, below the 3 percent margin, so L8c as written (gap fraction between carry_plain and reset_at_switch) would pass trivially against the wrong comparator. Run only if L18b finds no single horizon within 3 percent of the scenario best; then compare detect_reset to that horizon, not to carry_plain. Low value otherwise.

**Critic note (L8a, adversarial review 2026-10-19).** The PASS verdict stands and so does every verdict count (verdict fold: PASS cells 4 of 4, kill (i) 0 of 4, kill (ii) 0 of 4; swapped fold 4, 0, 0; pooled 16 4, 0, 0; gate 0 differences). The corrections below are to the interpretive text, not to any printed number. What I re-derived with separate code (scratchpad scripts, own bootstrap seed, 10,000 resamples): all 128 job JSONs and logs exist and their budget, order seed, mode and flip factor fields match the file names; every in-job self-check is true; no Traceback, error or warning text in any log; exit_code 0, 5 min between launch and done.flag. Gate: 64 gate files against runs/L7a drift, all 11 L7a arms plus batch_base_errors, 9,216 values, 0 differences, carry_plain_decay_hl100 the only new key; flip and gate runs are identical in batches 1 to 6 for every arm. Budget accounting is equal (after batch j every arm except reset_at_switch holds exactly j x B labels, no arm exceeds B hits in a batch). No leakage: the learner reads labels only for prompts already picked, thr_agreement reads agreement only, the flip multiplies features after the label-free per-batch z and never touches labels, and the diff of exp/l8a_flip.py against exp/l7a_drift.py is as the entry says. Reproduced to the printed precision: verdict-fold ratios 1.1224, 1.1896, 1.0506, 1.0911 (CI lower ends 1.086, 1.173, 1.025 to 1.026 depending on the bootstrap seed, 1.080), best forgetting / thr in 10-12 1.070, 1.133, 1.040, 1.067, kill (i) ratios 0.891, 0.841, 0.952, 0.916, best persistent / thr in 7-9 1.007, 1.096, 1.018, 1.011, swapped fold 1.148, 1.195, 1.060, 1.084, pooled 16 1.135, 1.192, 1.055 or 1.056, 1.088, every row of the Baselines table (24 arm-cell rows and the f = +1 column), and the post hoc items (a), (c), (d), (e) and (f). The pass rule follows from the pre-registered thresholds literally and does not hinge on the thin cell (d2 B=62 is 1.051, 1.060 and 1.055 across the three folds; 3 of 4 cells are needed and 4 pass).

Corrections. (1) The Phase 1 reading is overstated. The sentences "with a 125 to 250 label window or a 100-label half-life the learner is 1.01 to 1.10 of thr_agreement in batches 7 to 9 and 1.04 to 1.13 in 10 to 12" and "a learner with a roughly 250-label forgetting horizon stays at or above the fixed agreement rule through a two-feature relation flip, and well above it from the fourth batch on" quote the best arm chosen per cell (1.007, 1.096, 1.018, 1.011 in 7-9; 1.040 to 1.133 in 10-12), not the family they name. From the entry's own Baselines table (all 16 seeds, f = -1) the family spans 0.945 to 1.095 in 7-9 and 1.017 to 1.133 in 10-12: window_250 is 0.945, 1.082, 0.968, 1.011 and decay_hl100 is 0.970, 1.095, 0.973, 1.010 of thr_agreement in 7-9 (d1 B=62, d1 B=125, d2 B=62, d2 B=125). On the verdict fold the two recommended horizons are 0.948 and 0.964 (d1 B=62) and 0.988 and 0.982 (d2 B=62) in 7-9, and at or above thr_agreement over batches 7-9 in only 1, 5, 3 and 5 of 16 individual seeds in those two B=62 cells; over all six post-flip batches they are 0.998 and 1.012 (window_250) and 1.002 and 0.995 (hl100) of thr_agreement. The first post-flip batch whose ratio to thr_agreement reaches 1.03 (all 16 seeds) is batch 10 and 11 for window_250 and hl100 in d1 B=62, batch 11 for window_250 and never within batch 12 for hl100 (1.0298) in d2 B=62, against batch 8 and 9 for window_125; unbounded carry_plain never reaches it (largest per-batch ratio 1.004). In 10-12 hl100 is only 1.014 (verdict fold) of thr_agreement in d2 B=62, which is not "well above". So the safe-through-the-flip statement holds at B=125 and for window_125 (verdict fold 1.007, 1.094, 1.018, 1.020 in 7-9, but 0.999 in d2 B=62 over all 16 seeds and 9 to 10 of 16 seeds individually at B=62, with batch 7 below thr_agreement for every persistent arm in 2 of 4 cells), and it is not supported for a 250-label window or a 100-label half-life at B=62, where they sit 1.2 to 5.5 percent below the fixed rule for the first three batches after the flip and about at parity for six. (2) The Phase 1 number for E13. The pre-registered reading makes the best W or half-life the spec; the best is not identified (window_125 in 2 cells and decay_hl100 in 1, both at the grid edge, window_250 in 1), and the entry's substitution of "about 250 labels (or hl100)" is a post hoc compromise on the no-drift price that the data do not back for early safety. Corrected spec statement: a forgetting horizon is needed under a relation change; 125 labels is the only tested horizon at or above thr_agreement in batches 7-9 in all four cells on the verdict fold and costs 3.5 to 4.4 percent when nothing drifts; 250 labels or hl100 cost 1.5 to 2 percent when nothing drifts and give up 1.2 to 5.5 percent against thr_agreement in 7-9 at B=62; the safe horizon is bounded above by about 125 to 250 labels and below by the unexplored region under 125, so the horizon is an open parameter until L18a and L18b (window_62, decay_hl50, minimax over scenarios). The sentence "within 0.1 to 2.6 percent of a switch-aware restart" holds for the per-cell best arms only.

Smaller points, no number changes. (a) The thr_agreement counts per batch (47.7 / 45.4 / 46.2, 86.0 / 78.3 / 78.8, 46.0 / 47.8 / 48.1) are over all 16 seeds, not the verdict fold they follow in the text (verdict fold 47.6 / 45.5 / 46.1, 86.0 / 78.8 / 78.9, 46.1 / 47.6 / 47.4). (b) The Hypothesis cites the L7a global-z transfer AUC of 0.868 and 0.859 against 0.882 and 0.871 to say the swap barely moved the relation; this run's own premise check (rule fit on the six table-A batches of order seed 0, per-batch z) gives f = +1 transfer 0.824 against 0.872 in-sample in d1, a loss of 0.048, and 0.861 against 0.866 in d2. The two protocols are not reconciled; the f = -1 contrast (0.499 and 0.692) is the relevant premise and is unaffected. (c) The oracle ceiling "single-cell range 1.000 to 1.053" is the range over the eight (cell, scenario) pairs; the four cell values are 1.012 to 1.040 (window_250) and 1.002 to 1.035 (hl100). (d) The backlog metric list also asked for batches until each arm returns to 1.03 of thr_agreement, now given in correction (1). (e) "At B=62 the learner's whole edge over the fixed rule is gone for six batches after the flip (best window_125 1.043 and 1.021)" is true of window_250 and hl100 only; window_125 keeps 2 to 4 percent. (f) "holds only for label-noise drift" generalizes one judge swap; no other label-noise drift was run.

Corrected verdict: PASS (unchanged). Regression gate 0 differences in 9,216 values (re-verified); best forgetting / carry_plain over batches 7 to 12 on the verdict fold is 1.122 (1.086 to 1.161), 1.190 (1.173 to 1.212), 1.051 (1.025 to 1.077), 1.091 (1.080 to 1.103), best forgetting / thr_agreement in 10-12 is 1.070, 1.133, 1.040, 1.067, so the pass rule holds in 4 of 4 cells, kill (i) in 0 of 4 and kill (ii) in 0 of 4. Corrected reading: under a two-feature relation flip unbounded carry_plain is 5 to 10 percent below thr_agreement for three batches and 1.5 to 6 percent below for the next three, learn-then-freeze is unsafe, and a forgetting horizon repairs most of it; the per-cell best horizon (window_125 or decay_hl100) is at or above thr_agreement through the flip in the verdict fold, but the 250-label window and the 100-label half-life named as the Phase 1 spec are not (1.2 to 5.5 percent below it in batches 7-9 at B=62, above it from batch 10 or 11), so "use a window of about 250 labels" is withdrawn as the E13 number and the horizon stays an open parameter between 125 and 250 labels pending L18a and L18b.
